|                | 
|  INTRODUCTION  | 
|                | 
 ---------------- 
I worked for Faculty of Letters, Hokkaidou University, from 2010 to 
2021.  I guided many excursions at the time.  I made this file based on 
my excursion notes ( by T. Nihei ).  

This file is a continuation of Nihei lab 2019-2021.  Each chapter is 
for imaginary excursions beyond time and space. 

Place names are written as they are pronounced.  For example ; Hokkaido 
--> Hokkaidou.  This is because the content is for oral explanation.  
This method is called the ``universal spelling method'' ( by Nihei lab 
2025-2026_u ). 

Some place names are displayed in UTF ( Unicode Transformation Format ) 
code.  To convert UTF code to characters, use the ``echo'' command on a 
terminal emulator.  For example ; echo $'\u5317\u7ADC.' 

Or, these online command-line interpreters can be available.
--> http://codapi.org/shell/
--> http://www.tutorialspoint.com/compilers/online-bash-compiler.htm

ACKNOWLEDGMENTS 

We would like to dedicate this work to Baron Kondou ( Kijuurou Kondou ) 
who died in 2023.  He studied equitation science in England and 
thoroughbred breeding in our laboratory.  He told us interesting 
stories about old dreams.

|  PRONUNCIATION  |

 -----------------
1.  1900s = nineteen hundreds.
2.  1902 = nineteen oh two.
3.  2000s = two thousands.
4.  2006 = two thousand and six.
5.  41* 23.87' North = forty one degrees, twenty three point eight
    seven minutes north ( latitude ).  ( * = \u00B0. )

6.  9.1 *C = nine point one degrees Celsius.  ( * = \u00B0. )
7.  National Route 228 = national route two hundred twenty eight ( two
    twenty eight ).
8.  Prefectural Route 999 = prefectural route nine hundred ninety nine
    ( nine ninety nine, nine nine nine ).

9.  Statistics of beans, grains, pasture, potatoes, and orchards for
    sale.  English = Japanese = Kanji characters ( UTF code ).
( 1 ) adzuki beans = ``shouzu'' in Hokkaidou island or ``azuki'' in
      Honshuu island = \u5C0F\u8C46, \u5C0F\u8C46 ( same ).
( 2 ) barley = ``oomugui'' = \u5927\u9EA6.
( 3 ) buckwheat = ``soba'' = \u854E\u9EA6.
( 4 ) fodder ( fodder crops ) = ``shiryou-sakumotsu =
      \u98FC\u6599\u4F5C\u7269.
( 5 ) orchards = ``juen-chi'' = \u6A39\u5712\u5730.

( 6 ) paddy rice = ``suitou'' or ``kome'' = \u6C34\u7A32, \u7C73.  
( 7 ) pasture ( pasture land ) = ``bokusou-chi'' = \u7267\u8349\u5730.
( 8 ) potatoes ( white potatoes ) = ``bareisho'' or ``imo'' =
      \u99AC\u9234\u85AF, \u85AF.
( 9 ) soybeans = ``daizu'' = \u5927\u8C46.
( 10 ) sweet potatoes = ``satsuma-imo,'' ``imo,'' or ``kansho =
       \u85A9\u6469\u828B, \u828B, \u7518\u85AF.  ( The word ``kansha'' 
       is an old Japanese for sugarcane. ``kanansha'' = \u7518\u8517. )

( 11 ) wheat = ``komugui'' = \u5C0F\u9EA6.

( The area of paddy rice is listed in every municipality, because it's 
  an important crop when seeing landscapes. )

|                 |
|     OSHIMA      |
|                 |
|  MAP 1 - NO. 1  |
 -----------------
LOCATION

Oshima ( o-shi-ma ) is a general sub-prefecture in south Hokkaidou 
( ``dou-nan'' ).  Oshima is located in the south of the Oshima 
Peninsula.  The area faces Uchiura Bay ( Volcano Bay ), the Tsugaru 
Strait, and the Sea of Japan.  #1

( UTF code ; Oshima = \u6E21\u5CF6. )

( In Japanese ; ``General sub-prefecture'' is ``sougou-shinkou-
  kyoku.'' )

The temperature is high compared to other areas in Hokkaidou.  By 
Koeppen's climate classification, the coast of Matsumae Town is 
classified as Cfa ( humid subtropical ), whereas most areas in 
Hokkaidou is classified as Dfb ( humid continental, cool summer ). 
( Koeppen is a German surname. )

( UTF code ; Koeppen = K\u00F6ppen. )

In the case of Hakodate City, the mean annual temperature is 9.1 
degrees Celsius ( average from 1981 to 2010 ), the mean annual 
precipitation is 1,152 millimeters, and the mean annual snowfall is 381 
centimeters.  #2 

NAME 

The name ``Oshima'' is Japanese.  The pronunciation of the Kanji 
characters ``Watari-shima'' changed to Oshima.  The name ``Watari-
shima'' means ``across, island.'' 

( UTF code ; Watari-shima = \u6E21\u5CF6. ) 

In the mid 15th century, 12 forts were builts by Japanese people.  
( Forts are called ``tate'' in Japanse. )  During the Edo era, the 
Matsumae Domain ( Matsumae-han ) ruled this area.  The Tokugawa 
Shogunate also ruled this area at one point.  ( The Edo era continued 
from 1603 to 1868. ) 

( For the former 12 forts, see Kaminokuni Town in Hiyama sub-
  prefecture. )

( For history in the Edo era, see Matsumae Town and Hakodate City in 
  Oshima general sub-prefecture. ) 

HISTORY    

During the Meiji period, in 1869 ( Meiji 2 ), Oshima country ( Oshima-
no-kuni ) was established.  The Hokkaidou Development Commission 
( Kaitaku-shi ) was established in Hakodate.  In 1871 ( Meiji 4 ), the 
Hokkaidou Development Commission moved to Sapporo.  The Hakodate's 
building became a branch office.

( UTF code ; Oshima-no-kuni = \u6E21\u5CF6\u56FD. ) 

In 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), administrative offices of counties ( ``gun-
yakusho'' ) were established.  Administrative offices of villages and 
towns ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) were established.   The administrative 
offices opened before this year were a continuation of village chiefs 
( ``kocyou'' ) or town chiefs ( ``kocyou'' ) from the Edo era. 

( UTF code ; ``gun-yakusho'' = \u90E1\u5F79\u6240.  ``kochou-yakuba'' = 
  \u6238\u9577\u5F79\u5834. )

( In Japanese ; ``Administrative office of county'' in the Meiji period 
  is ``gun-yakusho.''  ``Administrative office of village ( or town )''
  in the Meiji period is ``kochou-yakuba.''  ``Village chief ( or town 
  chief )'' in the Edo era and the Meij period  are ``kochou.'' )

In 1882 ( Meiji 15 ), the Hokkaidou Development Commission was 
abolished.  Prefectures of Hakodate, Nemuro, and Sapporo were 
established.  In 1886 ( Meiji 19 ), three prefectures were abolished. 
Hokkaidou Prefecture Office ( Hokkaidou-chou ) was established.

In 1897 ( Meiji 30 ), administrative offices for counties were 
abolished.  Three sub-prefectures ( ``shichou'' ) were established in 
the area what is now Oshihma general sub-prefecture.  They were 
Hakodate, Kameda, and Matsumae.

( UTF code ; ``shichou'' = \u652F\u5E81. )

In 1899 ( Meiji 32 ), Hakodate sub-prefecture merged Kameda sub-
prefecture.  In 1903 ( Meiji 36 ), Hakodate sub-prefecture merged 
Matsumae sub-prefecture.  Meiji was a turbulent period.  In 1922 
( Taishou 11 ), Hakodate sub-prefecture was renamed Oshima sub-
prefecture.  In 2010, Oshima general sub-prefecture was established.

( In Japanese ; ``General sub-prefecture'' after 2010 is ``sougou-
  shinkou-kyoku.''  ``Sub-prefecture'' after 2010 is ``shinkou-kyoku.'' 
  ``Sub-prefecture'' until 2009 is ``shichou.'' )
    
( UTF code ; ``shichou'' = \u652F\u5E81.  ``shinkou-kyoku'' = 
  \u632F\u8208\u5C40.  ``sougou-shinkou-kyoku'' = 
  \u7DCF\u5408\u632F\u8208\u5C40. )

MUNICIPALITY 

These are municipalities in Oshima general sub-prefecture ; ( 1 ) 
Hakodate City 279,127 ( population in 2010, 3rd largest in the 
prefecture ), ( 2 ) Hokuto City 48,032 ( 17th largest ), ( 3 ) Nanae 
Town 28,453, ( 4 ) Yakumo Town 18,896, ( 5 ) Mori Town 17,859, 

( continue ) --> ( 6 ) Matsumae Town 8,748, ( 7 ) Oshamanbe Town 6,386, 
( 8 ) Kikonai Town 5,386, ( 9 ) Fukushima Town 5,114, ( 10 ) Shiriuchi 
Town 5,074, and ( 11 ) Shikabe Town 4,767.  The sub-prefecture bureau 
is located in Hakodate City.

NOTE

#1

Volcano Bay is called Funka-wan in Japanese.  The name ``Volcano Bay'' 
was named by William Robert Broughton in 1796.  He was a British naval 
officer.  At the time, volcanoes erupted around the bay.

Some students asked, ``Is Volcano Bay a caldera?''  It's not a 
caldera, although the shape resembles a caldera.  There is no caldera 
as large as this size in the country.  

The largest caldera is Kussharo Caldera in Hokkaidou.  It's located in 
Teshikaga Town in Kushiro general sub-prefecture.  When I was in 
elementary school, I heard that the world largest caldera was Aso 
Caldera in Kumamoto Prefecture.  Today, the world largest caldera is 
located in Indonesia.

#2

In Toukyou, the mean annual temperature is 15.4 degrees Celsius.  The
mean annual rainfall is 1,529 millimeters.  The climate is classified
as Cfa ( humid subtropical ) by Koeppen's climate classification.

( These values are the average from 1981 to 2010.  They were recorded
  in Chiyoda Ward, central Toukyou.  They're representative values for  
  the country. )

As for the coldest month, the mean temperature of Toukyou is 5.2 
degrees Celsius in January ( average from 1981 to 2010 ).  The mean 
temperature of Hakodate is -2.6 degrees Celsius in January.

As for the hottest month, the mean temperature of Toukyou is 26.4 
degrees Celsius in August.  The mean temperature of Hakodate is 22.0 
degrees Celsius in August. 

In Hokkadou, it's cold after Obon ( 15th August ).  In Toukyou, it's 
still hot after the equinox ( 23rd September, ``Shuubun'' in 
Japanese ). 

( Single of the chapter ; ``Betty Wrong'' by Tin Machine.  This was 
  used in the 1990 film ``The Crossing.''  --> 
  http://youtu.be/_bWxkaqHsSY )
 _________________
|                 |
|     HIYAMA      |
|                 |
|  MAP 1 - NO. 2  |
 ----------------- 
LOCATION 

Hiyama ( hi-ya-ma ) is a sub-prefecture in south Hokkaidou ( ``dou-
nan'' ).  Hiyama is located in the west of the Oshima Peninsula.  It 
faces the Sea of Japan.  The area is divided into north and south.  #1

( UTF code ; Hiyama = \u6A9C\u5C71. )

( In Japanese ; ``Sub-prefecture'' is ``shinkou-kyoku.'' )

The climate is mild compared to other regions in Hokkaidou.  The warm 
Tsushima Current flowing north influences.  In winter, it's windy due 
to the monsoon that blows from the continent.

According to Koeppen's climate classification, most of the region is 
classified as Dfb ( humid continental, cool summer ).  ( Koeppen is a 
German surname. )

( UTF code ; Koeppen = K\u00F6ppen. )

The coast of Esashi Town and the coast of Okushiri Island are 
classified as Cfa ( humid subtropical ).  The other coasts are 
classified as Cfb ( temperate oceanic ).  The population is distributs 
along the coast.

In the case of Esashi Town, the mean annual temperature is 10.1 degrees 
Celsius ( average from 1981 to 2010 ), the mean annual precipitation is 
1,197 millimeters, and the mean snowfall is 330 centimeters.  #2

NAME   

The name ``Hiyama'' is Japanese. ``Hi-yama'' means ``cypress ( sa'i-
pres ), mountain.''  The name was suggested by Takeshirou Matsuura in 
the beginning of the Meiji period.  Takeshirou Matsuura was an explorer 
of Ezo ( now Hokkaidou ) during the Edo era.  #3  

HISTORY 

There used to be natural forests of ``hiba'' trees in Hiyama. 
( ``Hiba'' is a kind of cypress. )  After the late 17th century, during 
the Edo era, the Matsumae Domain felled ``hiba'' trees.  The area was 
what is now Hiyama sub-prefecture.  #4  #5

Japanese migration has a long history.  In the mid 15th century, the 
Andou clan built 12 forts.  The area was what are now Hiayama sub-
prefecture and Oshima general sub-prefecture.  In the Edo era, the 
southern part of Hiyama sub-prefecture was ruled by the Matsumae 
Domain.  It was also ruled by the Tokugawa Shogunate for a time.  

After the Meiji period, in 1869 ( Meiji 2 ), this area was included in 
Oshima country ( Oshima-no-kuni ) and Shiribeshi country ( Shiribeshi-
no-kuni ).  In 1872 ( Meiji 5 ), Esashi branch of the Hokkaidou 
Development Commission was established.

( UTF code ; Oshima-no-kuni = \u6E21\u5CF6\u56FD.  Shiribeshi-no-kuni = 
  \u5F8C\u5FD7\u56FD. ) 

In 1897 ( Meiji 30 ), Hiyama sub-prefecture was established.  In 2010, 
Hiyama sub-prefecture was re-established.  

( In Japanese ; ``Sub-prefecture'' after 2010 is ``shinkou-kyoku.''  
  ``Sub-prefecture'' until 2009 is ``shichou.'' )

( UTF code ; ``shichou'' = \u652F\u5E81.  ``shinkou-kyoku'' = 
  \u632F\u8208\u5C40. )

MUNICIPALITY  

These are municipalities in Hiyama ; ( 1 ) Setana Town 9,590 
( population in 2010 ), ( 2 ) Esashi Town 9,004, ( 3 ) Imakane Town 
6,186, ( 4 ) Kaminokuni Town 5,428, ( 5 ) Assabu Town 4,409, ( 6 ) 
Otobe Town 4,408, and ( 7 ) Okushiri Town 3,033.  The sub-prefecture 
bureau is located in Esashi Town.

The population of Hiyama is the smallest of all the sub-prefectures.  
All municipalities in Hiyama are designated as depopulated regions.  

( In Japanese ; ``Depopulate region'' is ``kaso-chiiki.'' )

NOTE 
  
#1

Hiyama sub-prefecture is divided into two areas, the northern 
and the southern parts.  In the middle part, there was Kumaishi Town. 
Kumaishi was merged to Yakumo Town in Oshima general sub-prefecture.  

Oshima general sub-prefecture is located on the Pacific Ocean side.  
Hiyama sub-prefecture is located on the Sea of Japal side.  There were 
many reasons for the merger across sub-prefectures. 

#2

In Toukyou, the mean annual temperature is 15.4 degrees Celsius.  The
mean annual rainfall is 1,529 millimeters.  The climate is classified
as Cfa ( humid subtropical ) by Koeppen's climate classification.

( These values are the average from 1981 to 2010.  They were recorded
  in Chiyoda Ward, central Toukyou.  They're representative values for  
  the country. )

As for the coldest month, the mean temperature of Toukyou is 5.2 
degrees Celsius in January ( average from 1981-2010 ).  The mean 
temperature of Esashi is -0.8 degrees Celsius in January.
 
As for the hottest month, the mean temperature of Toukyou is 26.4 
degrees Celsius in August.  The mean temperature of Esashi is 22.6 
degrees Celsius in August.
  
#3

Takeshirou Matsuura ( 1818-1888 ) was an explorer of Ezo ( old 
Hokkaidou ) in the Edo era.  He documented place names in Ezo Island 
and published the book ``Ezo Region County Name Survey Report ( Ezochi-
gunmei-no-gui-torishirabe-sho ).''  

The book was submitted to the Meiji government in 1869 ( Meiji 2 ).  
The government determined place names in Ezo ( now Hokkaidou ) based on 
the book.  The name ``Hiyama'' was the one of them. 

As for the name ``Hiyama,'' Takeshirou Matsuura may have adopted the 
name of the old Hiyama Castle, which was built by the Andou clan in 
their home land. 

Before the Edo era, in the 15th century, the Andou clan migrated to Ezo 
and built 12 forts.  The clan returned to their home land, which was 
located in what is now the Touhoku region.  In their home land, the 
clan built Hiyama Castle, which was located in what is now Akita 
Prefecture. 
	
( For the former 12 forts, see Kaminokuni Town in Hiyama sub-
  prefecture. )

( UTF code ; Ezochi-gunmei-no-gui-torishirabe-sho = 
  \u8766\u5937\u5730\u90E1\u540D\u4E4B\u5100\u53D6\u8ABF\u66F8. )
 
#4

The name ``Hi-yama'' means ``cypress mountain.''  In Honshuu Island, 
usually, cypress trees are called ``hinoki.''  In Hokkaidou Island and 
Aomori Prefecture, cypress trees are called ``hiba.''  ``Hinoki'' and 
``hiba'' look alike, but they are different trees.
 
Hou-ryuu-ji Temple ( Buddhist temple ) is the oldest wooden building in 
the country.  It was rebuilt in 670.  The temple is made of ``hinoki'' 
trees.  Temple carpenters ( ``miya-daiku'' ) consider ``hinoki'' to be 
the best wood for construction ( by Nihei lab 2024-2026_e ).

Another name of ``hiba'' tree is ``hinoki-asunaro.''  It means 
``tomorrow, it will be `hinoki' trees'' in Japanese.  This means that 
``hiba'' wood is inferior to ``hinoki.''  But, ``hiba'' wood is also 
good.

The leaves of ``hiba'' are denser than ``hinoki.''  ``Hiba'' trees 
grow slower than trees of ``hinoki,'' ``sugui'' ( cedar ), and 
``todomatsu'' ( fir ).  ``Hiba'' trees grow even on shady north slopes 
and moist soils along the coast.  They grow slowy and steadily. 

``Hiba'' wood has a tight grain and is resistant to moisture.  The wood 
is less likely to warp and less likely to rot.  It was used for 
pillars, bathtubs, boats, bridges, and cutting boards.

The natural forests in Hiyama sub-prefecture were said ``Good trees 
even along the coastline ( Namiuchi-guiwa-yori-ryouzai-shigeri ).''  
The natural forests were disappeared because of felling and fires 
during the Edo era. 

Today, two natural forests are preserved in remote mountains.  They 
have been designated as National Natural Monuments ( Tennen-kinen-
butsu ). 

( For cypress felling, see Assabu Town and Esashi Town in Hiyama sub-
  prefecture. )

( For the oldest building in Hokkaidou, see Kaminokuni Town in Hiyama 
  sub-prefecture. )
 
#5

High school teacher Masao Kanamori says ; ``Hiyama's culture is 
different from other parts in Hokkaidou.  You will see it when you 
visit festivals.'' 

( Single of the chapter ; ``Watching As She Reels'' by Rafael Anton 
  Irisarri.  This was included in the record ``Hopes And Past 
  Desires.''  --> http://youtu.be/6bQ3dtBVYaw )

|                 |
|   SHIRIBESHI    |
|                 |
|  MAP 1 - NO. 3  |
 -----------------
LOCATION
 
Shiribeshi ( shi-ri-be-shi ) is a general sub-prefecture in central 
Hokkaidou ( ``dou-ou'' ).  The area faces the Sea of Japan. 

( UTF code ; Shiribeshi = \u5F8C\u5FD7. )

( In Japanese ; ``General sub-prefecture'' is ``sougou-shinkou-
  kyoku.'' )

The weather in spring and summer is warm and sunny.  In winter, 
seasonal winds from the northwest are strong.  The ground is covered 
with snow from mid-November to mid-April.  The area around Mt. Youtei-
zan is one of the snowiest in the prefecture.  

( The wind that changes direction by seasons is called a monsoon or a 
  seasonal wind.  In Japanese, it's called ``kisetsu-fuu.'' )

According to Koeppen's classification, Shiribeshi general sub-
prefecture is classified as Dfb ( humid continental, warm summer ).  
( Koeppen is a German surname. ) 

( UTF code ; Koeppen = K\u00F6ppen. )

In the case of Kucchan Town, the mean annual temperature is 7.0 degrees 
Celsius ( average from 1981 to 2010 ), the mean precipitation is 1,477 
millimeters, and the mean snowfall is 1,062 centimeters.  #1

NAME

The name ``Shiribeshi'' comes from ``sir-pet'' in Ainu language.  It 
means ``mountain, river.''  It signified the Shiribetsu River, which 
flows around Mt. Youtei-zan and empties in the Sea of Japan.  In the 
beginning of the Meiji period, Takeshirou Matsuura, who was an explorer
of Hokkaidou Island, suggested this name for this region. 

Takeshirou Matsuura adopted this name from the ancient record of Hirafu 
Abe.  Hirafu Abe was a general who conquered Shiribeshi region in 659.  
Historians estimated that Shiribeshi was the area around Mt. Youtei-
zan. 

HISTORY

In the Edo era, the Matsumae Domain established trading places 
( ``basho'' ) for herring fishing.  After the Meiji period, in 1869 
( Meiji 2 ), Shiribeshi country was established.  Zenibako branch of 
the Hokkaidou Development Commission was established.   

( In Japanese ; ``Shiribeshi country'' is Shiribeshi-no-kuni.  ``The 
  Hokkaidou Development Commission'' is Kaitaku-shi. )

( UTF code ; Shiribeshi-no-kuni = \u5F8C\u5FD7\u56FD. ) 

In 1870 ( Meiji 3 ), Otaru branch of the Hokkaidou Development 
Commission was established.  Zenibako branch was abolished.  In 1871 
( Meiji 4 ), the Hokkaidou Development Commission was moved from 
Hakodate to Sapporo.  Otaru branch was abolished.   

In 1897 ( Meiji 30 ), sub-prefectures of Otaru, Iwanai, and Suttsu were 
established in Shiribeshi country.  In 1910 ( Meiji 43 ), the three 
sub-prefectures were merged and Shiribeshi sub-prefecture was 
established.  ( Part of Shiribeshi country was included in Hiyama sub-
prefecture. ) 

In the same year, the sub-prefecture bureau of Shiribeshi was built in 
Kucchan Village.  In 2010, Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture was 
established. 

( In Japanese ; ``General sub-prefecture'' after 2010 is ``sougou-
  shinkou-kyoku.''  ``Sub-prefecture'' after 2010 is ``shinkou-kyoku.'' 
  ``Sub-prefecture'' until 2009 is ``shichou.'' )
 
( UTF code ; ``shichou'' = \u652F\u5E81.  ``shinkou-kyoku'' = 
  \u632F\u8208\u5C40.  ``sougou-shinkou-kyoku'' = 
  \u7DCF\u5408\u632F\u8208\u5C40. )

MUNICIPALITY 

These are municipalities in Shiribesh general sub-prefecture ; ( 1 ) 
Otaru City 131,928 ( population in 2010, 7th largest in the 
prefecture ), ( 2 ) Yoichi Town 21,258, ( 3 ) Kucchan Town 15,568, 
( 4 ) Iwanai Town 14,451, ( 5 ) Kyouuwa Town 6,428, 

( continued ) --> ( 6 ) Rankoshi Town 5,292, ( 7 ) Niseko Town 4,823, 
( 8 ) Kyougoku Town 3,811, ( 9 ) Niki Town 3,800, ( 10 ) Furubira Town 
3,611, ( 11 ) Suttsu Town 3,443, ( 12 ) Kuromatsunai Town 3,250, ( 13 ) 
Shakotan Town 2,516, ( 14 ) Kimobetsu Town 2,490, 

( continued ) --> ( 15 ) Makkari Village 2,189, ( 16 ) Rusutsu Village 
2,034, ( 17 ) Tomari Village 1,883, ( 18 ) Shimamaki Village 1,781, 
( 19 ) Akaigawa Village 1,262, and ( 20 ) Kamoenai Village 1,122.  The 
general sub-prefecture bureau is located in Kucchan Town. 

NOTE

#1
 
In Toukyou, the mean annual temperature is 15.4 degrees Celsius.  The 
mean annual rainfall is 1,529 millimeters.  The climate is classified 
as Cfa ( humid subtropical ) by Koeppen's climate classification.

( These values are the average from 1981 to 2010.  They were recorded
  in Chiyoda Ward, central Toukyou.  They're representative values for  
  the country. )
 
As for the coldest month, the mean temperature of Toukyou is 5.2 
degrees Celsius in January.  The mean temperature of Kucchan Town is 
-5.7 degrees Celsius in January.  As for the hottest month, the mean 
temperature of Toukyou is 26.4 degrees Celsius in August.  The mean 
temperature of Kucchan Town is 20.7 degrees Celsius in August. 

( Single of the chapter ; ``Pa Mi Es Igual ( Father Is The Same ) '' by 
  Angelis.  --> http://youtu.be/meVEzxbhlHk )

|                 |
|    MATSUMAE     |
|                 |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 1  |
 -----------------
AREA 293 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 256, CULTIVATED 4 ; COUNTY = MATSUMAE ; 
POP 13,546 ( 1990 ), 8,748 ( 2010 ) ; MEAN ANNUAL TEMP 10.3 C ( AV 
1981-2010 ), PRECIPI 1,263 MM ; COUNTRY SIGN = CASTLE.  
  
LOCATION 

Matsumae ( ma-tsu-ma-e ) is a town in Oshima general sub-prefecture, 
south Hokkaidou ( ``dou-nan'' ).  Matsumae is located in the southwest 
of the Oshima Peninsula.  The part is called the Matsumae Peninsula. 

( UTF code ; Matsumae = \u677E\u524D. )

Matsumae is the southernmost municipality in Hokkaidou.  The area faces 
the Tsugaru Strait and the Sea of Japan.  It includes Ooshima Island 
and Kojima Island on the Sea of Japan.  #1  #2 

The area is mountainous.  Forests occupy 87 percent.  Rivers are short. 
Mt. Dai-sen-guen ( 1,072 meters ) stands to the north.  There was a 
gold mine in Mt. Dai-sen-guen.

The climate along the coast is mild.  It is classified as ``Cfa'' by 
Koeppen's classification.  ``Cfa'' means ``humid subtropical.''  Almost 
all other parts of Hokkaidou are classified as ``Dfb,'' which means 
``humid continental and cool summer.''  

Camellia trees and Mousou-dake bamboo grow.  Camellia is a forever-
green broad-leaved tree.  Tea and Camellia are from the same family.  
Mousou-dake is a large bamboo.

In Hokkaidou, ``bamboo'' usually means ``bamboo grass.''  Bamboo grass 
is a small bamboo, which is called ``sasa'' in Japanese.  Although 
bamboo grass is small, it is a large organism, because the stems are 
linked by roots.  

( For gold miner in Mt. Dai-sen-guen, see Fukushima Town in Oshima 
  general sub-prefecture. ) 

NAME

The name ``Matsumae'' comes from ``mat-oma-i'' in Ainu language.  It 
means ``woman, live, place.''  It signified the uniqueness of the place 
where Japanese women lived.  The etymology is similar to Kuromatsunai 
Town in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture.  ( Ainu people are an 
indigenous ethnic group in Hokkaidou Island. ) 

In Ezo ( old Hokkaidou ), most Japanese people returned to their 
homelands in winter.  The name ``Matsumae'' signified that the place 
was the territory of Japanese people.  They could have children in the 
place.  They could reproduce. 

HISTORY 

In 720, the name ``Watari-shima-tsugaru-tsu'' was recorded.  It meant a 
port ( ``tsu'' ) between Watari-shima ( now Hokkaidou Prefecture ) and 
Tsugaru ( now Aomori Prefecture ).  It signified what is now the Port 
of Matsumae.

( UTF code ; Watari-shima-tsugaru-tsu = 
  \u6E21\u5CF6\u6D25\u8EFD\u6D25. ) 

In the 14th century, the name ``Matouma-inu'' was recorded.  From the 
13th to the 15th centuries, the Andou clan came from Tsugaru ( now 
Aomori Prefecture ).  The clan built 12 forts in what is now south 
Hokkaidou.  They traded with Ainu people. 

( UTF code ; Matouma-inu = \u4E07\u5802\u5B87\u6E80\u4F0A\u72AC. )

In 1457, Koshamain's War happened.  Koshamain was a chief of Ainu 
people.  In the war, the Ainu forces destroyed 10 forts out of the 12 
forts.  #3

During the Edo era, Matsumae was the capital of the Matsumae Domain.  
It was the northernmost castle town in Japan.  The Matsumae Domain ran 
major ports in Ezo ( old Hokkaidou ) ; Esashi, Hakodate, and Matsumae.
The ports were gateways to Ezo.  ``Matsmai'' in the novel Moby Dick 
means Hokkaidou.

At the end of the Edo era, in 1868, the Matsumae Domain separated from 
the Tokugawa Shogunate.  They supported the new Meiji government.  In 
the beginning of the Meiji period, the Boshin War happened.  The former 
Tokugawa Shogunate forces landed Ezo and captured the Port of Hakodate 
and Fukuyama Castle ( Matsumae Castle ). 

( For the former 12 forts and Koshamain's War, see Kaminokuni Town in 
  Hiyama sub-prefecture. )

In 1869 ( Meiji 2 ), the Meiji government forces recaptured Fukuyama 
Castle.  They advanced to the Port of Hakodate, defeated the 
former Tokugawa Shogunate, and ended the Boshin War.  After the war, 
the Matsumae Domain was renamed the Tate Domain.  It was named after 
Tate Castle in what is now Assabu Town.

In 1871 ( Meiji 4 ), the Tate Domain was abolished and Aomori 
Prefecture governed the south part of Hokkaidou.  In 1872 ( Meiji 5 ), 
Matsumae branch of the Hokkaidou Development Commission ( Kaitaku-shi ) 
was established.

In 1881 ( Meiji 14 ), the administrative office of Matsumae county 
( Matsumae-gun-yakusho ) was established.  Matsumae branch of the 
Hokkaidou Development Commission was abolished.  In 1897 ( Meiji 
30 ), Matsumae sub-prefecture was established.  The administrative 
office of Matsumae county was abolished. 

In 1900 ( Meiji 33 ), Fukuyama Town ( 1st-class municipality ) was 
established.  At the time, 34 towns in the built-up area of Matsumae 
were merged.  Those were small towns that had existed in the Edo era.  
#4 

( UTF code ; Fukuyama = \u798F\u5C71. ) 

In 1903 ( Meiji 36 ), Hakodate sub-prefecture merged Matsumae sub-
prefecture.  In 1915 ( Taishou 4 ), Ooshima Village ( 2nd-class 
municipality ) was established by the merger of three villages ; 
Eramachi, Haraguchi, and Kiyobe.  ( Ooshima village was not Ooshima 
Island. ) 

( UTF code ; Eramachi = \u6C5F\u826F\u753A.  Haraguchi = \u539F\u53E3, 
  Kiyobe = \u6E05\u90E8, Ooshima = \u5927\u5CF6. ) 

In 1923 ( Taishou 12 ), Kojima Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established by the merger of five villages ; Akagami, Nebuta, Mogusa, 
Satsumae, and Utari.  ( Kojima village was not Kojima Island. )

( UTF code ; Akagami = \u8D64\u795E.  Kojima = \u5C0F\u5CF6.  Mogusa = 
  \u8302\u8349.  Nebuta = \u6839\u90E8\u7530.  Satsumae = \u672D\u524D. 
  Utari = \u96E8\u5782. ) 

In the same year, Oosawa Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established by the merger of four villages ; Araya, Kami-oyobe, Oosawa, 
and Sumiyaki-zawa.

( UTF code ; Araya = \u8352\u8C37.  Kami-oyobe = \u4E0A\u53CA\u90E8. 
  Oosawa = \u5927\u6CA2.  Sumiyaki-zawa = \u70AD\u713C\u6CA2. ) 

In 1940 ( Shouwa 15 ), Fukuyama Town was renamed Matsumae Town.  In 
1954 ( Shouwa 29 ), Matsumae Town merged the villages of Kojima, 
Oosawa, and Ooshima.  #5 

( For Tate Castle, see Assabu Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. ) 

( For the Boshin War, see Hakodate City in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )

TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC

The town hall of Matsumae and Matsumae Castle ( Fukuyama Castle ) are 
located near the mouth of the Oomatsumae River.  National Route 228 
( Fukuyama-kaidou or Matsumae-kokudou ) passes there.  ``Fukuyama-
kaidou'' was the old road that connected Matsumae and Hakodate in the 
Edo era.  #6  

In the past, the Matsumae Line run.  The railroad connected Kikonai 
Town and Matsumae Town.  The terminus was Matsumae Station.  The 
population of Matsumae peaked at 20,072 in 1955.  #7

( For Fukuyama-kaidou, see Fukushima Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

INDUSTRY, CULTURE   

Distinctive industries in Matsumae are fishery, food processing 
( mainly Surume and Matsumae-zuke ), and sightseeing.  STATISTICS OF 
AGRICULTURE = fodder 192 hectares in 2015, pasture 154, and paddy rice 
X ( concealed ) ; dairy cows - ( zero ) in 2015.  STATISTICS OF 
FISHERIES = squid ( flying squid 540 tons in 2017, other squid 154 ), 

( continue ) --> snow crabs 442, atka mackerel 334, sea urchins 126, 
tuna 92, Pacific cod 61, ``konbu'' kelp ( cultured ) 57, octopus 49, 
sharks 47, olive flounder 6, shrimp/prawns 5, walleye pollock 4, 
abalone ( cultured ) 2, yellowtail 1, herring -, other fish 29+X, and 
other marine animals 11.  #8 

As for sightseeing, Matsumae Castle is famous for cherry blossoms.  In 
the castle, there is a botanical garden with 10,000 cherry trees of 250 
varieties.  #9 

There are many Buddhist temples in Matsumae, because it was a castle 
town.  In the past, monks were like civil servants.  They could read 
and write.  They documented names of people and places.  Today, 
observe buildings and gardens in temples. 
 
NOTE 
 
#1

The southernmost of Hokkaidou Island is Cape Shirakami.  It is located 
along National Route 228 in Matsumae Town.  The latitude is 41 degrees 
23.87 minutes north.  The longitude is 140 degrees 11.86 minutes east. 

If small islands are counted, the southernmost of Hokkaidou Prefecture 
is Kojima Island.  Kojima Island is also called Matsumae-kojima or 
Oshima-kojima.  It's an uninhabited island. 

Ooshima Island is the largest uninhabited island in the country.  The 
area is 9.7 square kilometers. Ooshima Island is also called Matsumae-
ooshima or Oshima-ooshima. 

Ooshima Island is a volcano.  It erupted in 1741 and caused tidal 
waves.  The tidal waves killed 1,467 people in the Matsumae Domain and 
566 people in the Tsugaru Domain.  ( In the Edo era, the Matsumae 
Domain ruled the south of Hokkaidou Prefecture.  The Tsugaru Domain 
ruled the west of Aomori Prefecture. ) 

Ordinary tourists cannot land Ooshima and Kojima islands.  In these 
islands, only shelters for fishing boats exist.  The islands are 
breeding grounds of seabirds such as murre ( mi'-ar ) ( ``umi-
garasu'' ) and streaked shearwater ( ``oo-mizunagui-dori'' ).

#2

Across the Tsugaru Strait, the distance between the Matsumae Peninsula 
and the Tsugaru Peninsula is 19.5 kilometers.  ( The Tsugaru Peninsula 
is located in Aomori Prefecture.  The length is measured between Cape 
Shirakami in Matsumae Town and Cape Tappi-zaki in Sotogahama Town. )

The distance between the Kameda Peninsula and the Shimokita Peninsula 
is 19.0 kilometers.  ( The Kameda Peninsula is located in Hakodate 
City.  The Shimokita Peninsula is locatd in Aomori Prefecture.  The 
length is measured between Cape Shiokubi in the former Toi Town ( now 
Hakodate City ) and Cape Ooma in Ooma Town. )

The Seikan Tunnel was constructed between the Matsumae Peninsula and 
the Tsugaru Peninsula.  The distance is longer, but the water is 
shallower. 

( For the Tsugaru Strait, see Hakaodate City in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. ) 
 
#3

In 1457, the warlord Nobuhiro Takeda ended Koshamain's War.  Nobuhiro 
Takeda was a vassal of the Andou clan.  Then, he inherited the Kakizaki 
clan, which was also a vassal of the Andou clan.  Around 1549, his 
great-grandson, Suehiro Kakizaki, made peace with Ainu chiefs.  

In 1593, Yoshihiro Kakizaki, son of Suehiro Kakizaki, became 
independent from the Andou clan.  He was able to ask Hideyoshi Toyotomi 
for the land of Ezo ( now Hokkaidou ).  Hideyoshi Toyotomi was the 
ruler during the Warring State era. 

The meeting between Hideyoshi Toyotomi and Yoshihiro Kakizaki was 
arranged by the Maeda clan.  The Maeda clan was the ruler of the Kaga 
Domain ( now Ishikawa Prefecture ).

In 1598, Hideyoshi Toyotomi died.  In 1599, Yoshihiro Kakizaki gave 
maps of Ezo to Ieyasu Tokugawa, who would become the founder of the 
Tokugawa Shogunate.  Maps were important for governing lands during the 
Warring States era.  Giving maps meant obedience.
 
In 1604, at the beginning of the Edo era, the Tokugawa Shogunate 
appointed Yoshihiro Kakizaki as the lord of the Matsumae Domain.  
Yoshihiro Kakizaki changed his family name from Kakizaki to Matsumae. 

There is a legend that the name ``Matsumae'' comes from Matsudaira and 
the Maeda clan ( Matsu + Mae ).  ``Matsudaira'' was Ieyasu Tokugawa's 
old family name. 

( For Nobuhiro Takeda, see Kaminokuni Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. ) 
 
#4

In Hokkaidou, 1st-class ( ``ikkyuu'' ) municipalities and 2nd-class 
( ``nikyuu'' ) municipalities were designated from 1899 to 1943.  The 
Hokkaidou government subsidized 2nd-class municipalities.  It was a 
pioneering period. 

The classification system was applied to villages and towns.  
Exceptions were Sapporo Ward ( Sapporo-ku ), Hakodate Ward ( Hakodate-
ku ), and Otaru Ward ( Otaru- ku ) designated in 1899. 

#5

As for the former Sumiyaki-zawa village, the name ``Sumi-yaki-zawa'' 
means ``charcoal, burn, stream'' in Japanese.  The name evokes an image 
of a rural area. 

Novelist Sei Itou was from Sumiyaki-zawa village.  He was influenced by 
the Irish novelist James Joyce.  He was one of the translators of 
Ulysses.  ( Ulysses is a novel written by James Joyce.  Its Japanese 
translation was published from 1931 to 1933. ) 

James Joyce published his novels in English.  He didn't use his native 
language Celtic in his work.  He died in Zurich, Switzerland.  Not only 
James Joyce but also Jonathan Swift, William Butler Yeats, and Oscar 
Wild were from Ireland.  They published their work in English.  They 
had the guts.

( For Sei Itou, see the Otaru West section of Otaru City, Shiribeshi 
  general sub-prefecture. )

#6

Fukuyama Castle is the former name of Matsumae Castle.  Fukuyama Castle 
was designated as National Treasure ( Kokuhou ).  It burned down in 
1949.  Currently, the only national treasure in Hokkaidou is a clay 
figurine, which was dug out from a potato field.

( For national treasure, see Hakodate City in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )
 
#7

Matsumae Town is a sister city with Oumi-hachiman City in Shiga 
Prefecture and Yanagawa Town ( now Date City ) in Fukushima Prefecture. 
The reasons date back to the Edo era as follows.

Merchants from Oumi-hachiman were called Hachiman-shounin.  They traded 
in Ezo island using Kitamae-bune ( North-bound ship ).  They based in 
the castle town of Matsumae.  They developed herring fishing grounds.  
( Hachiman-shounin was included in Oumi merchants ( Oumi-shounin ). )

( For Oumi merchant, see Esashi Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. )

Regarding Yanagawa Town, in 1807, the Tokugawa Shogunate directly ruled 
Ezo to prevent Russian southward movement.  The Tokugawa Shogunate 
judged that the Mastumae Domain's behavior towards Russians was bad. 

The vassals of Matsumae Domain were forced to moved to Yanagawa.  Half 
of the vassals became ``rounin'' ( jobless samurai ).  They lived in 
hardship for 14 years until the Matsumae Domain was returned to Ezo in 
1821. 

#8

Dried flying squid is called ``surume'' in Japanese.  Matsumae Town 
and neighboring Fukushima Town are the largest producers of dried 
flying squid in the country.

The tonnages of dried flying squid from the two towns account for 80 
percent of the national share.  In the late Meiji period ( about 100 
years ago ), herring fishing decreased and flying squid fishing 
prospered.

In the past, fishers processed flying squid into ``surume'' ( dried 
flying squid ).  As catches increased, after the 1970s, local companies 
started processing flying squid.  ( by Matsumae Town's website 
``History Of Matsumae Surume''  http://
www.town.matsumae.hokkaido.jp/hotnews/detail/00000228.html  last 
accessed in May 2019. ) 

Matsumae-zuke is pickles made with ``konbu'' kelp, dried flying squid, 
and herring roe.  A variety of Matsumae-zuke are sold at the souvenir 
shop ``Dosanko Plaza'' in Sapporo Station.  In the past, when herring 
catches were poor, Matsumae-zuke without herring roe was sold. 

#9

These are famous cherry blossom sites in Hokkaidou ; ( 1 ) Matsumae 
Castle ( in Matsumae Town, Oshima general sub-prefecture ), ( 2 ) 
Go-ryou-kaku Castle ( in Hakodate City, Oshima general sub-
prefecture ), ( 3 ) Rows of cherry trees that lead to Noboribetsu Hot 
Spring ( in Noboribetsu City, Iburi general sub-prefecture ), 

( continue ) --> ( 4 ) The Nijikken Road in Shizunai ( in what is now 
Shin-hidaka Town, Hidaka sub-prefecture ), and ( 5 ) Shiokari Pass ( in 
Wassamu Town in Kamikawa general sub-prefecture ).

These are cherry varieties and cherry blossom sites ; ( 1 ) Yaezakura 
( double-flowered cherry ) and many other varieties including Somei-
yoshino in Matsumae Castle, ( 2 ) Somei-yoshino in Go-ryou-kaku Castle, 
( 3 ) and Ezo-yamazakura ( Hokkaidou mountain cherry ) in Shizunai.

The cherry blossom season varies from year to year.  In Matsumae Town, 
it will be from late April to mid May.  In Shizunai Town ( now Shin-
hidaka Town ), it will be from early May to mid May.  ( In Toukyou, it 
will be from late March to early April. )

One of the oldest Somei-yoshino trees in the country grows at Matsumae 
Castle.  It was planted in 1882 ( Meiji 15 ).  Somei-yoshino is the 
most famous cherry variety in the country.  It was bred in Somei 
village near Edo ( now central Toukyou ) in the end of the Edo era.  
Somei village was located near present-day Ikebukuro.

Somei-yoshino was named after Mt. Yoshino, a famous cherry blossom site 
in Nara Prefecture.  The cherries in Mt. Yoshino are mountain cherries 
( not Somei-yoshino ). 

Somei-yoshino trees are clones.  If the soil and climate are the same, 
they grow into the same shape.  If you observe a row of the cherry 
trees, you'll find similarities in their height, trunk diameter, trunk 
twist, and flowering time.  Somei-yoshino trees are used as an 
indicator for the season of cherry blossom. 

Making plant clones are easier than animal clones.  Clones are more 
alike than siblings.  Plant clones are the predecessors of today's 
digital society ( by Nihei lab 2023-2026_t ).

( Single of the chapter ; ``Here I Sit'' by Ronettes.  I imagine the 
  broken heart of samurai.  --> http://youtu.be/ucvCAEldWo8 )

|                 |
|    FUKUSHIMA    |
|                 |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 2  |
 -----------------
AREA 187 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 173, CULTIVATED 0 ; COUNTY = MATSUMAE ; 
POP 8,111 ( 1990 ), 5,114 ( 2010 ) ; COUNTRY SIGN = SQUID WRESTLER. 
 
LOCATION   

Fukushima ( fu-ku-shi-ma ) is a town in Oshima general sub-prefecture, 
south Hokkaidou ( ``dou-nan'' ).  The area faces the Tsugaru Strait to 
the south.  The Fukushima River and the Shiriuchi River flow.

( UTF code ; Fukushima = \u798F\u5CF6. )

Forests cover 93 percent of the area.  Mt. Dai-sen-guen ( 1,072 
meters ) rises to the north.  Matsumae county consists of Matsumae Town 
and Fukushima Town and is located at the southern end of Hokkaidou.

NAME 

The name ``Fukushima'' is Japanese.  ``Fuku-shima'' means ``lucky, 
island.''  It's a good-luck name given by an oracle from Tsukizaki 
Shrine in 1624.  #1  #2 

HISTORY 

The first Japanese migrants to Ezo ( now Hokkaidou ) came from Ooshuu 
( now Touhoku region ) in 1189.  They were vassals of the Fujiwara 
clan, which were defeated by Yoritomo Minamoto.  They lived in what is 
now Yoshioka district, Fukushima Town.  ( Yoritomo Minamoto established 
the Kamakura Shogunate after he conquered Ooshuu. )  
 
In the mid 15th century, there was a fort whose name was Onnai-date in 
Yoshioka district.  The fort was destroyed by the Ainu forces during 
Koshamain's War.  In the Edo era, this area was ruled by the Matsumae 
Domain. Around 1617, a gold mine opened in Mt. Dai-sen-guen.  The gold 
miners were Catholic.  #3 
 
In the beginning of the Meiji period, the Matsumae Domain was renamed 
the Tate Domain.  The area that is now Fukushima Town belonged to the 
Tate Domain.  

In 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), two administrative offices ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) 
were established ; one was for Fukushima village and Shirafu village ; 
the other was for Miyanouta village, Reihige village, and Yoshioka 
village.

In 1900 ( Meiji 33 ), Fukushima Village ( 1st-class municipality ) was 
established by the merger of Fukushima village and Shirafu village.  In 
1902 ( Meiji 35 ), Yoshioka Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established by the merger of Miyanouta, Reihige, and Yoshioka villages. 

( UTF code ; Miyanouta = \u5BAE\u6B4C.  Reihige = \u793C\u9AED. 
  Shirafu = \u767D\u7B26.  Yoshioka = \u5409\u5CA1. )

In 1944 ( Shouwa 19 ), Fukushima Town was established.  In 1955 
( Shouwa 30 ), Fukushima Town and Yoshioka Village were merged.

( For 1st-class and 2nd-class municipalities, see the note of Matsumae 
  Town. )

( For Koshamain's war, see Kaminokuni Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. )

( For the Tate Domain, see Matsumae Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC

The town hall of Fukushima is located near the mouth of the Fukushima 
River.  National Route 228 ( Fukuyama-kaidou ) runs.  #4
 
In the past, the Matsumae Line ran.  It connected Matsumae Station in 
Matsumae Town and Kikonai Station in Kikonai Town.  There was Yoshioka-
kaitei Station on the Kaikyou Line.  It was an undersea station in the 
undersea Seikan Tunnel.  #5

INDUSTRY

Distinctive industries in Fukushima are fishery and forestry. 
STATISTICS OF AGRICULTIRE = paddy rice X ( concealed ) in 2015 ; dairy 
cows - ( zero ) in 2015.  STATISTICS OF FISHERIES = squid ( flying 
squid 895 tons in 2017, other squid 26 ), sea urchins 85, tuna 66, 

( continue ) --> octopus 48, seaweed ( ``konbu'' kelp 24, other seaweed 
10 ), atka mackerel 6, olive flounder 6, sea trout 6, right-eye 
flounder 1, abalone 1, herring 0, other fish 10, and other marine 
animals 3. 

Fishing ports are located in Fukushima, Yoshioka, Shirafu, and Urawa.  
The catch of squid accounts for about 80 percent of the fishery.  
Fukushima and neighboring Matsumae are the leading producers of 
``surume'' ( dried flying squid ) in the country.

As for forestry, the main trees are cedar ( si'-dar ), fir 
( ``todo-matsu'' ), and spruce ( ``ezo-matsu'' ).  The cedar is sold 
under the trade-name Dou-nan-sugui ( South Hokkaidou Cedar ).  #6 

Sightseeing destinations include Seikan Tunnel Memorial Hall, Tadataka 
Inou's Hokkaidou Survey Memorial Park, and Yokozuna Chiyo-no-Yama and 
Chiyo-no-Fuji Memorial Hall.  ``Yokozuna'' means the champions of Sumou 
wrestling. 

The country sign ( gateway sign ) of Fukushima Town features a squid 
Sumou wrestler.  The name of the roadside station is Yokozuna-no-sato, 
which means the hometown of Sumou champions.

( For dried squid ( ``surume'' ), see Matsumae Town in Oshima general 
  sub-prefecture. ) 

( For Tadataka Inou, see Kaminokuni Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. )

NOTE  

#1

The book ``Fukushima Town History Volume 2 ( Fukushima-chou-shi 2 ),'' 
published in 1995 states the etymology of Fukushima as follows ; 
``Once upon a time, Orikanai village was on the verge of being 
abandoned, because of a large fire and poor fishing catches.  

( continue ) --> At the time, there was an oracle from Tsukisaki 
Shrine, which said, `The name Orikanai must be changed to Fukushima.'  
The name `Fukushima' may have referred to Fukuyama.'' 
 
Fukuyama was the castle town on of the Matsumae Domain ( Now Matsumae 
Town. )  Matsumae and Fukushima are neighbouring municipalities.  The 
name ``Fuku-shima'' means ``lucky, island'' and ``Fuku-yama'' means 
``lucky, mountain.''   

The original name of Fukushima was ``Orikanai.''  It comes from 
``horka-nay'' in Ainu language, which means ``reverse, river.''  It 
signified the Fukushima River.  Seawater entered the estuary at high 
tide, or the river mouth meandered in a U-shape.  The etymology is the 
same as Horokanai Town in Kamikawa general sub-prefecture. 

#2

These are old Shintou shrines in Fukushima Town.  ( 1 ) Tsukisaki 
Shrine, reconstructed in 1492, ( 2 ) Fukushima Daijinguu Shrine, 
reconstructed in 1573, ( 3 ) Shirafu Daijinguu Shrine, established in 
1655 by the Matsumae Domain, and ( 4 ) Kamaya Shrine, established in 
1772. 

Shirafu Daijinguu Shirine preserves the traditional dance ``Shirafu-
arauma-odori ( Shirafu, wild horse, dance ).''  The dance represented 
the power of the Matsumae Domain.  It has been designated as National 
Intangible Cultural Property ( Mukei-bunka-zai ).

Kamaya Shrine is located in Shiogama district.  The name ``Shio-gama'' 
is Japanese, which means ``salt, pot.''  It was derived from the 
production of salt for sardine fishing.

#3

The mountain's name ``Dai-sen-guen'' is Japanese.  It means ``large, 
thousand, houses.''  It signified that there were 1,000 houses of gold 
miners.  During the Edo era, the Tokugawa Shogunate banned 
Christianity, but the regulation was loose in Ezo Island ( now 
Hokkaidou ), because the island was seen as an outer land 
( ``gaichi'' ). 

In 1637, Christians revolted in Kyuushuu Island.  In 1939, Christians 
in Ezo Island were executed.  At the site of the gold mine in Mt. Dai-
sen-guen, there is an inscription that reads ``Christian martyrdom 
( ma'r-ta-dam ).''

( For gold, see Naka-tonbetsu Town in Souya general sub-prefecture. )
 
#4

As for the Fukuyama Road ( Fukuyama-kaidou ), the name ``Fukuyama'' 
means the former castle town of the Matsumae Domain.  The Fukuyama 
Road connected Fukuyama Castle ( Matsume Castle ) and the Port of 
Hakodate.  Matsumae Domain's samurai traveled the road.  The distance 
was 100 kilometers.

The Fukuyama Road was located in Ezo ( now Hokkaidou Prefecture ).  The 
Matsumae Road was located in Tsugaru ( now Aomori Prefecture ).

( For the Matsumae Road, see Hokuto City in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )

#5

The Seikan Tunnel is an undersea railway tunnel, whose length is 54 
kilometers.  The Channel Tunnel or Le tunnel sous la Mance between 
England and France is 50 kilometers.  Gotthard Base Tunnel in 
Switzerland is 57 kilometers.  Cf. The total length of tunnels in a 
coal mine reached 200 kilometers.

( For coal mining tunnel, see Akabira City in Sorachi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

Yoshioka-kaitei Station in Fukushima Town was built at 150 meters below 
sea level.  The station was used for sightseeing and the maintenance of 
the undersea tunnel.  The station was closed in 2006 and demolished in 
2014. 
 
Yoshioka district was the base for the undersea tunnel.  The tunnel was 
constructed between 1961 and 1988.  The place flourished during the 
construction of the tunnel.  It's also the first place where Japanese 
migrants settled in the 12th century.  

( For the Seikan undersea tunnel, see Kikonai Town and Hakodate City in 
  Oshima general sub-prefecture. ) 

#6

Cedar trees are not common in Hokkaidou Island.  So, cedar pollen fever 
is rare.  But, white birch ( ``Shira-kanba'' ) and alder ( ``Han-no-
ki'' ) cause pollen fever, although the effects are weaker than cedar.

I didn't hear about pollen fever when I was a child.  It was a good 
time.  ( I caught pollen fever after I moved to Toukyou.  When I remove 
the motorbike cover, yellow dust ( pollen ) rises up like smoke. )

In the past, cedar trees were small.  In addition, people may have 
developed a tolerance for dust and pollen, because many people smoked. 
 
Cedar trees grow even on wet land.  Farmers in Honshuu Island planted 
cedar trees on abandoned rice paddies near valley heads.  During the 
high economic growth period, from the late 1960s to the early 1970s, 
farm successors left the countryside.  Land use was changed from 
agricultural to forest.  Taxes were reduced. 

( Single of the chapter ; ``In Die Tribulationis.''  In the West, 
  songs were used to study the bible.  -->  
  http://youtu.be/EjmYGemJcx4 )

|                 |
|    SHIRIUCHI    |
|                 |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 3  |
 -----------------
AREA 197 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 172, CULTIVATED 13 ; COUNTY = KAMIISO ; 
POP 6,383 ( 1990 ), 5,074 ( 2010 ) ; COUNTRY SIGN = SHINKANSEN, OYSTER, 
& NIRA GIRL.   
 
LOCATION 

Shiriuchi ( shi-ri-u-chi ) is a town in Oshima general sub-prefecture, 
south Hokkaidou ( ``dou-nan'' ).  Shiriuchi is located in the southwest 
of the Oshima Peninsula.  This part is called the Matsumae Peninsula.  
Shiriuchi faces the Tsugaru Strait to the east.  Forests cover 87 
percent of the area.

( UTF code ; Shiriuchi = \u77E5\u5185. ) 

NAME 

The name ``Shiriuchi`` comes from ``cir-ot-i'' in Ainu language.  It 
means ``bird, exist, place.''  It signified the Shiriuchi River.  In 
the Edo era, Shiriuchi was a hunting ground for hawks and falcons.  
Young hawks and falcons were caught and shipped to Edo ( now central 
Toukyou ) for falconry.  #1

HISTORY

In 1205, the feudal lord of Kai Province ( now Yamanashi Prefecture ) 
started placer mining along the Shiriuchi River.  It was an order from 
the Kamakura Shogunate.

In the 15th century, there was a fort called Wakimoto-date ( da'-te ).  
It was one of the 12 forts built by the Andou clan, who came from 
Tsugaru ( now Aomori Prefecture ).  During the Edo era, the area what 
is now Shiriuchi was ruled by the Matsumae Domain.  #2

After the Meiji period, in 1871 ( Meiji 4 ), the administrative office 
( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) of Shiriuchi village was established.  It was a 
continuation of village chief ( ``kochou'' ) system from the Edo era.

In 1881 ( Meiji 14 ), the administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) 
of Shiriuchi village was re-established.  It covered Kotaniishi 
village.  ( by ``New Shiriuchi Town History ( Shin-Shiriuchi-
choushi )'' published in 2015. ) 

In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), Shiriuchi Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established by the merger of Kotaniishi village and Shiriuchi village.  
In 1967 ( Shouwa 42 ), Shiriuchi Town was established.

( UTF code ; Kotaniishi = \u5C0F\u8C37\u77F3. )

( For 2nd-class municipality, see the note of Matsumae Town. ) 

TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC 

The town hall of Shiriuchi is located near the mouth of the Shiriuchi 
River.  National Route 228 ( Fukuyama-kaidou ) runs.  ( The name 
``Fukuyama'' means the castle town of the Matsumae Domain. )
 
The national highway runs along the coast, goes inland near the town 
hall, and leads to Fukushima Town.  Previously, there was Oshima-
shiriuchi Station on the Matsumae Line.

The Matsumae Line was operated by the Japanese National Railways 
( Kokutetsu ) until 1987.  It was abolished in 1988, shortly after the 
dismantling of the Japanese National Railways and JR Hokkaidou took 
over the operation.

There was Shiriuchi Station on the Kaikyou Line.  It became a roadside 
station ( Michi-no-eki ).  Visitors can observe the Shinkansen coming 
out of the undersea tunnel.  #3 

INDUSTRY 

Distinctive industries in Shiriuchi are agriculture, fishery, forestry, 
and power generation.  STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = paddy rice 422 
hectares in 2015, soybeans 124, buckwheat 85, pasture 54, fodder 18, 
spinach 14, wheat 3, potatoes 2, flowers 1, orchards 1, 

( continue ) --> and other vegetables 95+X ( including Nira ( Chinese 
chive or gynmigit ) ) ; dairy cows 112 head in 2015.  STATISTICS OF 
FISHERIES = squid ( flying squid 296 tons in 2017, other squid 20 ), 
salmon 66, sea urchins 30, seaweed 13 ( ``konbu'' kelp ), 

( continue ) --> shellfish 11 ( oysters ), olive flounder 10, right-eye 
flounder 6 ( marbled flounder ), octopus 5, anchovy 4, yellowtail 4, 
mackerel 1, sharks 1, herring 0, other fish 20, and other marine 
animals 3.
 
According to a study on agricultural regionalization, the town was 
classified as a ``small-scale, single-crop, paddy rice'' area.  Due to 
the decrease in rice consumption, paddy rice decreased and Nira 
( Chinese chive ) increased.

Today, Shiriuchi is the largest producer of Nira in Hokkaidou.  Nira is 
cultivated in greenhouses and harvested from January to October.  Nira 
is the vegetable included in Gyouza dumplings.  #4  #5 
 
NOTE  
 
#1

In the Edo era, the Matsumae Domain traded hawks and falcons.  ( In 
Japanese, hawk is ``taka'' and falcon is ``hayabusa.'' )  There were 
many bird-catching places in Ezo ( now Hokkaidou ).  Shiriuchi was one 
of the best places. 

Hawk catchers ( ``taka-machi'' ) searched for the nests of hawk and 
falcon nests in summer.  They wintered in Ezo, caught hawk chicks in 
spring, and handed them to the Matsumae Domain.

Young hawks and falcons were shipped to Edo ( now central Toukyou ).  
The Tokugawa Shogunate trained them for falconry.  Feathers of hawks 
were also traded for making arrow fletchings. 

Ainu people also caught hawks and falcons.  They hid in huts nearby 
streams.  Using salmon as lure, Ainu hunters caught the birds by 
hooking their legs.  The birds were sold to Japanese people.  

Ainu people kept hawks to take feathers.  They kept young bears too.  
Ainu people called bears ``kamuy,'' which means ``god.''  Today, 
Japanese people keep bears.  

( For bear ranch, see Noboribetsu City in Iburi general sub-
  prefecture. )

Pacific sea eagles ( ``oo-washi'' ) and white-taled eagles ( ``ojiro-
washi'' ) inhabit in western Hokkaidou.  Their feathers were also 
traded in the Edo era.  Ainu people called Pacific sea eagles 
``kapatcir-kamuy,'' which means ``flat-bird, god.''

#2

Shiriuchi has a long history of Japanese migration.  Wakimoto Shrine in 
Kitsune-goshi district was established in 1239.  Raikou Shrine in the 
center of the town was established in 1244.  Shiriuchi Hot Spring was 
discovered by gold miners in 1247.  These are the oldest ones in 
Hokkaidou.

In the past, priests at Shintou shrines were like village chiefs.  They 
documented names of places and poeple.  ( Many Shintou shrines in  
Toukyou are newer than those in Shiriuchi. )
 
Fort Wakimoto-date was located in Wakimoto district in Shiriuchi Town.  
It was located near Cape Kitsune-goshi.  The fort was destroyed by the 
Ainu forces during Koshamain's War. 

( For gold, see Naka-tonbetsu Town in Souya general sub-prefecture. )

( For Koshamain's War, see Kaminokuni Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. )
 
Cape Kitsune-goshi is located to the south of Wakimoto district.  
Wakimoto is one of the oldest villages in Hokkaidou.  The name 
``Kitsune-goshi'' is Japanese, which means ``fox, go through.''

To the west of Cape Kitsune-goshi is Kotaniishi district.  To the west 
of Kotaniishi district is Cape Yagoshi.  The name ``Ya-goshi'' is 
Japanese, which means ``arrow, go through.''  Cape Yagoshi is a sea 
cliff whose altitude is 121 meters.  The road along the sea is a dead 
end.  

#3

At the observatory of Shiriuchi Roadside Station, there is a time table 
for the Shinkansen trains ( bullet trains ) entering and leaving the 
undersea Seikan Tunnel.

The former Shiriuchi Station on the Kaikyou Line became Yu-no-sato 
Signal Station.  The signal station is the end of the undersea tunnel.  
There was another Yu-no-sato Station on the former Matsumae Line.

The name ``Yu-no-sato'' is Japanese, which means ``village of hot 
spring.''  Yu-no-sato is a district along the Shiriuchi River.  There 
is Shiriuchi Hot Spring.

( For the Kaikyou Line, see Kikonai Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )
 
#4

Oysters are cultivated in the open sea in Shiriuchi.  It's a unique 
method.  Generally, oysters are cultivated along the coast.  Shiriuch 
Oysters are advertised at tasting events in Susukino.  ( Susukino is 
the largest entertainment district in Hokkaidou.  The location is 
Chuuou Ward, Sapporo City. )

As for power generation, Hokkaidou Electric Power Company runs
Shiriuchi Thermal Power Plant.  The location is Motomachi district.  
The power plant uses heavy oil and Orimulsion ( ori-ma'l-sion ).  
Orimulsion is a new fossil fuel. 

In summer, the power plant sells electricity to Toukyou Electric Power 
Company.  Toukyou metropolitan areas consume a huge amount of 
electricity in summer.  The climate is hot and humid.

The electricity is transmitted through the undersea Seikan Tunnel.  The 
power cables are direct current ( ``choku-ryuu'' ).  Direct current is 
suitable for high voltage and long-distance transmission.  ( Generally, 
electricity is transmitted as alternating current ( ``kouku-ryuu'' ). )
  
#5

Shiriuchi is the hometown of Saburou Kitajima, a famous Enka singer.  
Enka is a traditional Japanese musical style.  There is Saburou 
Kitajima Memorial Museum in Hakodate City. 

When I guided an excursion in Toukyou, we passed by the Enka singer's 
residence.  It was near the former site of another Enka singer's 
residence.  Students didn't know them.  Time has passed.  

This is a lyric from one of Kitajima's songs.
``Yosaku-wa-ki-wo-kiru   Hey-hey-hoo.''
  ( Yosaku cuts trees, hey hey hoo. )'

When I hear the song, I imagine a forestry office ( ``eirin-sho'' ) in 
Hokkaidou. 

( For Enka singer, see Makkari Village in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

( Single of the chapter ; ``If You Want To Be A Bird'' by The Holy 
  Modal Rounders.  --> http://youtu.be/_vmvmxpDp-Q )

|                 |
|     KIKONAI     |
|                 |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 4  |
 -----------------
AREA 222 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 196, CULTIVATED 7 ; COUNTY = KAMIISO ; 
POP 7,826 ( 1990 ), 5,341 ( 2010 ) ; MEAN ANNUAL TEMP 8.6 C ( AV 1981-
2010 ), PRECIPI 1,309 MM ; COUNTRY SIGN = NAKED BOY.  

LOCATION

Kikonai ( ki-ko-na-i ) is a town in Oshima general sub-prefecture, 
south Hokkaidou ( ``dou-nan'' ).  Kikonai is located in the southwest 
of the Oshima Peninsula.  This part is called the Matsumae Peninsula.  
Kikonai faces the Tsugaru Strait.  Forests cover 88 percent of the 
area.

( UTF code ; Kikonai = \u6728\u53E4\u5185. )

NAME  

The name ``Kikonai'' is derived from ``rir-o-nay'' in Ainu language.  
It means ``tide, enter, stream.''  It signified the Kikonai River.  
Saltwater entered the estuary at high tide.

HISTORY 

In the 15th century, Fort Nakano ( Nakano-date ) existed.  That was one 
of 12 forts in Ezo ( old Hokkaidou ).  In the Edo era, the Matsumae 
Domain ruled this area.  The name ``Kikonai'' appears on the map drawn 
by the Matsumae Domain in 1624.

After the Meiji period, in 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), the administrative office 
( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) of Kikonai village was established.  In 1902 
( Meiji 35 ), Kikonai Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established by the merger of four villages ; Izumisawa, Kamatani, 
Kikonai, and Sakkari.

( UTF code ; Izumisawa = \u6CC9\u6CA2.  Kamatani = \u91DC\u8C37. 
  Sakkari = \u672D\u82C5. )

In 1919 ( Taishou 8 ), Kikonai Village ( 1st-class municipality ) was 
established.  In 1942 ( Shouwa 17 ), Kikonai Town was established.  #1  
#2

( For 1st-class and 2nd-class municipalities, see the note of Matsumae 
  Town. )

( For the former 12 forts, see Kaminokuni Town in Hiyama sub-
  prefecture. ) 

TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC   

The town hall of Kikonai is located near the mouth of the Kikonai 
River.  National Route 228 ( Fukuyama-kaidou ) runs.  Kikonai is a 
station on the Hokkaidou Shinkansen.  Kikonai is also the terminus 
of the Dou-nan Isaribi Railway.  #3

The name ``Isaribi'' is Japanese.  It means ``fishing lamp.''  Lamps 
for squid fishing float on the sea.  The fishing season is from June to 
December.

In the past, the Esashi Line and the Matsumae Line ran.  Kikonai was a 
transportation hub in the Matsumae Peninsula.  Governmental offices 
were located in the center of the town, such as a summary court, a 
forestry office ( ``eirin-sho'' ), and a railway yard by Japanese 
National Railways ( Kokutetsu ).  #4

( For squid fishing, see Hakodate City and Matsumae Town in Oshima 
  general sub-prefecture. ) 

INDUSTRY, CULTURE

Distinctive industries in Kikonai are fishery, agriculture, animal 
husbandry ( including Hakodate Wagyuu Beef ), and forestry ( including 
Dou-nan Cedar ).  STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = paddy rice 291 hectares 
in 2015, pasture 188, fodder 26, orchards 5, potatoes 4, spinach 2, 

( continue ) --> radishes 1, soybeans 1, and yams 1 ; dairy cows 291 
head in 2015, and beef cattle X ( concealed ).  STATISTICS OF 
FISHERIERS = salmon 65 tons in 2017 ( autumn salmon ), sea urchins 22, 
seaweed 18 ( ``konbu,'' ``wakame,'' and ``iwa-nori'' kelps ), 

( continue ) --> shellfish ( abalone 1, other shellfish 15 ), olive 
flounder 14, right-eye flounder 9 ( marbled flounder ), octopus 8, 
mackerel 5, sharks 4, Pacific cod 1, yellowtail 1, herring 0, other 
fish 7, and other marine animals 6.  #5 

The country sign ( gateway sign ) of Kikonai signifies Kanchuu-misogui 
festival.  The name ``Kanchuu-misogui'' means ``winter, purification'' 
in Japanese.  The festival has been held since 1831.  

During the festival, practitioners enter the sea in January.  The 
temperature of the sea in winter is warmer than lakes and rivers, 
however, the location is Hokkaidou Island.
 
NOTE 
 
#1

In 1869 ( Meiji 2), during the Boshin War, the battle of Kikonai 
happened.  The Meiji government forces advanced from Esashi to 
Hakodate.  The former Tokugawa Shogunate forces counter-attacked. 

One of the routes taken by the Meiji government forces was the Kikonai 
Mountain Road ( Kikonai-sandou ).  The commander of the former Tokugawa 
Shogunate was Keisuke Ootori.  He studied English, Western medicine, 
and Western military tactics.  His English teacher was Manjirou Johon. 

Keisuke Ootori was imprisoned after being defeated in the Boshin War.  
He was released and served for the Meiji government.  He became Japan's 
ambassador to Korea.  

( Keisuke Ootori --> http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%8Ctori_Keisuke )

( Manjirou John --> http://
  en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakahama_Manjir%C5%8D )

( For the Boshin War, see Hakodate City in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

#2

In 1871 ( Meiji 4 ), Kanrin-maru ( a ship ) sank at Cape Saraki in 
Kikonai.  Along National Route 228, there is a monument to the ship 
that reads, ``End Of Kanrin-mauru ( Kanrin-maru-shuuen-no-chi ).''
The anchor of the ship is displayed in Kikonai Town's museum.  
( Thinking about an anchor calms me down. ) 

Kanrin-maru was a battleship, which the Tokugawa Shogunate purchased 
from the Netherlands.  It was the first Japanese ship that crossed the 
Pacific Ocean.  In 1860, to complete the conclusion of the Japan-US 
Treaty of Amity and Commerce, Kanrin-maru departed from the Port of 
Shinagawa on January 13th. 

Kanrin-maru arrived in the Port of San Francisco on February 26th.  The 
captain was Kaishuu Katsu and the interpreter was Manjirou John.  On 
the outbound voyage, bad weather caused Japanese crew to be sea-sick.  
Americans took over piloting.

In 1867, the steam of Kanrin-maru was removed.  It became a cargo ship. 
After the Meiji period, the new Meiji government owned the ship.  It 
sank during a heavy storm in September 1871, off the coast of Kikonai. 

At the time, Kanrin-maru carried 401 migrants from Shiroishi Castle in 
the Sendai Domain ( now Miyagui Prefecture ) to Hokkaidou.  No one died 
in the the shipwreck.
  
#3

Hokkaidou Shinkansen opened in 2016.  It is operated by JR Hokkaidou.  
In the same year, the Esashi Line changed to the Dou-nan Isaribi 
Railway.  The operation was transferred from JR Hokkaidou to South 
Hokkaidou Railway Company ( Dou-nan-isaribi-tetsudou ). 

The Esashi Line ran along the Tsugaru Strait.  It connected Go-ryou-
kaku Station in Hakodate City and Kikonai Station.  In the past, the 
Esashi Line entered mountains from Kikonai Town and leached to Esashi 
Town on the sea of Japan side.

In 2012, Kikonai High School was closed.  In 2014, a section between 
Kikonai Station and Esashi Station on the Esashi Line closed.  High 
school students are the main users of local railways.  Part of the 
abolished Esashi Line was converted to a trolley line for sightseeing.

Kikonai Station on Hokkaidou Shinkansen is also the terminus of the 
Kaikyou Line.  The word ``kaikyou'' means ``strait'' in Japanese.  The 
Kakyou Line runs through the undersea Seikan Tunnel.  After the opening 
of the Hokkaidou Shinkansen, only freight ( fre'it ) trains and special 
trains for group tours use the Kaikyou Line.  

The Hokkaidou Shinkansen uses the same undersea tunnel, but the 
Shinkansen is not categorized as the Kaikyou line.  The track gauge 
( distance between two rails ) is different between the Shinkansen and 
conventional railways including the Kaikyou Line.

The Seikan Tunnel was made as a dual-gauge ( three rails ) and double-
truck railway.  It means that there are six rails in the tunnel.  The 
Shinkansen ( bullet train ) is standard gauge ( 1,435 millimeters ).  
Conventional railways are narrow gauge ( 1,067 millimeters ).  Narrow 
gauge is cheaper to build.

The former Matsumae Line connected Kikonai Station and Matsumae 
Station.  The railway was abolished in 1988.  It was two years after 
the privatization of JNR ( Japanese National Railways ).  In the past, 
there were JNR-branded ashtrays in all passenger cars.  The ashtrays 
were made of thick steel.  They made a good sound. 

There were many railways and their branch routes in Hokkaidou.  The 
main lines are called ``hon-sen'' and branches are called ``shi-sen.''  
In the past, many politicians promised many railways.

( For undersea tunnel, see Fukushima Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )

#4

Kikonai was a transportation hub in the Matsumae Peninsula.  
Woodworking factories were operated near the station.  In the past, 
even television sets were made from wooden boards.  Today, in Japanese 
offices, all desks and chairs are made of steel.  ( My office is like a 
used book store, whose space is occupied by steel shelves. ) 

When I had a zoom meeting with a professor in Vietnam, I found a wooden 
desk and wooden door in the office.  When I visited a professor in Sao 
Paulo, Brazil, I also found a wooden desk, wooden chairs, and a wooden 
book shelf in the office.

#5

As for dairy farming in Kikonai, in 1897 ( Meiji 30 ), a milk 
collection facility was built.  Horse-drawn carriage transported raw 
milk to Toubetsu Trappist Monastery.  In 1919 ( Taishou 8 ), condensed 
milk butter ( ``ren-nyuu'' butter ) was produced in Kikonai.
 
( For dairy farming, see Eniwa City in Ishikari sub-prefecture. ) 

( For monastery, see Hokuto City in Oshima general sub-prefecture. )

( Single of the chapter ; ``Etude Opus 25 No. 1'' by Chopin.  The 
  final part reminds me of entering the winter ocean.  --> 
  http://youtu.be/hLldVAK5U04 )

|                 |
|     HOKUTO      |
|                 |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 5  |
 -----------------
AREA 397 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 302, CULTIVATED 36 ; POP 39,741 ( 1990 ) 
( KAMIISO 30,950, OONO 8,791 ), 48,032 ( 2010 ) ( + ) ; MEAN ANNUAL 
TEMP 8.3 C ( AV 1981-2010 ), PRECIPI 1,159 MM ; COUNTRY SIGN = 
SHINKANSEN & OLD BUILDING. 
  
LOCATION 

Hokuto ( ho-ku-to ) is a city in Oshima general sub-prefecture, south 
Hokkaidou ( ``dou-nan'' ).  The area faces Hakodate Bay and the Tsugaru 
Strait.  The Moheji River, the Oono River, and the Kunebetsu River 
flow. 

( UTF code ; Hokuto = \u5317\u6597. )

NAME 

The name ``Hokuto'' is Japanese.  It means the Big Dipper that shines 
in the northern sky.  It was named by public offering when Kamiiso Town 
and Oono Town were merged in 2006.  #1

( UTF code ; Kamiiso = \u4E0A\u78EF.  Oono = \u5927\u91CE. )

HISTORY 

In the mid 15th century, Mobetsu-date ( da'-te ) ( a fort ) was built 
near the mouth of the Moheji River.  It was one of the former 12 forts 
built by the Andou clan.  During the Edo era, the area was ruled by the 
Matsumae Domain. 

After the Meiji period, in 1880 ( Meiji 13 ), an administrative office 
( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) was established for Oono and five other villages. 
Another administrative office was established for Kamiiso and four 
villages. 

( UTF code ; ``kochou-yakuba'' = \u6238\u9577\u5F79\u5834. )

In 1885 ( Meiji 18 ), an administrative office was established in 
Moheji village.  It covered Ishibetsu village.  In 1900 ( Meiji 33 ), 
Kamiiso Village ( 1st-class municipality ) and Oono Village ( 1st-class 
municipality ) were established.  

In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), Mobetsu Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established by the merger of Ishibetsu and Moheji villages.  In 1918 
( Taishou 7 ), Kamiiso Town was established.  In 1955 ( Shouwa 30 ), 
Kamiiso Town merged Mobetsu Village.  In 1957 ( Shouwa 32 ), Oono Town 
was established.

( UTF code ; Ishibetsu = \u77F3\u5225.  Mobetsu = \u8302\u5225.  Moheji 
  = \u8302\u8FBA\u5730. ) 

( For 1st-class and 2nd-class municipalities, see the note of Matsumae 
  Town. ) 

( For the former 12 forts in south Hokkaidou, see Kaminokuni Town in 
  Hiyama sub-prefecture. ) 

CITY HALL, TRAFFIC, POPULATION 

The city hall of Hokuto is located near the mouth of the Oono River.  
It was the town hall of Kamiiso Town.  National Route 228 ( Fukuyama-
kaidou ) and the Dou-nan Isaribi Railway run along Hakodate Bay.  #2 

The population of Hokuto City increased.  In the southern part of the 
municipality, residential, commercial, and manufacturing areas are 
continue from the center of Hakodate City.
 
In the northern part, National Route 227 ( Oono-kokudou ) runs through 
the Oono Plain.  The national highway crosses the ridge and leads to 
Esashi Town.  Shin-hakodate-hokuto Station on the Hokkaidou Shinkansen 
is located near the border with Nanae Town.  It used to be Oshima-oono 
Station on the Hakodate Main Line.  

( For Fukuyama-kaidou, see Fukushima Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For the Dou-nan Isaribi Railway, see Kikonai Town in Oshima general 
  sub-prefecture. ) 

INDUSTRY 

Distinctive industries in Hokuto are manufacturing, agriculture, and 
fishery.  STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = paddy rice 1,198 hectares in 
2015, fodder 659, beans ( soybeans 239, adzuki beans 9, other beans 
5 ), green onions 131, pasture 110, radishes 57, tomatoes 50, potatoes 
39, spinach 37, cabbages 28, Chinese cabbages 16, lettuces 14, 

( continue ) --> cucumbers 14, orchards 11, strawberries 9, yams 6, 
flowers 4, onions 2, other vegetables 139+X, other grains 8, and other 
fruits 1 ; dairy cows 336 head in 2015.  STATISTICS OF FISHERIES = 
salmon 387 tons in 2017, anchovy 242, yellowtail 88, shellfish 
( abalone 1, Manila clam 1, other shellfish 74 ), herring 51, 

( continue ) --> right-eye flounder 37, Pacific cod 34, olive flounder 
26, crabs 25, squid ( flying squid 21, other squid 1 ), seaweed 21, sea 
urchins 15, mackerel 13, octopus 11, sea trout 7, puffer fish 2, red 
seabream 1, other fish 44+X, and other marine animals 10.

On satellite images, a straight line stretches from Hokuto City to 
Hakodate Bay.  The line is a jetty of Taiheiyou Cement's Kamiiso 
Factory.  The limestone deposit for the cement industry was discovered 
in 1872.  It was discovered by Benjamin Smith Lyman, who was an 
American mining scholar invited by the Meiji government.  #3

Paddy rice cultivation started in Oono district in the late 17th 
century.  It was the first rice cultivation in Hokkaidou.  #4

As for sightseeing, the old-fashioned building drawn on the country 
sign ( gateway sign ) is Toubetsu Trappist Monastery ( mo'na-steri ).  
The monastery is located to the west of Hokuto City, near the border 
with Kikonai Town.  The site has old-fashioned red-brick buildings and 
tree-lined avenues.  #5

NOTE 

#1

The former Kamiiso Town was located along Hakodate Bay.  The name 
``Kamiiso'' was derived from Kamiiso county, which was established in 
1869 ( Meiji 2 ).  The county's name was suggestion by Takeshirou 
Matsuura, who explored Ezo ( now Hokkaidou ) in the Edo era.

The name ``Kami-iso'' is Japanese.  It means ``upper, coast.''  The 
word ``upper'' refers to the west, where the castle of the Matsumae 
Domain was located.

The former Oono Town was located inland.  The name ``Oo-no'' is 
Japanese, which means ``large, plain.''   The name ``Oono'' is old.  It
was documented in ``Matsjmae Island Village Record ( Matsumae-tou-gou-
chou ),'' published in 1700.

Takeshirou Matsuura may have derived the name ``Kamiiso'' from the 
Kamiiso Road ( Kamiiso-kaidou ) in Tsugaru ( now Aomori Prefecture ).
 
The Matsumae Road ( Matsumae-kaidou ) in Tsugaru was also called the 
Kamiiso Road.  The Matsume Road connected Aburakawa in Aomori City and 
Min-maya in Sotoga-hama Town, Aomori Prefecture.  Min-maya was a port 
to Matsumae.

Thus, the Matsumae Road was located in Honshuu Island.  The Fukuyama 
Road ( Fukuyama-kaidou ) was located in Ezo ( now Hokkaidou ), 
connecting Matsumae and Hakodate.  Fukuyama was the castle town of the 
Matsumae Domain.

( For Fukuyama-kaidou, see Fukushima Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For Matsumae Island Village Record, see Hakodate City in Oshima 
  general sub-prefecture. )
 
#2

We took a bus excursion that traveled on National Route 228 ( Fukuyama-
kaidou ).  I saw many concrete wave-breaking blocks along the coast of 
Hokuto City.  Hokuto is one of the largest cement producing 
municipalities in the country.

Wave-breaking blocks are known as Tetrapods.  The name was the patent 
of Neyrpic, a French company.  I heard this story from Professor Tsuguo 
Sunamura at University of Tsukuba in 1995.
 
``When executives of Neyrpic visited Japan, they found many fake 
  Tetrapods along the coast.  So, Nippon Tetrapod, a branch of Neyrpic, 
  was established in 1961.

  ( continue ) --> The fake Tetrapods in Japan had metal hooks.  They 
  were easy to install, but they were less durable.  The metal hook 
   corrodes ( co-ro'-ds ).''

( Professor Tsuguo Sunamura, taken in Shimoda City, March 1995 ( during 
  my student year ) --> HTTP://2242.MOOO.COM/POSI/95IZU/1452.JPG ; 
  http://www.researchgate.net/publication/408166334  

  Tetrapods with metal hooks, taken in Shimoda City, March 1995 --> 
  HTTP://2242.MOOO.COM/POSI/95IZU/1473.JPG ;  http:// 
  www.researchgate.net/publication/408166057 ) 

#3

As for cement industry in Hokuto City, Taiheiyou Cement Kamiiso Factory 
extracts limestone at Garou Mine.  The cement is transported to cargo 
ships via a long jetty.  The length is two kilometers.  The jetty makes 
a long line on the map.  
 
In 1884 ( Meiji 17 ), a cement factory was established.  In 1890 
( Meiji 23 ), Hokkaidou Cement was established.  In 1915 ( Taishou 4 ), 
Asano Cement acquired Hokkaidou Cement.

After World War II, Asano zaibatsu was dismantled. ( The word 
``zaibatsu'' means a large conglomerate. )  Asano Cement changed its 
name to Nihon Cement.  In 1998 ( Shouwa 63 ), Taiheiyou Cement was 
established by the merger of Nihon Cement and Chichibu Onoda Cement.  
The product name ``Asano Cement'' was used until 1998. 

Concrete is made from cement.  Cement is made from limestone, gravel, 
and water.  Limestone is self-sufficient in the small country.  Cities 
are like concrete jungles.  Limestone mountains are like skeletons. 

Today, almost all utility poles are made of concrete.  The oldest 
concrete utility pole is still used in Hakodate City.  It was installed 
in 1923 ( Taishou 12 ) to prevent it from burning by fire.  ( I like 
old utility poles, although tourist destinations remove utility 
poles. ) 

#4

The former Oono Town is the birth place of paddy rice cultivation in 
Hokkaidou.  It started in the Edo era.  There is a stone monument that 
commemorates the first paddy rice.  The inscription reads ; 

``In 1692, a farmer Sakuemon opened a paddy filed of 450 tsubo ( 0.15 
hectares ) using natural water and gained rice of 10-hyou.''  ( One 
``hyou'' of rice is 60 kg. )
 
The record was excellent at the time. Today, a good paddy field has a 
yield of 600 kg per 0.1 hectares.  Such paddy field is called ``jippyo-
dori ( 10-hyou gaining paddy ).''

According to oral tradition, paddy rice cultivation in the former Oono 
Town dates back to the Kanbun period ( 1661-1672 ) and 1685.  ( by 
Yoshiaki Ishizuka & Tatsuzou Hoshino eds. 1994.  ``Rice Cultivation Of 
Hokkaidou ( Hokkaidou-no-inasaku )'' published by Hokunoukai ( Sapporo 
City ). )

Kametarou Ootomo ( 1834-1897 ) was an engineer in the Edo era.  He 
instructed agriculture in Oono village ( now Hokuto City ).  In 1866, 
at the end of the Edo era, he moved to Sapporo with villagers and 
started developing.

Kametarou Ootomo constructed the Ootomo Canal.  It is now called the 
Sousei River and flows through the center of Sapporo City.  Kametarou 
Ootomo was a disciple of Sontoku Ninomiya ( 1787-1856 ).

Kyuuzou Nakayama was the owner of a post station ( ``eki-tei'' ) in 
what is now Kita-hirosima City.  He was known as the father of cold-
climate paddy rice cultivation.  In the Meiji period, he obtained rice 
seeds in Oono village, and succeeded in paddy rice cultivation in the 
Ishikari Plain.

( For rice cultivation under a cold climate, see Kita-hiroshima City in 
  Ishikari sub-prefecture. )

( For Sontoku Ninomiya's Thought, see Hokuryuu Town in Sorachi general 
  sub-prefecture ; Toyokoro Town in Tokachi general sub-prefecture. ) 
 
#5

Toubetsu Trappist Monastery sells butter, ice cream, and cookies.  The 
monastery was established in 1896 ( Meiji 29 ).  The monks started 
dairy farming.  Dairy farming spread to farmers in neighboring Kikonai 
village in the Meiji period.

A monastery is a place where monks live together.  It is Catholic.  It 
is also Catholic to have statues of Mary and saints, although they do 
not pray to them.  Praying to idols is called idolatry ( aido'-latry ).

In the past, ordinary people could not read, so churches made statues 
to explain their religion.  ( Today, university teachers use PowerPoint 
too much.  The situation is like idolatry.  Students need to read 
bibles. )  

( Single of the chapter ; ``Untitled'' by Radiohead.  This 
  reminds me of the north sky.  --> http://youtu.be/L_PvBLtvZto )
