|                 |
|    HAKODATE     |
|                 |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 6  |
 -----------------
AREA 678 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 549, CURLTIVATED 15 ; POP 328,493 
( 1990 ) ( HAKODATE 307,249, MINAMI-KAYABE 8,911, ESAN 5,729, TOI 
4,677, TODOHOKKE 1,927 ), 279,127 ( 2010 ) ; MEAN ANNUAL TEMP 9.1 C 
( AV 1981- 2010 ), PRECIPI 1,152 MM, SNOW 381 CM ; COUNTRY SIGN = 
CHURCH & SEAGULL. 
  
LOCATION 

Hakodate ( ha-ko-da-te ) is a city in Oshima general sub-prefecture, 
south Hokkaidou ( ``dou-nan'' ).  The area faces Hakodate Bay, Tsugaru 
Strait, and the Pacific Ocean.  It occupies the southeast of the Oshima 
Peninsula.  This part is called the Kameda Peninsula. 

( UTF code ; Hakodate = \u51FD\u9928. )

Mt. Hakodate stands from Hakodate Bay.  The height is 334 meters.  
( Toukyou Tower is 333 meters. )  The area of Hakodate was newly formed 
in 2004 by the merger of Hakodate City, Toi Town, Minami-kayabe Town, 
Esan Town, and Todohokke Village.  #1  #2  #3 

NAME 

The name ``Hako-date'' means ``box, fort'' in Japanese.  It referred to 
a fortress, whose shape resembled a box.  The name of the fort was 
Usukeshi-date.  It was managed by the Kouno clan in the 1450s.  The 
Kouno clan was one of the vassals of the Andou clan, who built 12 forts 
in what is now south Hokkaidou.

HISTORY   

Koshamain's War occurred in 1457.  Koshamain was a chief of Ainu 
people.  During the war, Usukeshi-date, so-called Hako-date, was 
destroyed by the Ainu forces.

In the Edo era, the Port of Hakodate was managed by the Matsumae Domain 
and later by the Tokugawa Shogunate.  The port opened for American 
ships in 1854 under the Convention of Kanagawa ( Japan-US Treaty of 
Peace and Amity ).  #4

In the beginning of the Meiji period, Hakodate became the battle ground 
of the Boshin War ( 1868-1869 ).  In 1869 ( Meiji 2 ), the Hokkaidou 
Development Commission was established.  In 1871, the head office of 
the Hokkaidou Development Commission moved to Sapporo.  #5

In 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), Hakodate ward ( Hakodate-ku ) was established.  
This ``ward'' was an administrative district and a municipality.  ( A 
municipality has a local government. )  In 1899 ( Meiji 32 ), Hakodate 
Ward ( Hakodate-ku ) was established.  This ``Ward'' was a 
municipality, which was designated especially in Hokkaidou.

In 1922 ( Taishou 11 ), Hakodate City was established.  In 1939 
( Shouwa 14 ), Hakodate City merged Yunokawa Town ( 1st-class 
municipality ).  Yunokawa Town was located around Yunokawa Hot Spring.

( UTF code ; Yunokawa = \u6E6F\u5DDD. )

In 1966 ( Shouwa 41 ), Hakodate City merged Zenigame-zawa Village ( the 
former 1st-class municipality ).  Zenigame-zawa Village was located 
near Hakodate Airport.  In 1973 ( Shouwa 48 ), Hakodate City merged 
Kameda City.  #6

( UTF code ; Kameda = \u4E80\u7530.  Zenigame-zawa = 
  \u92AD\u4E80\u6CA2. ) 

( For 1st-class municipality, see the note of Matsumae Town. )

( For the Andou clan and Koshamain's War, see Kaminokuni Town in Hiyama 
  sub-prefecture. ) 

( For Ward  ( ``ku'' ) in Hokkaidou, see Kushiro Town in Kushiro 
  general sub-prefecture. )

CITY HALL, TRAFFIC, POPULATION
  
The city hall of Hakodate and the Port of Hakodate are located at the 
foot of Mt. Hakodate.  National Route 5 and the Hakodate Main Line 
start from the port.

Hakodate was the gateway to Japan in the late 19th century, during the 
Edo era.  Hakodate was the gateway to Hokkaidou from the late 19th 
century to the late 20th century.

The Seikan train ferry ( Seikan-renraku-sen ) connected Hakodate and 
Aomori.  The name ``Sei-kan'' is an abbreviation of Aomori and 
Hakodate in Kanji characters.  #7

The population peaked in 1980 at 345,165 ( including municipalities of 
Hakodate, Toi, Esan, Minami-kayabe, and Todohokke ).  The population 
decreased, because of ; ( 1 ) end of pelagic fishing ( deep-sea 
fishing ), ( 2 ) opening of the Seikan Tunnel, ( 3 ) increase in air 
travelers, and ( 4 ) motorization and suburbanization.  #8

INDUSTRY 

Distinctive industries in Hakodate are sightseeing, fishery, fish 
processing, trade, and ship-building.  STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = 
potatoes 393 hectares in 2015, pasture 260, paddy rice 73, cabbages 54, 
beans ( adzuki beans 19, soybeans 9, other beans 1 ), fodder 27, 

( continue ) --> orchards 18, Chinese cabbages 16, green onions 10, 
flowers 8, broccoli 7, spinach 3, tomatoes 2, onions 1, cucumbers 1, 
and other vegetables 47+X ; dairy cows 467 head in 2015.  STATISTICS 
OF FISHERIES = squid ( flying squid 5,660 tons in 2017, other squid 
377 ), walleye pollock 5,162, sardines 4,889, yellowtail 4,045, 

( continue ) --> anchovy 2,414, mackerel 1,717, seaweed ( ``konbu'' and 
``gagome-konbu'' kelps ) 1,463, salmon 1,105, octopus 919, sea urchins 
691, tuna 687, Pacific cod 615, right-eye flounder 248, shellfish 
( abalone 3, other shellfish 237 ), sharks 122, sand lance 119, 

( continue ) --> sea trout 92, olive flounder 46, atka mackerel 38, 
horse mackerel 33, shrimp/prawns 27, herring 21, puffer fish 21, marine 
mammals 18, ``kichiji'' 14, crabs 4, bonito 2, ``hatahata 1,'' other 
fish 571+X, and other marine animals 122.  #9

As for sightseeing, famous tourist sites include Go-ryou-kaku Castle, 
Mt. Hakodate, night view from Mt. Hakodate, old Western-style 
buildings, old churches in Motomachi district, brick warehouses on an 
old wharf, Ekini market, Yunokawa Hot Spring, and landscape of the 
slopes ( ``saka-michi'' ) filmed in movies and TV dramas.  #10

Go-ryou-kaku Castle was built inland to avoid artillery from foreign 
ships.  In 1854, the Tokugawa Shogunate singed the Japan-US Treaty of 
Peace and Amity ( Nichibei-washin-jouyaku ).  From 1857 to 1866, the
Tokugawa Shogunate constructed Go-ryou-kaku Castle.  It became the last 
castle of the shogunate and fell during the Boshin War.

Today, tourists enjoy climbing up an observation tower in front of the 
castle.  The height of the tower is 100 meters.  Visitors can look down 
on the pentagonal shape of the castle.  The name ``Go-ryou-kaku'' is 
Japanese.  It means ``five, point, fort.''  Cannons could be placed at 
the points. 

Western-style buildings that were built in the Meiji period has been 
preserved, for example, Hokkaidou Government Hakodate Branch Office and 
Hakodate Ward Public Hall.  They were constructed after the great fires 
in the Meiji period.  #11

At Ekini market, tourists can enjoy squid fishing.  They can eat the 
squid on the spot.  Ekini market used to be a morning fair in front of 
Hakodate Station.  #12

NOTE
  
#1  

Mt. Hakodate is also called Ga-gyuu-san in Japanese.  It means ``lying-
cow mountain.''  The shape of the mountain resembles a lying cow.

In the Meiji period, the top of Mt. Hakodate was removed by 14 meters, 
because guns were placed.  Mt. Hakodate was a military base from the 
Meiji period to the early Shouwa period ( before Wold War II ).

Some people say that the nature of Mt. Hakodate remains, because only 
military personnel entered the mountain.  But, after the fort called 
Hako-date was built in the 15th century, people cut down native trees.  
In the past, trees were used not only as building materials but also 
as energy ( firewood ). 

Today, cedar trees can be seen along the tourist road of Mt. Hakodate.  
Buses go on the road and a ropeway runs above it.  The cedar forest 
has been cultivated since the Edo era ( about 200 years ago ).

The night view from Mt. Hakodate is counted as one of the World Top 
Three Night Views, along with Hong Kong and Naples.  Sometimes Nagasaki 
is counted as the top three instead of Hakodate.  Nagasaki is also a 
port city.

World Top Three rankings were counted by Japanese people.  In the past, 
they traveled all over the world by ship.  Japan is a maritime country.

( For the World Top Three Sunsets, see Kushiro City in Kushiro general 
  sub-prefecture. )

Mt. Hakodate is a mountain and a land-tied island.  The city hall of 
Hakodate is located on a sandbar.  A sandbar that links a land-tied 
island is called ``tombolo.''  In the Meiji period, the central 
business district ( CBD ) of Hakodate was located on a tombolo.

The CBD moved inland.  It was moved from a tombolo to the front of 
Hakodate Station in the Shouwa period.  Currently, it is located in 
front of Go-ryou-kaku Castle.  When I visited Hakodate in 2013, I found 
many ``For Rent'' signs on the second floor near Hakodate Station.
 
#2

The Tsugaru Strait has two necks.  ( A neck is a narrow part of the 
sea. )  The east neck is located between the Kameda Peninsula and the 
Shimokita Peninsula.  ( The Kameda Peninsula is located in Hakodate 
City and the Shimokita Peninsula is located in Aomori Prefecture. )

The west neck is located between the Matsumae Peninsula and the 
Tsugaru Peninsula.  ( The Matsumae Peninsula is located in Matsumae 
Town and the Tsugaru Peninsula is located in Aomori Prefecture. )  The 
Seikan Tunnel was constructed under the west neck.  The west neck is 
longer but shallower.

( For the Seikan undersea tunnel, see Fukushima Town in Oshima general 
  sub-prefecture. ) 

( For the Tsugaru Strait, see Matsumae Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. ) 
   
#3

The former Toi Town ( now Hakodate City ) was located in the southern 
end of the Kameda Peninsula.  The name ``Toi'' comes from ``toi-o-i'' 
in Ainu language.  It means ``edible soil, exist, place.''  

( UTF code ; Toi = \u6238\u4E95. ) 

It signified that there was diatomite ( dai'-ato-maito ).  Diatomite is 
called ``keisoudo'' in Japanese.  It is a fossil of diatom 
( ``keisou'' ).  It's edible.

In the Edo era, Toi was a village.  The name was documented in 
``Matsumae Island Village Record ( Matsumae-tou-gou-chou ),'' which was 
published in the Tenpou period ( 1831-1845 ) of the Edo era.

( UTF code ; Matsumae-tou-gou-chou = \u677E\u524D\u5CF6\u90F7\u5E33. )

After the Meiji period, in 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), the administrative office 
( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) of Toi village was established.  In 1902 ( Meiji 
35 ), Toi Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was established.  In 1919 
( Taishou 8 ), Toi Village ( 1st-class municipality ) was established. 
In 1968 ( Shouwa 43 ), Toi Town was established.

( UTF code ; ``kochou-yakuba'' = \u6238\u9577\u5F79\u5834. )

The other side of Toi, across the Tsugaru Strait, is Ooma Town in 
Aomori Prefecture.  Ooma is famous for Ooma's tuna.  The same tuna is 
caught in Toi.  Toi's tuna is cheaper than Ooma's tuna.  ( This is 
heard in the 2010s. )  The taste of the tuna caught in the Tsugaru 
Strait is good.  The tuna eat flying squid. 

( ``Matsumae Island Village Record'' published in 1700 --> http://
  ja.wikisource.org/wiki/
  %E6%9D%BE%E5%89%8D%E5%B3%B6%E9%83%B7%E5%B8%B3 )

( ``Matsumae Island Village Record'' published in 1834 --> http://
  www.digital.archives.go.jp/gallery/0000000323 ) 

( For 1st-class and 2nd-class municipalities, see the note of Matsumae 
  Town. ) 

* * * 

The former Minami-kayabe Town ( now Hakodate City ) was located to the 
east of the Kameda Peninsula.  The name ``Minami-kayabe'' means 
``south, kayabe.''

( For the name ``Kayabe,'' see Mori Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

In 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), the administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) 
of Osatsube village was established.  In 1880 ( Meiji 13 ), the 
administrative office of Usujiri village was established.  Those 
villages had continue from the Edo era.

In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), Osatsube Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) and 
Usujiri Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) were established.  In 1959 
( Shouwa 39 ), Minami-kayabe Town was established by the merger of 
Osatsube Village and Usujiri Village.

( UTF code ; Minami-kayabe = \u5357\u8305\u90E8.  Osatsube = 
  \u5C3E\u672D\u90E8.  Usujiri = \u81FC\u5C3B. ) 

In 1975, a hollow clay figurine was dug from a potato field in Minami-
kayambe Town.  It is the only National Treasure ( Kokuhou ) in 
Hokkaidou.  Previously, Matsumae Castle ( Fukuyama Castle ) was 
designated as National Treasure, but it was destroyed by fire ( not 
by war ). 

( For Matsumae Castle, see Matsumae Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )

* * *

The former Esan Town ( now Hakodate City ) was located in the eastern 
end of the Kameda Peninsula.  The area faces the Tsugaru Strait.

The name ``Esan'' comes from ``e-san-i'' in Ainu language.  It means 
``head, stick out, place.''  It signified Mt. Esan ( 618 meters ).  Mt. 
Esan is an active volcano.  The bare soil stands out from the Pacific 
Ocean.

In the the Edo era, Shirikishinai village was established.  After the 
Meiji period, in 1872 ( Meiji 5 ), the administrative office 
( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) of Shirikishinai village was established.  It was 
a continuation of village chief ( ``kochou'') system from the Edo era.

In 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), the administrative office of Shirikishinai 
village was re-established.  In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), Shirikishinai 
Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was established.  In 1964 ( Shouwa 
39 ), Shirikishinai Town was established.  In 1985 ( Shouwa 60 ), the 
name was changed to Esan.  It was named after Mt. Esan.

( UTF code ; Esan = \u6075\u5C71.  Shirikishinai = 
  \u5C3B\u5CB8\u5185. )

* * *

The former Todohokke Village in Hakodate City was located in the 
eastern end of the Kameda Peninsula.  It was the smallest municipality 
in Hokkaidou.  The area was 25 square kilometers, as same as Atsubetsu 
Ward in Sapporo City.

( UTF code ; Todohokke = \u6934\u6CD5\u83EF. )

The name ``Todohokke'' comes from ``tu-pok-ke'' in Ainu language.  It 
means ``mountain foot, under, place.''  It signified the mountain foot 
of Mt. Esan.  The Kanji characters for ``Todohokke'' are very difficult 
to read. 

In the Edo era, the area of Todohokke was included in Osatsube village. 
After the Meiji period, in 1880 ( Meiji 13 ), the administrative office 
of Todohokke village was established.  In 1919 ( Taishou 8 ), Todohokke 
Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was established.
 
#4

The Port of Hakodate was a natural port.  Japanese people called it 
``tsuna-shirazu ( rope, no need ).''  They also called it Tomoe Port, 
which meant a spiral port, because of the spiral-like topography.

The Japan-US Treaty of Peace and Amity ( Nichibei-washin-jouyaku ) was 
concluded in 1854.  It was concluded between the Tokugawa Shogunate and 
the United States of America.  The ports of Hakodate and Shimoda were 
opened.  ( Shimoda is located in the Izu Peninsula, Shizuoka 
Prefecture. )

( The name ``Japan'' is English.  In Japanese, it is called ``Nihon.''  
  So, the name ``the Sea of Japan'' is 100 percent English. )  

Before the treaty was concluded, the commander of the East India 
Squadron, Matthew Perry, had appeared in many ports in Japan.  Japanese 
people were surprised to see the American ships.  They moved by steam 
engine.  ( Japanese ships were moved by the wind. )  The flagship was 
Susquehanna.  It was called the black ship ( ``kuro-fune'' ).
 
( For steamship, see Takikawa City in Sorachi general sub-prefecture. )

In 1858, the Ansei Five-Power Treaties ( Ansei-gokakoku-jouyaku ) was 
signed between Japan ( the Tokugawa Shogunate ), the USA, the UK, 
Russia, the Netherlands, and France.  The most influential of the 
treaties was the Treaty of Amity and Commerce Between the United States 
and Japan ( Nichibei-shuukou-tsuushou-jouyaku ).

Then, along with Hakodate, the ports of Kanagawa ( now Yokohama ), 
Nagasaki, Hyougo ( now Koube ), and Niigata were opened.  The Port of 
Shimoda was closed instead of Kanagawa.  Foreign settlements were built 
near the ports.  Cities of Edo ( now central Toukyou ) and Oosaka were 
opened for foreign visitors.  

Thus, Japanese isolationist policy was ended, due to the effort 
( pressure ) of Matthew Perry.  The isolationist policy continued for 
250 years.  It was a period that resembled a perfect closed circle.

#5

The Boshin War was a civil war between the new Meiji government forces 
and the former Tokugawa Shogunate forces.  The final phase is called 
the Hakodate War.  The former Tokugawa Shogunate forces aimed to 
establish the Republic of Ezo.  ( Ezo is what is now Hokkaidou. ) 

( For the Boshin War, see Kikonai Town, Matsumae Town, Mori Town in 
  Oshima general sub-prefecture ; Assabu Town, Esashi Town, and Otobe 
  Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. )
 
Takeaki Enomoto ( 1836-1908 ) was the commander of the former Tokugawa 
Shogunate forces.  He learned English from John Manjirou and studied in 
the Netherlands.  After the defeat in the Boshin War, Takeaki Enomoto 
was appointed by Kiyotaka Kuroda and worked for the Hokkaidou 
Development Commission. 

Kiyotaka Kuroda ( 1840-1900 ) was a samurai in the Satsuma Domain ( now 
Kagoshima Prefecture. )  He served as an officer in the new Meiji 
government forces.  After the war, he became the undersecretary ( vice 
minister ) of the Hokkaidou Development Commission.  He invited 
American advisors to Hokkaidou.

Toshizou Hijikata ( 1835-1869 ) was an officer of the former Tokugawa 
Shogunate forces.  He was killed in the Hakodate War.  In the Edo era, 
he was the vice-commander of Shinsen-gumi.  Shinsen-gumi was the 
special forces of the Tokugawa Shogunate.
 
Shinpachi Nagakura ( 1839-1915 ) was the strongest swordsman in 
Shinsen-gumi.  There is a small plaque to him near the main gate of 
Hokkaidou University.  He was born and raised in the Edo branch of the 
Matsumae Domain.  ( Edo was the center of today's Toukyou.  The 
Matsumae Domain ruled what is now south Hokkaidou in the Edo era. ) 

Shinpachi Nagakura learned several swordsmanship schools in Edo.  Then, 
he fled the Matsumae Domain and joined Shinsen-gumi.  After Shinsen-
gumi was abolished, he participated in the Boshin War as a member of 
the Tokugawa Shogunate forces.

He didn't participated in the Hakodate War.  ( The Hakodate War was the 
final phase of the Boshin War. )  If he had participated in the 
Hakodate War, he would have to fight against the Matsumae Domain, which 
was his former monarch.

In the Meiji period, Shinpachi Nagakura taught swordsmanship in Kabato 
Prison and in Agricultural College of Touhoku Imperial University ( now 
Hokkaidou University ).

Of all the swordsmanship schools that Shinpachi Nagakura learned, he 
got the highest rank ( ``menkyo-kaiden'' ) in Shintou-munen-ryuu.  
Shintou-munen-ryuu features powerful sword strokes. 

( For Shinpachi Nagakura, see Tsukigata Town in Sorachi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

( For Takeaki Enomoto, see Utashinai City in Sorachi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 
 
#6

As for the former Kameda City ( now Hakodate City ), the name ``Kame-
da'' is Japanese.  It means ``turtle, paddy field.''  It's a good-luck 
name.  According to an old Chinese legend, turtle ( ``kame'' ) lives 
10,000 years and cranes ( ``tsuru'' ) live 1,000 years.  

Kameda is an old name whose Kanji characters were written on a map 
drawn in the 1660s.  The place may have been called ``Kanmete'' in the
1440s.  ( by ``Hakodate City Local History Archive'' http://
adeac.jp/hakodate-city/text-list/d100030/ht000540 )

( UTF code ; Kanmete = \u30AB\u30F3\u30E1\u30C6. )

( For good-luck name ( auspicious name ), see Chitose City in Ishikari 
  sub-prefecture. )

In the early 17th century, the Matsumae Domain built a guardhouse 
( ``bansho'' ) in Kameda.  It was moved to Hakodate in the 18th 
century.  After the Meiji period, from 1879 ( Meiji 12 ) to 1881 
( Meiji 14 ), seven administrative offices for 25 villages were 
established in Kameda county. 

In 1902 ( Meiji 23 ), Kameda Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established.  At the time, five villages were merged ; Kameda, 
Kamiyama, Kaji, Kikyou, and Ishikawa.  In 1919 ( Taishou 8 ), 
Kameda Village ( 1st-class municipality ) was established.

In 1962 ( Shouwa 37 ), Kameda Town was established.  In 1971 ( Shouwa 
46 ), Kameda City was established.  In 1973 ( Shouwa 48 ), Kameda City 
was merged by Hakodate City.  Kameda City existed only for two years 
and one month.  ( Fushimi City in Kyouto Prefecture existed only for 
one year and eleven months. )

The former Kameda City is located west of Hakodate City.  The area 
includes Go-ryou-kaku Station and Kikyou Station on the Hakodate Main 
Line.  The address of Go-ryou-kaku Station is Kameda-honchou, which 
means ``Kameda, main town'' in Japanese.

( For 1st-class and 2nd-class municipalities, see the note of Matsumae 
  Town. ) 

#7

The Seikan train ferry operated from 1908 ( Meiji 41 ) to 1988 ( Shouwa 
63 ).  In September 1954, the train ferry Touya-maru was sunk in the 
Touya-maru Typhoon ( Typhoon Marie ).  The shipwreck killed 1,155 
people.  Then, plans for the Seikan undersea tunnel moved forward.

( Typhoon Marie also caused a great fire in Iwanai Town in Shiribeshi 
  general sub-prefecture. )

( For shipwreck, see the note of Suttsu Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

There is a famous song called Tsugaru-kaikyou-fuyu-gueshiki ( Tsugaru 
Strait Winter Scene ).  The lyrics depict the people on the Seikan 
train ferry.  These are like this ; ``People returning north are quiet. 
 They only hear the sound of the sea.''
 
This was my sales pitch to invite master students ; ``It's good to live 
in the north island for two years.  Cities in Honshuu Island are too 
hot.''

( For the Seikan Tunnel, see Fukushima Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

Travelers from Honshuu Island would think that Hakodate and Sapporo are 
close, because they are located in the same prefecture.  But, it takes 
4 hours by express train, 4 hours by expressway, and 5.5 hours by bus.  
The route goes around Uchiura Bay ( Volcano Bay ).

#8

The Exclusive Economic Zone ( EEZ, a 200 nautical mile zone ) was 
activated in the 1980s.  ( 200 nautical mile is about 370 kilometers. ) 
It ended North Ocean Fishing ( deep-sea fishing, ``hokuyou-gyogyou'' in 
Japanese ).

In the past, the ports of Hakodate, Kushiro, and Nemuro were bases for 
North Ocean Fishing.  ( These ports also included North Ocean Fishing ; 
Otaru, Yoichi, Furubira in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture ; Rumoi in 
Rumoi sub-prefecture ; Wakkanai in Sourya general sub-prefecture ; 
Monbetsu and Abashiri in Okhotsk general sub-prefecture. )

Groups of ships departed these ports and catch salmon, crabs, and 
walleye pollock.  The waters were the northern Pacific Ocean, near 
the Kamchatka Peninsula in Russia and Alaska in the USA.  

Hakodate was a base for mother-ship ( ``bosen'' ) type fishing.  
Kushiro and Nemuro were bases for mother-port ( ``bokou'' ) type 
fishing.  The mother-ship equipped a factory to make canned salmon and
crabs, and minced wall-eye pollock ( ingredients for ``kamaboko'' 
( fish paste ) ).

( Incidentally, there is ``mother'' in ``sea'' in French and Japanese.  
  Namely, ``mer'' and ``mere'' in French, and ``haha'' and ``umi'' in 
  Japanese. )

( UTF code ; ``haha''( Japanese )  = \u6BCD.  ``mere'' ( French ) = 
  m\u00E8re.  ``umi'' ( Japanese ) = \u6D77. )

( For North Ocean Fishing, see Furubira Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )
 
#9

As for trade in Hakodate City, the main importing countries are Russia 
( coal, fish ), China ( fish ), and South Korea ( petroleum products ).
The main exporting country is Panama ( flag-of-convenience ships ) ( as 
of 2011, by Hakodate Customs ).  Coal from Russia is used in the cement 
industry in Hokuto City.

As for ship-building, Hakodate Dock manufactures bulk carriers, cement 
carriers, and fishing boats.  As for fishery, squid are caught near the 
coast from June to December.  During the fishing season, fishing lamps 
float on the sea.  It is a fantastic scene.  

( Fishing lamps on the Tsugaru Straight, taken in September 1998 
  --> HTTP://2242.MOOO.COM/POSI/98HOK/6052.JPG ; http://
  www.researchgate.net/publication/408166517 ) 

#10 

Traditional port cities are leaning topographically.  For example, 
Hakodate, Nagasaki, Sao Fransisco, and Rio de Janeiro ( by Nihei lab 
2023-2026_t ).  Slopes in Hakodate are straight and lead to the sea.  
The landscape is unique.  Japan is a mountainous country and many roads 
curve along contours. 

( For slope, see Otaru City in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture. )

( For straight road, see Yuni Town in Sorachi general sub-prefecture. )
 
#11 

The center of Hakodate suffered many fires.  Large fires occurred in 
1879 ( Meiji 12 ), 1899 ( Meiji 32 ), 1907 ( Meiji 40 ), 1934 ( Shouwa 
9 ), and 1945 ( Shouwa 25, air raids ).  There is a buffer zone near 
the city hall, where Tochi-no-ki ( horse chestnut, marronnier ) trees 
are planted.

In Honshuu Island, people say ``Mochi-no-ki trees prevent the spread of 
fire.''  Mochi-no-ki is bird-lime holly, a kind of forever-
green broad-leaved tree.  Bird-lime ( ``tori-mochi'' ) was extracted 
from Mochi-no-ki trees.

When I was a child, my neighbors caught birds using bird-lime, such as
warbling white-eye ( ``mejiro'' ).  Warbling white-eye is expensive.  
The bird sings in a beautiful sound.  There are competitions of the 
bird singing ( ``naki-awase'' ).  Today, capturing wild birds is 
prohibited by law. 
  
#12

I visited Hakodate in May 2013 and May 2017.  In both years, a Toshizou 
Hijikata Look-alike Contest was held at Go-ryou-kaku Castle.  The 
winner in 2017 was a woman.  Toshizou Hijikawa was the commander of the 
former Tokugawa Shogunate forces.  He dead in Hakodate at the end of 
the Boshin War. 

As for tram in Hakodate, a very old car runs.  In the old car, a young 
ticket seller is on board.  If you see a red tram, you will be happy.  
( It must be a Coca-cola tram. )

The last stop on the tram is Yunokawa Hot Spring.  The hot spring is 
called Hakodate's Oku-zashiki, which means the parlor of Hakodate.  
Yunokawa Hot spring was opened by the Matsumae Domain in 1653.

In Yunokawa Hot Spring, there was a nice and inexpensive hotel, which 
we stayed during excursions.  Today, it is difficult to stay at the 
hotel, because it became popular with foreign tourists.  The price went 
up.

Local food in Hakodate includes Lucky Pierrot hamburgers, Madalena 
cookies from Trappistine Monastery, curry and rice, and the grilled-
chicken lunch-box of Hasegawa Store.

Gotou-ken restaurnt opened in the Meiji period.  Its curry and rice 
is popular.  Nowadays, tourists enjoy curry and rice even for breakfast
at hotel buffets.

As for Lucky Pierrot hamburgers ; ( 1 ) It's popular than McDonald in 
Hakodate, ( 2 ) It serves whale hamburgers, ( 3 ) It serves large 
hambergers, and ( 4 ) The shops are located near high schools.  The 
food in port cities is hearty and delicious.

( For food in port city, see Kushiro City in Kushiro general sub-
  prefecture ; Nemuro City in Nemuro sub-prefecture. ) 

Curry was brought to Japan via the British Navy.  The original place is 
is India, which was the vassal state of the British Empire.  Naval 
cities appeal in Japan their curry and rice such as Yokosuka and 
Sasebo.  Indian curry is a soup.  Japanese curry is like a sauce, 
because naval crews could eat it on traveling warships.

In Hokkaidou, curry was introduced not only in Hakodate but also in 
Sapporo.  At Sapporo Agricultural College, curry and rice were served 
for American teachers and Japanese students 

( For curry and rice, see Hokkaidou University in Kita Ward, Sapporo 
  City, Ishikari sub-prefecture. )
 
Curry is basically a soup.  If a family serves curry and ``miso'' 
( soybean paste ) soup for dinner, they retain an old fashion.  Curry 
and rice has been eaten by Japanese people for a long time.

In the past, farmers had many children.  Children were always hungry 
and liked curry and rice.  Mothers cooked ``miso'' soup and curry so 
that all family members could eat curry.

Brazilian people ate ``feijoada'' on Saturdays and Wednesdays.  
``Feijoada'' is a soup made with black beans and intestines.  Japanese 
people ate curry and rice on Saturdays.  Those dishes provided stamina. 
Those were special dishes for laborers.  ( I ate leftover curry on 
Sundays. )

There was a TV drama called ``Shin-ya-shokudou ( Midnight Dinner ).''  
One story featured about butter rice in Hakodate City.  Butter rice is 
rice topped with butter and soy sauce. 

Professor Tooru Ikeda at Faculty of Letters, Hokkaidou University,  
recommended butter rice too.  He was from Hokkaidou.  Butter rice is 
popular in Hokkaidou.  ( Butter rice --> http://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/
%E3%83%90%E3%82%BF%E3%83%BC%E3%81%94%E9%A3%AF )

( Single of the chapter ; ``Set The Night To Music'' by Roberta Flack. 
  --> http://youtu.be/BNajXJ9ScbM )

|      NANAE      |

|  MAP 2 - NO. 7  |
 -----------------
AREA 217 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 129, CULTIVATED 26 ; COUNTY = KAMEDA ; 
POP 23,963 ( 1990 ) 28,463 ( 2010 ) ( + ) ; COUNTRY SIGN = SWAN, 
LAKE, & MOUNTAIN. 
  
LOCATION

Nanae ( na-na-e ) is a town in Oshima general sub-prefecture, south 
Hokkaidou ( ``dou-nan'' ).  Nanae is an inland town.  The Kunebetsu 
River flows south and empties in Hakodate Bay.

( UTF code ; Nanae = \u4E03\u98EF. )

Mt. Komagatake ( 1,131 meters, volcano ) rises to the north.  Lake 
Oonuma is located at the foot of Mt. Komagatake.  Lake Oonuma and 
Mt. Komgatake are designated as Oonuma Quasi National Park ( Oonuma-
kokutei-kouen ). 

NAME 

The name ``Nanae'' comes from ``naa-nay'' in Ainu language, which means 
``many, stream.''  Or, it comes from ``nam-nay'' in Ainu language, 
which means ``cold, stream.''  Both signified Nanae-hama beach along 
Hakodate Bay.

Nanae-hama beach now belongs to Hokuto City.  Nanae Town has a long 
history of Japanese migration, so the origin of the name is vague.  
Nanae Town belongs to Kameda county.  The name ``Kame-da'' means 
``turtle, paddy.''  It is a good-luck name.

( For the name ``Kameda,'' see Hakodate City in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

HISTORY   
 
Mishima Shrine was built in 1532, during the Muromachi era.  It was 
around the time that a Japanese village was established.  ( When a 
Japanese village was established, a Shintou shrine was established ( by 
Nihei lab 2023-2026_t ). ) 

During the Edo era, Nanae was a Japanese village.  It was governed by 
the Matsumae Domain.  ( Edo era continued from 1603 to 1868. ) 

After the Meiji period, in 1870 ( Meiji 3 ), Nanae Kan-en ( government 
farm ) was established by the Hokkaidou Development Commission.  An 
American advisor, Edwin Dun, instructed Western-style farming on the 
government farm.  It included dairy farming and butter production.  #1

In 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), the administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) 
of Nanae village was established by the merger of Iida and Nanae 
villages.  The administrative office of Oonakayama village was 
established by the merger of Nakajima and Ookawa.

( UTF code ; Iida = \u98EF\u7530.  Nakajima = \u4E2D\u5CF6.  Nanae 
  ( after 1879 ) = \u4E03\u98EF.  Nanae ( before 1879 ) = \u4E03\u91CD. 
  Ookawa = \u5927\u5DDD.  Oonakayama = \u5927\u4E2D\u5C71. ) 

In 1885 ( Meiji 18 ), Nanae village merged Oonakayama village.  In 1902 
( Meiji 35 ), Nanae Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was established. 
Nanae Station on the Hokkaidou Railway ( now Hakodate Main Line ) 
opened.  In 1907 ( Meiji 40 ), Nanae Village ( 1st-class municipality ) 
was established.  In 1957 ( Shouwa 32 ), Nanae Town was established.

( For 1st-class and 2nd-class municipalities, see the note of Matsumae 
  Town. )

TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC, POPULATION 

The town hall of Nanae is located in the southern part of the Hakodate 
Plain.  The part is called Oono Plain.  National Route 5 ( Oonuma-
kokudou ) and the Hakodate Main Line run through the plain.  The row 
of red pine trees ( Aka-matsu-namiki ) along National Route 5 is 
designated as one of the 100 Roads of Japan.  #2  #3  #4

The southern part of Nanae Town is the built-up area that continues 
from Hakodate City.  The part includes Oonakayama Station on the 
Hakodate Main Line.  The population increased.  Nanae is the second 
largest town ( ``machi'' ) in Hokkaidou.  ( The largest is Otofuke Town 
in Tokachi general sub-prefecture. )

INDUSTRY

Distinctive industries in Nanae are manufacturing, agriculture, dairy 
farming, and sightseeing.  The main manufacturing products are soft 
drinks ( including Guarana ), wine, and semiconductors.  STATISTICS OF 
AGRICULTURE = pasture 669 hectares in 2015, paddy rice 400, fodder 280, 

( UTF code ; Guarana = Guaran\u00E1. ) 

( continue ) --> carrots 210, green onions 114, radishes 89, orchards 
60, flowers 22 ( carnations and other flowers ), spinach 18, beans 
( soybeans 9, adzuki beans 4, other beans 4 ), tomatoes 11, cabbages 8, 

( continue ) --> cucumbers 8, broccoli 3, onions 3, apples X 
( concealed ), other vegetables 91+X ( sweet corn, pumpkins, 
asparagus ), and other grains 3 ; dairy cows 1,988 head in 2015 and 
hens 12,800.  #5  

As for sightseeing, Oonuma has been designated as the New Three Scenic 
Views of Japan.  Niyama Ski Ground is located next to Niyama Station.

NOTE
 
#1

As for Nanae Governmental Farm, the Gaertner brothers rented land and 
started Western-style farming in 1869.  They came from Prussia ( now 
Germany ).  The farm area was 990 hectares.  ( Cf. Hokkaidou University 
Ranch has 470 hectares in Shin-hidaka Town, Hidaka sub-prefecture. )

( UTF code ; Gaertner = G\u00E4rtner. )

The Gaertner brothers primarily engaged in trade and consulship.  They 
also cultivated apples, pears, gooseberries, cherries, and vegetables.  
The apples were the first Western varieties cultivated in Japan.  The 
Gaertoner brothers taught Western-style farming to neighboring farmers.

They also sold food to foreign ships in the Port of Hakodate.  There 
are no photographs or paintings of them.  They planted beech trees.  
The beech trees became a forest along National Route 5.  

( Beech trees were used for grazing pigs in their home country ( now 
  Germany ).  Beech trees have acorns.  The acorns were used for 
  forest grazing for pigs.  Also, the meat of a wild boar that ate 
  acorns is good. )

( In Japanese ; ``Forest grazing'' is ``rinkan-houboku.''  ``Wild 
  boar'' is ``inoshishi.'' ) 

( UTF code ; ``rinkan-houboku'' = \u6797\u9593\u653E\u7267. )

In 1870 ( Meiji 3 ), the Gaertner brothers' farm was closed.  The Meiji 
government bought the land back.  On the former site, Nanae Government 
Farm ( Nanae-kan-en ) opened.  

* * *

To introduce Western-style farming to Hokkaidou, the Meiji government 
established five government farms in Toukyou and Hokkaidou.  The 
locations were Aoyama and Azabu in Toukyou, and Nanae, Sapporo, and 
Nemuro in Hokkaidou.  Nanae Government Farm was the largest.

An English writer Isabella Lucy Bird visited Nanae Government Farm in 
1878.  She reported the scenery like this ; ``The farm looked like a 
Western country.  It had Western-style buildings such as animal 
stables, a large watermill, and a butter factory.''

In 1882, the Hokkaidou Development Commission ( ``Kaitaku-shi'' ) was 
abolished.  Nanae Government Farm was gradually sold off and closed in 
1894.  Only its stone wall remains near Nanae Elementary School.

( For Isabella Lucy Bird, see Mori Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )

* * *
 
As for the government farms ( ``kan-en'' ) in the Meiji period, Nanae 
farm was established in 1870 ( Meiji 3 ) ; Aoyama, Azabu, and Sapporo 
farms in 1871 ( Meiji 4 ) ; Nemuro farm in 1874 ( Meiji 7 ).  Aoyama 
and Azabu farms were located in Toukyo, others were located in 
Hokkaidou.  They were managed by the Hokkaidou Development Commission. 

( UTF code ; ``kan-en'' = \u5B98\u5712. )

Following the abolition of the Hokkaidou Development Commission in 1882 
( Meiji 15 ), the land of the gorvernment farms was gradually sold off.

Aoyama Government Farm ( Aoyama-kan-en ) was located at the border 
between Shibuya and Minato words in Toukyou. Part of the farm became 
Aoyama-gakuin Universtiy.  Azabu Government Farm was also located at 
the border between Shibuya and Minato words.  Part of the farm became 
Japan Red Cross Medical Center.

* * *

From 1872 ( Meiji 5 ) to 1876 ( Meiji 9 ), the government imported 
420,000 fruit saplings from the USA and Canada.  ( by Nanae Historical 
Museum. ``On The History Of Western Apple ( Seiyou-ringo-no-rekishi-ni-
tsuite )'' http://www2.town.nanae.hokkaido.jp/rekisikan/menu_4/apple/
last accessed July 2026. )

The imported fruit saplings were apple, pear, peach, grape, cherry, and 
plum.  Considering the large amount of imports, the first Japanese 
visitors to the USA must have found the fruit very tasty.  

The imported fruit saplings were at first planted at governemnt farms 
in Toukyou ( Aoyama and Azabu farms mentioned above ) and agricultural 
experiment ceners in Tokyou.  

The agricultural experiment centers in Tokyou were Naitou-shinjuku 
Agricultural Experiment Station ( Naitou-shinjuku-shiken-jou ) and Mita 
Breeding Station ( Mita-ikushu-jou ).  Naitou-shinuku Agricultural 
Experiment Station existed from 1872 ( Meiji 5 ) to 1906 ( Meiji 39 ). 
Mita Breeding Station existed from 1874 ( Meiji 7 ) to 1886 ( Meiji 
19 ). 
 
The former site of Naitou-shinuku Agricultural Experiment Station is 
Shinjuku-gyoen Public Garden in Shinjuku and Shibuya wards in Toukyou.
The former site of Mita Breeding Station is a business area in Mita 
district, Minato Ward, Toukyou.

* * * 

Louis Boehmer, an American advisor to the Japanese government, 
instructed how to cultivate the imported fruit saplings on the 
government farms in Toukyou and Sappro, and also on agricultural 
experiment centers in Toukyou.  ( Boehmer is a German surname. )

( UTF code ; Boehmer = B\u00F6hmer. )

Then, the government distributed the fruit saplings mainly to Hokkaidou 
and western Japan.  Thus, many Japanese fruits originate from Western 
varieties imported in the Meiji period.  The exceptions are Satsuma 
mandarin ( ``Citrus unshiu'' ), Japanese pear ( ``wa-nashi'' ), and 
persimmon ( ``kaki'' ). 

( For fruit, see Yoichi Town in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture. )

After the abolition of the Hokkaidou Development Commission in 1882, 
Louis Boehmer moved to Yokohama and launched L. Boehmer Co.  He 
started the export business of lily bulbs.  The lily bulbs were for 
observing flowers, not for eat.  

( For lily for eat, see Makkari Village in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

Easter lilies have been used at funerals in Western countries.  Easter 
lily bulbs were exported from Japan to Western countries.  The below is 
a postcard from L. Boehmer Co. in Yokohama to a business partner in the 
UK  ( by ``On The Bluff'' http://
onthebluff.hatenablog.com/entry/915a12150501a71d7287671de27d0a22  last 
accessed in August 2026 ).

                                          Yokohma, March 18th 1907
      Dear Sirs : -
      We take much pleasure in sending you by same mail our new 
   Catalogue, & beg to offer you 10% off our prices on Lilium 
   longiflorum therein quoted.
      We do not think that after the low prices which last year's 
   Auction Sales brought those people, who considered Bulbs to the 
   London Auction rooms, will again make consignments. -
      Intending purchasers will have to order directory therefore.
      We hope to be favored with your orders.
                                          Yours faithfully,
                                          L. Boehmer Co.
 
The Japanese name of Easter lily is ``teppou-yuri.''  The academic name 
of Easter lily is ``Lilium longiflorum.''  L. Boehmer Co. continued 
from 1882 to 1908. 

I live in the Tama Hills now.  Many Easter lilies bloom in the hills in 
August.  Their fragrance drifts on the air in the evening.  

Seeing Easter lilies, I remember my friends who died younger than me ; 
Professor M. Sasaoka of Hokkaidou University, who died in 2025, and 
Professor T. Ooishi of Okayama Shouka University, who died in 2026.  
I've lived long enough to see such situations.

( For lily, see Makkari Village in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture ; 
  Nihei lab 2023-2026_t. )
 
#2

Mt. Nanae ( 779 meters ) rises near the town hall of Nanae.  From the 
mountain, Hakodate's night view can be seen.  The scene is another side 
of Hakodate's night view ( Hakodate-ura-yakei ).

#3

Oonuma Station on the Hakodate Main Line is a junction between Oonuma 
main route and Sawara branch route.  Another branch route exists to the 
south, between Oonuma Station and Nanae Station.  It's Fujishiro branch 
route, which was built by Japanese National Railways in 1966.  It was 
planned before motorization, to increase transport capacity.  

( For motorization, see the note of Otobe Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For Sawara branch route, see Mori Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )

#4

Red pine trees ( Aka-matsu ) along National Route 5 are large.  They 
ere reminiscent of old roads ( ``kaidou'' ) in Honshuu Island.  In 1876 
( Meiji 9 ), the red pine trees were transplanted from Go-ryou-kaku 
Castle to the roadside. 

The pine trees were transplanted to make a scenic road, because Emperor 
Meiji would visit Nanae Government Farm.  Cf. Cherry trees along the 
Nijikken Road in Hidaka sub-prefecture were also transplanted to 
welcome Emperor Meiji.  

( For red pine tree, see Uryuu Town in Sorachi general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For the Nijikken Road, see Shin-hidaka Town in Hidaka sub-
  prefecture. )
  
#5  

As for Guarana, some students are drinking Guarana during lectures at 
Hokkaidou University.  Guarana is a popular drink in Brazil.  It's like 
Brazilian Coca-cola. 

Guarana is made from the berries of Guarana trees.  The berries are 
small and red.  Guarana trees are native to the Amazon region, which 
includes the world's largest tropical rainforest.

In Japan, Coca-cola became popular in Honshuu Island after World War 
II.  Before the American product landed in Hokkaidou Island, a company 
in Nanane started to produce and sell Guarana.  It's still popular in 
Hokkaidou.

During the lectures, when I saw a student drinking a can of Guarana, I 
would tell this story ; ``Japanese people pronounce Guarana as `ga-ra-
na.'  It's like English.  In the original place of Guarana, Brazil, 
people pronounce it `gu-a-ra-na.'  It's like Latin.

When I took domestic flight in Brazil, I would order Guarana as my 
drink.  One day, I was thinking which I would order between Guarana and 
Tonica.  ( Tonica is a Tonic Water.  See Nihei lab 2023-2026_s for 
Tonica. )

Then, I said, ``Ah, Ga-ra-na.  Por farvor.'  Then, the stewardess gave 
me a bottle of water.  It must have sounded `Agua, ne?' ( Water, 
OK? ).''
 
#6

These are the New Three Scenic Views of Japan ( Shin-nihon-sankei ) ; 
Mihono-matsubara in Shizuoka Prefecture ( Honshuu Island ), Yabakei in 
Ooita Prefecture ( Kyuushuu Island ), and Oonuma in Hokkaidou 
Prefecture ( Hokkaidou Island ).  They were designated in 1915 
( Taishou 4 ).

Today, they are nothing new.  Japanese people like putting ``shin 
( new )'' to things.  For exammple, Shin-kansen ( bullet train ), 
Shin-den ( newly reclaimed paddy ), and Shin-kokuritsu-gekijou ( New 
National Theatre ).  They also like ``top-three things.''  ( For the 
World Top Three Night Views, see Hakodate City. )

The original Three Scenic Views are located in Honshuu Island.  They 
are Matsushima in Miyagui Prefecture ( Pacific Ocean ), Amano-hashidate 
in Kyouto Prefecture ( the Sea of Japan ), and Itsukushima in Hiroshima 
Prefecture ( the Seto Inland Sea ).

The original Three Scenic Views were selected in 1643, during the Edo 
era.  The Tokugawa Shogunate ruled the country.  It was a feudal 
period.  Ordinary people couldn't travel freely.

#7

Oonuma Quasi National Park includes three lakes ; Lake Oonuma, Lake 
Konuma, and Lake Junsai.  The shapes of these lakes were formed by the 
eruption of Mt. Komagatake in 1640.

In Lake Oonuma, smelt and shrimp are caught.  In Lake Junsai, 
``junsai'' ( buds of watershield ) is harvested.  ``Junsai'' is tasty 
in ``dashi'' soup.  ( ``Dashi'' soup is a transparent soup made from 
``konbu'' kelp and ``katsuo-bushi'' ( shaved dried bonito ).  My wife 
doesn't eat ``junsai.  She thinks it's like nasal mucus. ) 

At the end of the Edo era, Lake Oonuma was opened to foreigners for 
walking.  In the Meiji period, an ice factory was located on the shore 
of Lake Oonuma.

The factory cut ice cubes from Lake Oonuma in winter.  The ice cubes 
were stored until summer and shipped from Hakodate to Toukyou.  ( by 
Professor Emeritus Katsuhiko Yamashita at the Meeting of Hokkaidou 
Geographical Society in 2014. ) 

Ice was expensive in summer in the past.  The story of the ice factory 
reminds me of the etymology of Nanae, which means ``cold stream.''  

After the 1960s, villas were developed on the slope from Lake  
Oonuma to Mt. Komagatake.  Aerial photographs show roundabouts and 
``cul-de-sac'' in the area.  A cul-de-sac is a dead end that has a 
circular shape. 

In Japanese, there are no difference between roundabout and rotary.  
Both are called rotary.  As for roundabout, vehicles can pass through 
it without stopping.  I've never seen a roundabout or a rotary in 
Honshuu Island.  In Canada, I drove through several roundabouts.

( For roundabout and rotary, see Otaru City in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture ; Kushiro City in Kushiro general sub-prefecture. ) 

The famous song ``Be Thousand Winds ( Sen-no-kaze-ni-natte )'' was 
translated by a composer at a villa in Oonuma.  The original is an 
American poem, ``Do not stand at my grave and weep.''  

The poem continues, ``I am not there, I am the thousand winds that 
blow.''  I agree.  When I die and become a ghost, I will not be in the 
dreary graveyard.  I will go to Daimaru department store and watch 
people.

#8

This is a legend about Lake Oonuma and Mt. Komagatake ; ``Masatane 
Aihara was the load of Fort Oodate in the 15th century.  The fort was 
located in what is now Matsumae Town.  Aihara came to Ezo Island ( now 
Hokkaidou ) with Masasue Andou, Sueshigue Kakizaki, and Nobuhiro 
Takeda.
 
( Masasue Andou, the leader of them, built 12 forts in Ezo Island and 
  returned to the main island.  Nobuhiro Takeda, who ended Koshamain's 
  war, inherited the Kakizaki clan.  ( Koshamain was an Ainu chief. )
  Nobuhiro Takeda changed his family name to Kakizaki and ruled Ezo 
  Island. )  

Masatane Aihara came into conflict with Nobuhiro Kakizaki.  Aihara was 
defeated and fled to Lake Oonuma.  He drowned himself into the lake.  
Later, the Kakizaki clan became the lord of the Matsumae 
Domain during the Edo era ( from the 17th to the 19th centuries ).

Even in the Edo era, Masatane Aihara's horse was seen near Lake 
Oonuma.  The horse had a very long mane that reached on the ground.''

This legend says that the name of Mt. Komagatake signifies the 
Aihara's ghost horse.  The name ``Koma-ga-take'' means ``horse, of, 
mountain'' in Japanese.  ( It is generally said that the shape of the 
mountain looks like a horse. )

This story about Aihara's horse is a legend.  Masatane Aihara may have 
been killed during Koshamain's War.  This story resembles the legend of 
Yoshitsune Minamoto. 

Yoshitsune Minamoto ( 1159-1189 ) was a military commander at the 
end of the Heian era.  He was exiled from the capital ( Kyou ) and 
killed by his elder brother, Yoritomo Minamoto, who established the 
Kamakura Shogunate.  

Japanese people feel sympathy for tragic heroes.  The sympathy is 
called Hangan-biiki ( Hougan-biiki ).  The word ``hangan'' means the 
job position of Yoshitsune Minamoto.  The word ``hiiki ( biiki )'' 
means sympathy. 

( For Hangan-date Rock, see Niikappu Town in Hidaka sub-prefecture. )

( For the former 12 forts built by the Andou clan, see Kaminokuni Town 
  in Hiyama sub-prefecture. )

( For Yoshitsune legend, see Biratori Town in Hidaka sub-prefecture. ) 

( Single of the chapter ;``Repente'' from Brasil.  --> 
  http://youtu.be/umBZCXnzwEA )

|     SHIKABE     |
|                 |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 8  |
 -----------------
AREA 111 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 90, CULTIVATED 1 ; COUNTY = KAYABE ; POP 
4,979 ( 1990 ), 4,767 ( 2010 ) ; COUNTRY SIGN = GEYSER, PUMICE, & 
MOUNTAIN. 

LOCATION 
 
Shikabe ( shi-ka-be ) is a town in Oshima general sub-prefecture, south 
Hokkaidou ( ``dou-nan'' ).  The Orito River and the Tokoro River flow 
into the Pacific Ocean.  The Orito River flows from Lake Oonuma in 
Oonuma Quasi National Park.

( UTF code ; Shikabe = \u9E7F\u90E8. )

Mt. Komagatake ( 1,131 meters, volcano ) stands to the north.  It 
erupted in 1929.  A large amount of pumice piled up.

( For Mt. Komagatake and Lake Oonuma, see Nanae Town in Oshima general 
  sub-prefecture. )

NAME 

The name ``Shikabe'' comes from ``sikerpe'' in Ainu language.  It means 
``Amur cork tree.''  ( Amur cork is called ``Kihada'' in Japanese. ) 
It signified the mouth of the Shikabe River.  #1

Or, it comes from ``sike-un-pe'' in Ainu language, which means ``carry, 
it, place.''  This means a place where Ainu people got off their boats 
and carried their luggage.  It signified the mouth of the Shikabe 
River.  
 
HISTORY

In 1666, during the Edo era, a hot spring was discovered by Guen-gorou 
Itou, who came from Tsugaru ( now Aomori Prefecture ).  He found a deer 
( ``shika'' ) that healed its wounds in the hot spring.  It's the 
origin of Shikabe Hot Spring.  In the Edo era, fishermen migrated from 
Nanbu ( now Aomori Prefecture ).
 
After the Meiji period, in 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), the administrative office 
( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) of Shikabe village was established.  In 1906 
( Meiji 39 ), Shikabe Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established.  In 1983 ( Shouwa 58 ), Shikabe Town was established.

( For 2nd-class municipality, see the note of Matsumae Town. )

TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC

The town hall of Shikabe is located near the mouth of the Shikabe 
River.  National Route 278 ( Esan-kokudou ) runs.  At the foot of Mt. 
Komagatake, Sawara branch route of the Hakodate Main Line runs.  It 
runs along the Pacific coast, avoiding the slope of Mt. Komagatake. 

INDUSTRY

Distinctive industries in Shikabe are fishery and tourism.  STATISTICS 
OF AGRICULTURE = pasture 52 hectares in 2015, fodder 16, and paddy 
rice - ( zero ) ; dairy cows - ( zero ) head in 2015. STATISTICS OF 
FISHERIES = walleye pollock 4,165 tons in 2017, sardines 701, 

( continue ) --> octopus 401, shellfish 327 ( including scallops ), 
mackerel 146, squid ( flying squid 134, other squid 8 ), right-eye 
flounder 125, yellowtail 95, ``konbu'' kelp 89, salmon 75, 

( continue ) --> Pacific cod 55, sea urchins 47, tuna 11, atka 
mackerel 7, ``hatahata'' 7, herring 4, ``kichiji'' 4, olive flounder 4, 
shrimp/prawns 4, sea trout 3, horse mackerel 2, other fish 108+X 
( concealed ), and other marine animals 62.

Fishing ports are located in Shikabe, Honbetsu, and Dekima districts.  
As for sightseeing, cottages, golf courses, and a resort hotel are 
located near Shikabe Station.  ( Shikabe Station is far from the center 
of the town. )

Shikabe Roadside Station ( Michino-eki-shikabe ) is located on National 
Route 278.  There is a geyser that gushes hot water up to 15 meters.  
It's a part of Shikabe Hot Spring.  #2

( In Japanese ; ``Hot spring water' is ``yu'' or ``onsen-sui.''  ``Hot 
  water'' is ``yu'' or ``oyu.'' )
 
NOTE 
 
#1

The name ``Shikabe'' means ``Amur cork tree'' in Ainu language, Amur 
cork tree was used as a yellow dye and also as an intestinal medicine.  
Amur cork tree is called ``ki-hada'' in Japanese.  It means ``yellow, 
skin.''

Large Amur cork trees grow on the central lawn of Hokkaidou University. 
In 2018, during Typhoon No. 21, their large branches were fell.  The 
yellow color of their barks stood out.

Ainu people made ritual sticks from branches of these trees ; 
``kihada,'' ``yanagui,'' ``mizuki,'' and ``enju.''  Ainu people 
believed that these trees have good spirits.  ( Cups made from 
``enju'' wood are sold in Ainu villages. )

( In Ainu language ; ``Ritual stick'' is ``inau.'' )

( In English ; ``Enju'' is pagoda tree.  ``Mizuki'' is dogwood.  
  ``Yanagui'' is willow. )

Ainu's ritual sticks can be found in Hokkaidou University.  The 
location is a forest in Archaeological Preservation Area ( Iseki-hozon-
teien ).  There were Ainu villages in the forest.  Ainu's ritual sticks 
look like dread hair.  ( Japanese people make ritual sticks from 
``sakaki'' trees.  ``Sakaki'' is a broad-leaved evergreen tree. )  

( For Ainu culture, see Biratori Town in Hidaka sub-prefecture. )

( For bad spirit tree, see Rankoshi Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For ``enju'' tree, see Abashiri City in Okhotsk general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For ``sakaki'' tree, see Nihei lab 2023-2026_t. )

#2

Shiakbe Station on the Hakodate Main Line was called Takamachi Station 
in the past.  There was another Shikabe Station on the Oonuma-dentetsu 
Line.  The Oonuma-dentetsu Line ran from 1929 and 1952 along the Orito 
River.  After the abolition of the railroad, Takamachi Station was 
renamed Shikabe Station.

The name ``Takamachi'' means ``hawk catcher'' in Japanese.  During the 
Edo era, hawk catchers caught hawks and falcons for falconry.

( For falconry, see Shiriuchi Town in Oshima general sub-prefecture. ) 

( Single of the chapter ; ``Rydeen'' by Yellow Magic Orchestra.  --> 
  http://youtu.be/vTyG2huYI7Y )

|                 | 
|      MORI       | 
|                 | 
|  MAP 2 - NO. 9  |
 -----------------
AREA 368 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 280, CULTIVATED 23 ; COUNTY = KAYABE ; 
POP 22,142 ( 1990 ) ( MORI 16,374, SAWARA 5,768 ), 17,859 ( 2010 ) ; 
MEAN ANNUAL TEMP 8.1 C ( AV 2002-2010 ), PRECIPI 1,097 MM ; COUNTRY 
SIGN = CHERRY & MOUNNTAIN.  #1
 
LOCATION 

Mori ( mo-ri ) is a town in Oshima general sub-prefecture, south 
Hokkaidou ( Dou-nan ).  The area faces Uchiura Bay ( Volcano Bay ).  
The Nigorikawa River, the Torizaki River, and the Oshironai River flow. 

( UTF code ; Mori = \u68EE. )
 
Mt. Komagatake ( 1,131 meters, volcano ) rises to the south.  The area 
of Mori was newly established by the merger of Mori Town and Sawara 
Town in 2005.  #2

NAME 

The name ``Mori'' means ``forest'' in Japanese.  The original name was 
Oniushi.  The name ``Oniushi'' comes from ``o-ni-us-pet'' in Ainu 
language.  It means ``river mouth, tree, many, river.''  It signified 
the Torizaki River or the Mori River.

The pronunciation of Oni-ushi sounds like ``demon, ox'' in Japanese.  
Maybe Japanese migrants didn't like the name.

HISTORY 

In the 16th century, the first Japanese migrants came from Tsugaru 
( now Aomori Prefecture ).  In the Edo era, Kayabe-basho ( trading 
place ) was established.  It was for herring fishing and was managed by 
the Matsumae Domain.  Nigorikawa Hot Spring opened in 1805 in 
Nigorikawa Caldera.  #3   

In the Edo era, villages of Washinoki, Mori, and Oshironai were 
established.  The following is the history after the Meiji period ;

OLD MORI TOWN ; In 1880 ( Meiji 13 ), an administrative office 
( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) was established in Washinoki village.  It covered 
villages of Ebiya and Ishikura.

( UTF code ; Ebiya = \u86EF\u8C37.  Ishikura = \u77F3\u5009. ) 

In 1881 ( Meiji 14 ), the administrative office of Mori village was 
established.  It covered villages of Oshironai and Shukunobe.  In 1899 
( Meiji 22 ), Mori village merged Washinoki village.  The 
administrative office covered six villages ; Ebiya, Ishikura. Mori, 
Oshironai, Shukunobe, and Washinoki.  #4

( UTF code ; Oshironai = \u5C3E\u767D\u5185.  Shukunobe = 
  \u5BBF\u91CE\u8FBA.  Washinoki = \u9DF2\u30CE\u6728. )

In 1902 ( Meiji 35 ), Mori Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established.  In 1903 ( Meiji 36 ), Mori Station on Hokkaidou Railway 
( now Hakodate Main Line ) opened.  In 1907 ( Meiji 40 ), Mori Village 
( 1st-class municipality ) was established.  In 1921 ( Taishou 10 ), 
Mori Town was established.

( For 1st-class and 2nd-class municipalities, see the note of Matsumae 
  Town. )

OLD SAWARA TOWN ; In 1880 ( Meiji 13 ), the administrative office 
( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) of Sawara village was established.  It covered 
Kakeruma village.  In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), Sawara Village ( 2nd-class 
municipality ) was established.

( UTF code ; Kakeruma = \u639B\u6F97.  Sawara = \u7802\u539F. )

In 1927 ( Shouwa 2 ), Sawara Station on Oshima-kaigan-tetsudou ( Oshima 
Coastal Railway ) opened.  It was the current Sawara branch route of 
the Hakodate Main Line.  In 1970 ( Shouwa 45 ), Sawara Town was 
established.

TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC 

The town hall of Mori is located between the Torizaki River and the 
Mori River.  National Route 278 ( Esan-kokudou ) branches off from 
National Route 5 ( Oonuma-kokudou ) near the town hall.  National Route 
278 runs along Uchiura Bay.  #5

Mori Station on the Hakodate Main Line is the junction of Oonuma main 
route and Sawara branch route.  Heavy freight ( fre'it ) trains go the 
branch route along the bay.  #6

INDUSTRY 

Distinctive industries in Mori are fishery, agriculture, animal 
husbandry, food processing, and geothermal power generation.  
STATISTICS OF AGRICUTURE = pasture 471 hectares in 2015, beans 
( soybeans 184, adzuki beans 101, other beans 15 ), potatoes 225, paddy 
rice 207, sugar beets 119, wheat 69, radishes 50, fodder 42, 

( continue ) --> broccoli 27, flowers 6, orchards 5, watermelons 1, 
other vegetables 514+X ( pumpkins, sweet corn ), and tomatoes X 
( concealed ) ; pigs and sows 73,493 head in 2015 ( the largest in 
Hokkaidou ), dairy cows 238, and hens 1,200.  STATISTICS OF FISHERIES = 
sardines 7,668 tons in 2017, walleye pollock 3,251, 

( continue ) --> right-eye flounder 1,328, squid ( flying squid 726, 
other squid 14 ), mackerel 578, shellfish ( scallops ( cultured ) 161, 
other shellfish 198 ), octopus 254, salmon 242, shrimp/prawns 169 
( including Botan shrimp ), ``konbu'' kelp 164, yellowtail 126, 

( continue ) --> sand lance 71, crabs ( snow crabs 44, other

atka mackerel 3, ``hatahata'' 2, ``kichiji'' 2, marine mammals 2, sea 
trout 2, congers 1, other fish 481, and other marine animals 66.  
#7  #8

At Nigorikawa district, farmers cultivate Onsen Tomato ( hot-spring 
tomatoes ).  The tomatoes are grown in the greenhouses that are heated 
by hot spring water.  ( by Katsuki Umeda 2007.  Development and 
sustainability of vegetable farming in geothermal greenhouses. 
``Geographical Review Of Japan'' doi.org/10.4157/grj.80.225 )

As for sightseeing, Mori Station is the birth place of Ika-meshi 
( squid rice ).  Squid rice became famous all over the country for 
department stores' station-lunch festivals.  #9

( For tomato, see Biratori Town in Hidaka sub-prefecture. )

NOTE

#1

Mori Town belongs to Kayabe county ( Kayabe-gun ).  The name ``Kayabe'' 
comes from ``kaya-un-pe'' in Ainu language.  It means ``sail, exist, 
place.''  It signified Mt. Komagatake.  Ainu people viewed the shape of 
the mountain as a sailing ship.

( UTF code ; Kayabe = \u8305\u90E8. )

``Komagatake Hazard Map'' was published in 1983.  It was the first 
volcanic hazard map in the country.  The map included the towns of 
Mori, Shikabe, and Nanae.  Today, each municipality publshes own hazard 
map.

Who makes hazard maps?  The answer is consulting companies.  They are 
the main employers of graduates from geological and geographical 
faculties ( by Kazutaka Mannen 2020.  ``Volcanoes As Seen From Cutting-
edge Science'' publihsed by Bestbook ( written in Japanese ) ).  Many 
of my classmates work for consulting companies. 

( For Mt. Komagatake, see Nanae Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )

#2

The pronunciation of ``Mori Town'' in Japanese is ``Mori-machi.''  All 
other towns in Hokkaidou are pronounced ``-chou,'' for example, Kayabe-
chou, Yakumo-chou, and Oshamanbe-chou.

The municipality decides the pronunciation.  It is said that ``-machi'' 
is located mainly in eastern Japan and ``-chou'' is located mainly in 
western Japan.  According to the Chuu-nichi Shinbun ( 2021.7.19 ), the 
town hall of Mori surveyed old telegrams and old gazetteers, and 
decided the name ``Mori-machi'' in 1987.

( Cf. The former Ishikari 10 towns in Ishikari City are mixture of 
  ``-machi'' and ``-chou.'' ) 

( For the mixuture of ``-machi'' and ``-chou,'' see Furubira Town, 
  Otaru City, and Yoichi Town in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture ; 
  Ishikari City in Ishikari sub-prefecture. )

The river name ``Torizaki'' comes from ``torii ( shrine gate )'' in 
Japanese.  There was a Shintou shrine in the forest.  It had many Torii 
gates.  The place was called Torii-ga-saki, which means ``shrine gate, 
of, cape.'' ( by Kenji Kimura 1798.  ``Ezo-nikki ( Ezo Diary ).'' )

On the map published by Takeshirou Matsuura in 1859, during the Edo era 
( http://koukita.github.io/touzaiezo/map/ ), the river is written as 
Torisaki-kawa.  It's an old name.

( For Japanese river name, see Setana Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. )
   
#3

The first migrants came to Sawara ( now Mori Town ) from Kanita in 
1532.  Kanita was located in what is now Sotogahama Town in Aomori 
Prefecture.  ( by ``Sawara Town History ( Sawara-chou-shi )'' published 
in 2000. )

The former Sawara Town has a long history of Japanese migrants, so the 
origin of the name is unclear.  These are estimations from the book 
``Sawara Town History'' ;
( 1 ) The name `` Sawara'' comes from ``sar'' in Ainu language.  It 
means ``reed.''  Reed ( plant ) is called ``ashi'' in Japanese.

( 2 ) The name comes from ``sarki'' in Ainu language.  It means ``reed, 
stem.''  This may signify that the reed is long or stiff.

( 3 ) The name comes from ``sawara ( suna-hara )'' in Japanese.  It 
means ``sandy, field.'' 

#4

In the beginning of the Meiji period, the former Tokugawa Shogunate 
forces landed on the coast of Washinoki.  It was the beggining of the 
Hakodate War.  The Hakodate War was the final phase of the Boshin War 
( civil war ).
 
The landing took place on December 4th, 1868.  A strong wind was 
blowing on the day.  The local strong winter wind is called Taba-
kaze. 

The commanding officers of the Tokugawa Shogunate were Takeaki Enomoto 
and Toshizou Hijikata.  They were aboard the battleships Kaiyou and 
Kaiten.  Kaiyou was made in the Netherlands.  Kaiten was made in 
Prussia ( now Germany ).  The battleship Kaiyou was sunk in Esashi.  
The battleship Kaiten was sunk in Hakodate.  

( For battleship Kaiyou, see Esashi Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. ) 

( For Taba-kaze ( local wind ), see Easahi Town in Hiyama sub-
  prefecture. )

( For the Boshin War, see Hakodate City in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )

#5

National Route 5 runs along a railway.  The highway connects Hakodate 
and Sapporo.  Its predecessor opened in 1873 ( Meiji 6 ).  The old 
route was called the Sapporo Main Road ( Sapporo-hondou ).  It crossed 
Uchiura Bay ( Volcano Bay ) by ferry.

There was a ferry line between Mori to Muroran. It operated from 1872 
to 1893 and again from 1908 to 1928.  It was called Shin-ran sea route. 
( by http://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E6%A3%AE%E6%B8%AF )

( UTF code ; Shin-ran = \u68EE\u862D. )
 
In 1878 ( Meiji 11 ), a British writer, Isabella Lucy Bird, stayed at 
an inn in Mori.  She was traveling from Hakodate to Biratori to see 
Ainu culture.  She documented Mori like this, ``Mori is a large village 
in the south of Volcano Bay, but the houses are about to collapse at 
any moment.''  But, she liked the view from the inn. 

( For Isabella Lucy Bird, see Nanae Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture ; Toyoura Town in Iburi general sub-prefecture ; Biratori 
  Town in Hidaka sub-prefecture. ) 

#6

The Sawara branch route of the Hakodate Main Line connects Mori Station 
and Oonuma Station.  The branch route passes through the former Shikabe 
Town.  It was constructed during the Second World War to increase 
transportation capacity.

The branch route was constructed using two existing railroads ; Oshima-
kaigan-tetsudou ( Oshima Coastal Railway ) and Oonuma-dentetsu ( Oonuma 
Electric Railway ).  In June 1945, the branch route opened.  In August 
1945, Japan surrendered to the war.

#7

As for animal husbandry, there are swine farms in Mori Town.  They are 
managed by NH Foods ( Nippon Ham Foods ).  The company's processing 
plant is located in neighboring Yakumo Town.  

( For swine industry, see Toubetsu Town in Ishikari sub-prefecture. )

As for food processing, Nichirei Foods produces croquettes and minced 
cutlets.  Maruha Nichiro produces salmon flakes and minced chicken. 

Nichirei Foods has a stone monument in the garden.  It commemorates the 
birth place of frozen food in Japan.  Their frozen food business 
started in 1920 ( Taishou 9 ). 

At the time, Kuzuhara-shoukai Co. ( now Nichirei Foods ) produced 
frozen fish using a charcoal gas engine.  The frozen fish was sold in 
Toukyou.  The company's freezing plant was constructed by American 
father-and-son engineers.  The company also owned reefer ships.

Regarding frozen food, the first home refrigerator was sold by GE 
( General Electric ) in the USA in the 1920s.  After the mid 1960s, 
Japanese companies produced a lot of refrigerators and exported them.

My parents used a Hitachi refrigerator.  It lasted 50 years.  The next 
one was broken in two years.  Today, companies pursue profit. The 
refrigerators that never break are bad for them.  ( Today, universities 
are like companies.  Professors who don't earn money are bod for 
them. ) 

I know old Japanese products.  I don't always think the newer the 
better.  ( I know old university.  I don't always think the newer the 
better. ) 

#8

Hokkaidou Electric Power ( Hokuden ) runs a geothermal power plant in 
Mori Town.  It is located on the slope of Nigorikawa caldera.  
Geothermal power generation is suitable to the small volcanic country.  
There is no fuel cost.

But, geothermal power generation has these disadvantages ; ( 1 ) The 
output is small compared to the size of the building.  ( 2 ) 
Construction cost is high.  ( 3 ) Many volcanoes are located in 
national parks, so nature conservation laws restrict the construction. 

#9

Abe-shouten ( Abe Store ) started to sell Ika-meshi ( squid rice ) at 
Mori Station in 1941.  It was during the Second World War.  Trains 
stopped at Mori Station for a long tie because it was the junction of 
the Hakodate Main Line and the Oshima Coastal Railway ( Oshima-kaigan-
tetsudou ).

Squid rice became popular among young soldiers who stationed in 
Hokkaidou.  At the time, the catch of flying squid was good.  Also, 
squid rice can be cooked with a small amount of rice.  During the war, 
rice distribution was controlled. 
 
Today, many companies sell squid rice.  The original Abe-shouten's 
squid rice was not sold at souvenir shops, because the expiry date was 
one day.  So, it was sold through demonstration sales at department 
stores. 
 
The original Abe-shouten's squid rice became vacuum packed in 2016.  
Souvenir shops started selling the original squid rice at Sapporo 
Station and New Chitose Airport.  Incidentally, in Mori Town, squid-
shaped potteries of 4000-year-old were excavated in 2002.
 
As for department stores, there used to be department stores in front 
of large stations.  Many of them closed.  Large shopping malls with 
large parking areas opened in the former paddy fields.  Your 
grandmothers like to shop at department stores.  ( Department stores 
also manage out-of-store sales ( ``gaishou'' ) ). 

( Single of the chapter ; ``The Wizard'' by T-Rex.  He met it in the 
  wood.  --> http://youtu.be/tYXGks0jQP0 )
