|                  |
|      OTOBE       |
|                  |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 15  |
 ------------------
AREA 163 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 141, CULTIVATED 5 ; COUNTY = NISHI ; 
POP 6,011 ( 1990 ), 4,408 ( 2010 ) ; COUNTRY SIGN = SWIMMING POOL.  #1
  
LOCATION 

Otobe ( o-to-be ) is a town in Hiyama sub-prefecture, south Hokkaidou 
( ``dou-nan'' ).  Otobe is located in the west of the Oshima Peninsula. 
The area faces the Sea of Japan.  Forests occupy 87 percent of the 
area.

( UTF code ; Otobe = \u4E59\u90E8. )

NAME

The name ``Otobe'' comes from ``o-to-un-pe'' in Ainu language.  It 
means ``river mouth, swamp, exist, thing ( river ).''  It signified 
the Hime River, which flows through the center of the town.  In the Edo 
era, the river was called the Otohe River.  ( by Takeshirou Matsuura's 
map published in 1859, http://koukita.github.io/touzaiezo/map/ )  #2  

HISTORY 

In the Edo era, the area was governed by the Matsumae Domain.  Herring 
fishing thrived.  According to oral tradition, Hanaiso-suwa Shrine 
( Shintou shrine ) was established in 1617 ( Guen-na 3 ).  The shrine's 
name is recorded in a document written in 1704.  #3

After the Meiji period, in 1869 ( Meiji 2 ), the Meiji government 
forces landed in Otobe.  It was during the Boshin War.  ( Boshin War 
was a civil war. )  They advanced to Esashi and then Hakodate, 
defeating the former Tokugawa Shogunate, and bringing the war to an 
end.  #4

In 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), the administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) 
of Otobe village was established.  It also covered villages of Komonai 
and Toppu.  The administrative office of Mitsuya village was 
established.  It also covered Kabashira village.

( UTF code ; Kabashira = \u868A\u67F1.  Komonai = \u5C0F\u8302\u5185. 
  Mitsuya = \u4E09\u30C4\u8C37.  Toppu = \u7A81\u7B26. )
 
In 1902 ( Meiji 35 ), Otobe Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established by the merger of five villages.  In 1965 ( Shouwa 40 ), 
Otobe Town was established.

( For 2nd-class municipality, see the note of Matsumae Town. )

TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC

The town hall of Otobe is located near the mouth of the Hime River.  
National Route 229 ( Hiyama-kokudou ) runs along the sea.  The national 
highway connects old settlements that flourished in fishery or 
forestry.  #5 

INDUSTRY 

Distinctive industries in Otobe are fishery, agriculture, fish 
processing, and forestry.  STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = paddy rice 127 
hectares in 2015, soybeans 89, broccoli 26, adzuki beans 17, fodder 9, 
pasture 4, orchards 2, strawberries 2, radishes 1, 

( continue ) --> other vegetables 32+X ( including lily bulbs ), and 
other grains 20 ; dairy cows - ( zero ) in 2015.  STATISTICS OF 
FISHERIES = walleye pollock 286 tons in 2017, squid ( flying squid 214, 
other squid 1 ), sea urchins 39, octopus 32, salmon 13, Pacific cod 5,

( continue ) --> ``konbu'' kelp 3, olive flounder 3, shrimp/prawns 2, 
abalone 1, atka mackerel 1, right-eye flounder 1, yellowtail 1, herring 
0, other fish 17, and other marine animals 16 ( including sea 
cucumbers ).

Fishing ports are located in Otobe, Guen-na, and Toyohama.  As for 
other industries, a factory of CAC Cosmetics and Tomioka Winery are 
located.  The founder of CAC Cosmetics was born in a pharmacy in Otobe. 

Tomioka Winery was founded in 1976.  It was acquired in 2008 by the 
distilling company Sapporo-shusei.  ( The head office of Sapporo-shusei 
is located in Nishi Ward, Sapporo City, Ishikari sub-prefecture. )

As for sightseeing, Guen-na-dai Beach Park has been selected as one of 
the 100 Beaches of Japan.  It's the only 100 Beaches in Hokkaidou 
Island.  ( The 100 Beaches of Japan is designated by the Ministry of 
the Environment. )  

The country sign ( gateway sign ) of Otobe depicts the artificial 
seawater pool at Guen-na-dai Beach Park.  The pool looks like herring 
pens that used to be located along the coast of Hokkaidou Island. 

( For herring pen, see Kamoenai Village in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

These are other scenic locations.  ( 1 ) Cape Shibi-no-saki, which is 
andesite columnar jointing.  The shape of the cape resembles tuna 
scales.  ( The word ``shibi'' means tuna in old Japanese.  The word 
``andesite'' is ``anzan-gan'' in Japanese.  The word ``columnar 
jointing'' is ``chuujou-setsuri'' in Japanese. )

( continue ) --> ( 2 ) Cape Tateno-saki, whose nickname is the Grand 
Canyon of the Orient.  ( 3 ) Takise coast, which is known for Shira-
hura.  Shira-fura is a white coastal cliff.  #6

NOTE
 
#1

Otobe Town belongs to Nishi county.  The Kanji characters for Nishi are 
difficult to pronounce even for Japanese people.  The name was decided 
in the beginning of the Meiji period.

( UTF code ; Nishi ( county ) = \u723E\u5FD7. )

Takeshirou Matsuura, an explorer of Ezo ( now Hokkaidou ), suggested 
the name ``Nishi ( West )'' for the county's name, because the area 
was located in the west of the Oshima Peninsula.  His suggestion was an 
easy Kanji character for Nishi ( west ).  

( UTF code ; Nishi ( west ) = \u897F. )

The Meiji government changed it to the difficult characters, because 
there were identical names in Honshuu Island.  ( by Hidezou Yamada & 
Toshikazu Sasaki eds. 1988.  ``Ainu Language Place Name Material 
Collection ( Ainu-go-chimei-shiryou-shuusei )'' published by 
Soufuu-kan, ISBN 4883230368 )
 
#2

The name ``Otohe'' means ``river mouth, swamp, exist, thing'' in Ainu 
language.  As the name suggests, there were many swamps and ponds in 
the lower reaches of the Otohe River ( now Hime River ).  They were 
reclaimed and turned into paddy fields and a built-up area.

The current river name ``Hime'' means ``princess'' or ``noble lady'' in 
Japanese.  It refers to Shizuka-gozen in the case of Otobe Town.  
Shizuka-gozen was a ``mekake ( concubine )'' of Yoshitsune Minamoto.

Yoshitsune Minamoto was a commander of the Minamoto clan in the 12th 
century.  He was killed in battle in Oushuu ( now Touhoku region ), but 
many legends mention that he fled to Ezo ( now Hokkaidou ).  This is 
one ofe Yoshitsune legends in Otobe ;

``Shizuka-gozen came Otohe in search of Yoshitsune.  She could not find 
  him, because he had escaped far inland.  She was disappointed and 
  committed suicide by drowning in the river.''  So, the river was 
  named the Hime River after Shizuka-gozen.

I guess that the legend was about the death of a courtesan ( ko'u-ti-
zan ) in the end of Edo era or in the Meiji Period.  ( Cf. There is 
Oiran-buchi in Minami Ward, Sapporo City, Ishikari sub-prefecture.  The 
name ``Oiran-buchi'' means ``Oiran ( courtesan ), abyss.''  In the 
past, a courtesan committed suicide by drowning in the river. )

( For river name, see Oshamanbe Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

( For Yoshitsune legend, see Biratori Town in Hidaka sub-prefecture ;  
  Esashi Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. )
 
#3

During the Tenmei period ( 1781-1789 ) in the Edo era, Otobe was 
``sengoku-basho'' of herring.  The word ``sengoku-basho'' means that 
the place boasted 1,000-koku catches of herring.  The word ``koku'' is 
a unit of volume.  One ``koku'' is equivalent to 150 kg of dried 
herring or 200 kg of fresh herring. 
 
( For one ``koku'' ( unit ) of rice, see Esashi Town in Hiyama sub-
  prefecture. ) 

( For one ``koku'' ( unit )  of rice wine, see Kuriyama Town in Sorachi 
  general sub-prefecture. ) 

During the Tenmei Famine ( fa'min ) ( 1782-1788 ) and the Tenpou Famine 
( 1833-1839 ), Japanese people from Nanbu, Hirosaki, and Akita 
imigrated to Otobe.  ( Nanbu was what are now Iwate and Aomori 
prefectures.  Hirosaki was what is now Aomori Prefecture. )

Otobe Town has districts of Sakaehama and Toyohama.  ``Sakae-hama'' 
means ``thriving beach'' in Japanese.  ``Toyo-hama'' means ``rich 
beach'' in Japanese.  These names are reminiscent of good old days.  
After 1917 ( Taishou 6 ), schools of herring disappeared from Otobe.

( For herring fishing, see Yoichi Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture ; Esashi Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. )
 
#4

In December 1868 ( Meiji 1 ), during the Boshin War, the Tokugawa 
Shogunate forces advanced to the castle town of the Matsumae Domain 
( now Matsumae Town ).  It was November of the lunar calendar used at 
the time.

The Matsumae Domain's forces escaped to Tate Castle in Assabu.  Tate 
Castle fell too.  They escaped to Otobe.  The lord of the Matsumae 
Domain escaped to Hirosaki ( now Aomori Prefecture ), and the rest of 
the Matsumae Domain's forces surrendered.  Villagers in Otobe hid 
Matsumae soldiers by dressing them in farmer's clothes.

( For the Boshin War, see Hakodate City in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For the end of the Matsumae Domain, see Assabu Town in Hiyama sub-
  prefecture. )
 
#5

A bus stop ``Otobe-juuji-gai'' is located near the town hall.  The name 
``juuji-gai'' is Japanese.  It means ``cross, road.''  The word ``juu-
ji'' means ``ten, character.''  The shape of the Kanji character for 
ten ( ``juu'' ) is a cross.

( UTF code ; ``juu'' = \u534. )

The place name``Juuji-gai'' can be found also in Fukagawa, Hakodate, 
Kushiro, Okushiri, Otaru, Shikaoi, Rumoi, and Yoichi in Hokkaidou.

( For the name ``juuji-gai,'' see Otaru City in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

The Kanji character ``juu'' is mysterious.  It becomes ``10,'' ``1,'' 
and ``0.''  For example, ``juu'' means 10, ``juu-go'' means 15, and 
``ni-juu'' means 20. 

In contrast to cross road ( ``juu-ji-gai'' ), castle towns in Honshuu 
Island have T-junctions ( ``tei-ji-ro'' ) and crank-shaped roads 
( ``kaguino-te'' ).  The Kanji character ``tei'' resembles the letter 
``T'' in both shape and pronunciation.

( UTF code ; ``tei'' = \u4E01. )

The complicated roads ( T-junctions and crank-shaped roads ) in castle 
towns were constructed for defensive purposes.  During the period of 
motorization, the complicated roads were obstructs for automobile 
traffic.  Today, some of them are suitable for sightseeing.

Motorization in Japan started in the late 1960s.  ( My father bought 
Nissan Cherry in the 1970s. )  The novel ``The Grapes Of Wrath'' 
depicts that the motorization in the USA started in the early 20th 
century.

( For crank-shaped road, see Toubetsu Town in Ishikari sub-
  prefecture. )

( For motorization, see the note of Kaminokuni Town in Oshima general 
  sub-prefecture ; the Otaru South section of Otaru City, Shiribeshi 
  general sub-prefecture. )

( For straight road, see Yuni Town in Sorachi general sub-prefecture. )
 
#6

Fishing ports in Otobe are old.  The name ``Guen-na-dai'' signifies the 
Guen-na period ( 1615-1624 ) in the Edo era.  A fishing ground was 
built in the period.  Near the fishing port of Toyohama, Hanaiso-suwa 
Shrine is located. It was built in 1617.  

The fishing port of Toyohama was included in the former Kabashira 
village.  Cape Shibi-no-saki, a tourist site, was also included in 
Kabashira village.  

Kabashira is a funny name.  It means ``mosquito, pillar'' in Japanese. 
The name comes from ``kapara-sirar'' in Ainu language, which  means 
``flat, rock.''

When I see the name ``Kabashira ( mosquito, pillar ), I remember seeing 
a large mosquito pillar in a hotel room in a tropical country.  ( A 
mosquito pillar is not a mosquito trap.  A lot of winged insects such 
as non-biting midges  ( mi'giz ) fly and form a pillar. )  

As for Shira-fura cliff, I guess that the name comes from ``sirar-hu-
ra'' in Ainu language, which means ``rock, raw, intestine.''  The 
eroded cliff resembles fish intestines.  
 
( Single of the chapter ; ``Viejo Rincon ( Old Corner )'' by Angelis.  
  --> http://youtu.be/rczPwb2O7B4 )

|                  |
|     OKUSHIRI     |
|                  |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 16  |
 ------------------
AREA 143 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 123, CULTIVATED 1 ; COUNTY = OKUSHIRI ; 
POP 4,604 ( 1990 ), 3,033 ( 2010 ) ; MEAN ANNUAL TEMP 10.2 C ( AV 
2000-2010 ), PRECIPI 859 MM ; COUNTRY SIGN = MAP OF ISLAND. 
 
LOCATION 

Okushiri ( o-ku-shi-ri ) is a town in Hiyama sub-prefecture, south 
Hokkaidou ( ``dou-nan'' ).  The area is Okushiri Island in the Sea of 
Japan.  Okushiri is the second largest island in Hokkaidou after 
Rishiri Island.

( UTF code ; Okushiri = \u5965\u5C3B. )

The size of Okushiri Island is about the same as Lake Saroma.  Coastal 
terraces are located to the east, paddy fields to the south, and 
coastal cliffs to the west and north.  Forests cover 86 percent of the 
area.

NAME 

The name ``Okushiri'' comes from ``i-kus-un-sir'' in Ainu language.  It 
means ``it, over there, exist, island.''  Or, it comes from ``i-kus-
sir'' in Ainu language.  It means ``it, over there, island.''  Both 
signified Okushiri Island, located across the sea as seen from 
Kumaishi.

HISTORY 

In the Edo era, the Matsumae Domain built Kujiri-basho ( a trading 
place ) in Okushiri Island.  Herring fishing prospered through the Edo 
era to the Meiji period.  In 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), an administrative 
office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) was built for the villages of Akaishi, 
Aonae, Tsurikake, and Yakushi. 

( UTF code ; Akaishi = \u8D64\u77F3.  Aonae = \u9752\u82D7.  Tsurikake 
  = \u91E3\u61F8.  Yakushi = \u85AC\u5E2B. )

In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), Okushiri Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established by the merger of four villages.  In 1966 ( Shouwa 41 ), 
Okushiri Town was established. 

( For 2nd-class municipality, see the note of Matsumae Town. )

TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC  

The town hall of Okushiri is located in Okushiri district to the east.  
Other built-up areas are located in Aonae district to the south and 
in Kamui-waki district ( now Yunohama district ) to the west.

Regarding transportation, Heart Land Ferry connects ports of Okushiri, 
Setana, and Esashi.  Okushiri Airport opened in 1974 in Aonae district. 
Hokkaidou Air System connects Okushiri and Hakodate airports. 

INDUSTRY 

Distinctive industries in Okushiri are fishing, food processing, and 
sightseeing.  STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = pasture 41 hectares in 2015, 
paddy rice 37, and orchards 31 ; dairy cows X ( concealed ) in 2015 and 
beef cattle X.  STATISTICS OF FISHERIES = squid ( flying squid 417 tons 
in 2017, other squid 8 ), sea urchins 111, Pacific cod 69, octopus 59, 

( continue ) --> atka mackerel 58, shellfish 22 ( abalone, 
``sakuragai'' ( mactra ), whelks ), right-eye flounder 7, puffer fish 
5, seaweed 5, olive flounder 4, yellowtail 1, herring - ( zero ), 
other fish 52, and other marine animals 23.

As for food processing, local specialties are rice wine ( ``sake'' ) 
and wine.  As for sightseeing, Nabetsuru Rock is located near the 
ferry port.  It's an icon of the island.

The country sign ( gateway sign ) is a map of the island.  It shows the 
location of strange shaped rocks and Bentenguu Shrine.  A trip to a 
remote island fascinates some people a lot.  #1  #2  #3

NOTE  

#1

In the past, Okushiri was the island of alcohol prohibition.  ( Alcohol 
prohibition is called ``kinshu'' in Japanese. )  When the herring catch 
was poor, fishermen drunk, and quarreled too much.  So, village chiefs 
( ``kochou'' ) banned drinking alcohol.  Prohibition lasted from 1885 
to 1890.

Today, rice wine ( ``sake'' ) and wine are the specialties of Okushiri. 
There even is an ``Ordinance to promote toasts with Okushiri's local 
rice wine.''  ( Japanese people became more polite when drinking. )

( For rice wine, see Asahikawa City in Kamikawa general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

( For wine, see Ikeda Town in Tokachi general sub-prefecture. )

#2

In July 1993, Okushiri earthquake happened and killed 230 people.  The 
magnitude was 7.8.  The disaster was due to tidal waves 
( ``tsunami'' ), fires, and landslides.  Geologically, Okushiri Island 
is located near the boundary between the Eurasian Plate and North 
American Plate.  
 
Being close to the epicenter, the first tidal wave arrived two to three 
minutes after the earthquake.  Tidal waves killed 198 people.  Soon 
after the earthquake, television channels broadcast live footage from 
the sky.  I watched on TV that Aonae district was burning at that 
night.  

In 1998, the town declared ``completion of recovery.''  In 2001, the 
Tsunami Museum opened in Aonae district.  
 
* * *

As for tidal waves, the Sanriku Coast in the Touhoku region was hit by 
tidal waves.  During the massve tidal waves in 2011, all children at an 
elementary school along the coast could escape.  Grandparents taught 
them the phrase ``tsunami-tendenko ( tidal wave, alone ).''  If a large 
earthquake occurs, you must escape alone as soon as possible. 

Earthquakes, tidal waves, eruptions, and torrential downpours are 
natural phenomena.  These are a cycle that has continued for millions 
of years.  The cycle shaped the land and eroded.  Without human 
presence, disasters don't occur.  Disasters occur because humans 
perceive them as such. 

I am not part of nature.  I am the subject that perceives nature.  I 
see the rain and think, ``Pluit, ergo sum ( It's raining, therefore I 
am ).'' 

#3  

These are strange shaped rocks in Okushiri Island ; ( 1 ) Nabe-tsuru 
Rock  ( the handle of an iron pot rock ), ( 2 ) Kabuto Rock ( warrior's 
helmet rock ), ( 3 ) Hoya Rock ( sea pineapple rock ), ( 4 ) Mottate 
Rock ( erection rock ), and ( 4 ) Muean-jima ( lonely island ).  There 
are unique stories with unique rocks.

The shape of Nabe-tsuru Rock was changed by the major earthquake of 
1993.  It was restored.  ( If you study geology, you will understand 
that ``Everything that has shape will disapper.'' )

( Single of the chapter ; ``Another Night Out'' by Unkle.  --> 
  http://youtu.be/xf__WD2e0dM )

|                  |
|      SETANA      |
|                  |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 17  |
 ------------------
AREA 639 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 500, CULTIVATED 54 ; COUNTY = KUDOU ; POP 
13,389 ( 1990 ) ( KITA-HIYAMA 7,030, SETANA 3,191, TAISEI 3,168 ), 
9,590 ( 2010 ) ; MEAN ANNUAL TEMP 8.8 C ( AV 1981-2010 ), PRECIPI 1,056 
MM ; COUNTRY SIGN = ROCK ON THE SEA. 

LOCATION   

Setana ( se-ta-na ) is a town in Hiyama sub-prefecture, south Hokkaidou 
( `dou-nan'' ).  Setana is located in the west of the Oshima Peninsula, 
facing the Sea of Japan.  The area was newly delimited in 2005 by the 
merger of three towns ; Taisei, Kita-hiyama, and Setana ( from south to 
north ).

Cape Obana is the western tip of Hokkaidou Island.  Ordinary tourists 
cannot go to the western tip, because it's a steep sea cliff.  #1

NAME

The name ``Taisei'' is Japanese.  It means ``big success.''  It was 
named by local residents when Kudou Village and Kaitorima Villages were 
merged in 1955.

( UTF code ; Kaitorima = \u8C9D\u53D6\u6F97.  Kudou = \u4E45\u9060. 
  Taisei = \u5927\u6210. ) 

The name ``Kita-hiyama'' is Japanese.  It means ``north, Hiyama.''  
The name ``Hiyama'' means ``cypress mountain.''  

( UTF code ; Kita-hiyama = \u5317\u6A9C\u5C71. )  

The name ``Setana'' comes from ``seta-rupespe-nay'' in Ainu language.  
It means ``dog, cross, river.''  It signified a place near the mouth of 
the Baba River.  Large rocks stand near the mouth of the river.  ( The 
Baba River flows through the center of the old Setana Town. ) 

( UTF code ; Setana ( before 2005 ) = \u702C\u68DA. )

The rocks are Sanbon-sugui-iwa Rock and Rousoku-iwa Rock.  ( In 
Japanese, ``sanbon-sugi'' means ``three cedars'' and ``rousoku'' means 
``candle.'' )  Sanbon-sugui-iwa Rocks stand from the sea and Rousoku-
iwa Rock stands at the Port of Setana.  They look like dogs.  #2

( For the name ``Hiyama,'' see Hiyama sub-prefecture. )

HISTORY 

In the Edo era, the Matsumae Domain established three trading places 
( ``basho'' ) for fishing.  They were located in Kudou, Futoro, and 
Setanai ( from south to north ).  The places thrived from herring 
fishing.  ( Futoro was located at the mouth of the Futoro River.  It 
flows through the former Kita-hiyama Town. ) 

After the Meiji period, three counties were established in 1869 
( Meiji 2 ).  They were Kudou, Futoro, and Setana counties.  The 
following is the history of the former three towns ;

OLD TAISEI TOWN ; In 1872 ( Meiji 5 ), Kudou branch of the Hokkaidou 
Development Commission was established.  In 1880 ( Meiji 13 ), the 
administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) of Issouma village was 
established.  It covered other five villages ; Hikata-tomari, Oota, 
Sansouma, Uekotan, and Yunoshiri.

( UTF code ; Hikata-tomari = \u65E5\u65B9\u6CCA.  Issouma = 
  \u4E00\u8258\u6F97.  Oota = \u592A\u7530.  Sansouma = 
  \u4E09\u8258\u6F97.  Uekotan = \u4E0A\u53E4\u4E39.  Yunoshiri = 
  \u6E6F\u5C3B. ) 

In the same year, the administrative office of Hiratanai was 
established.  It covered other two villages ; Kaitorima and Nagaiso. 
In 1881 ( Meiji 14 ), the administrative office of Kudou village was 
established by the merger of Hikata-tomari, Issouma, and Sansouma. 

In 1902 ( Meiji 35 ), Kudou Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established by the merger of Kudou, Oota, Uekotan, and Yunoshiri.  In 
1923 ( Taishou 12 ), Kaitorima Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established by the merger of Hiratanai, Kaitorima, and Nagaiso.

( UTF code ; Hiratanai = \u5E73\u7530\u5185.  Nagaiso = \u9577\u78EF. )

In 1955 ( Shouwa 30 ), Taisei Village was established by the merger of 
Kudou and Kaitorima.  In 1966 ( Shouwa 41 ), Taisei Town was 
established.

OLD KITA-HIYAMA TOWN ; In 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), an administrative office 
( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) was established for four villages in Futoro 
county ; Futoro, Gorota, Raruishi, and Udomari.  In 1902 ( Meiji 35 ), 
the administrative office of Higashi-setana village was established.  
It was separated from Setana village.

( UTF code ; Futoro = \u592A\u6AD3.  Gorota = \u53E4\u6AD3\u591A. 
  Higashi-setana = \u6771\u702C\u68DA.  Raruishi = \u826F\u7460\u77F3. 
  Udomari = \u9D5C\u6CCA. )

In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), Futoro Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established by the merger of Futoro, Gorota, Raruishi, and Udomari.  In 
the same year, Higashi-setana Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established. 

In 1953 ( Shouwa 28 ), Higashi-setana Town was established.  In 1955 
( Shouwa 30 ), Kita-hiyama Town was established by the merger of 
Higashi-setana Town and Futoro Village.

OLD SETANA TOWN ; In 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), two administrative offices 
( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) were established for five villages in Setana 
county ; Abura, Baikatsu, Nakauta, Setana, and Shimauta.  In 1897 
( Meiji 30 ), Toshibetsu village was separated from Setana village.  It 
was what is now Imakane Town, Hiyama sub-prefecture.

( UTF code ; Abura = \u867B\u7F85.  Baikatsu = \u6885\u82B1\u90FD. 
  Nakauta = \u4E2D\u6B4C.  Shimauta = \u5CF6\u6B4C. )

In 1902 ( Meiji 35 ), Setana Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established by the merger of five villages in Setana county.  Higashi-
setana village was separated from Setana Village.  ( This means that an 
administrative office was built in Higashi-setana village.  Higashi-
setana was merged to Kita-hiyama. ) 

In 1919 ( Taishou 8 ), Setana Village ( 1st-class municipality ) was 
established.  In 1921 ( Taishou 10 ), Setana Town was established.

TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC

The town hall of Setana is located near the mouth of the Toshibetsu 
River ( Shiribeshi-toshibetsu River ).  It used to be the town hall of 
Kita-hiyama Town.  National Route 229 ( Hiyama-kokudou ) runs along the 
Sea of Japan.  #3

National Route 230 ( Pirika-kokudou ) separates from National Route 229 
near the town hall.  It crosses the Oshima Peninsula along the 
Toshibetsu River and the Kunnui River.

Formerly, the Setana Line of the Japanese National Railways 
( Kokutetsu ) ran along National Route 230.  The railway connected 
Setana Station and Kun-nui Station in Oshamanbe Town.

The Port of Setana has a ferry line to Okushiri Island.  Windmills 
near the port are the first offshore windmills in the country.  In the 
built-up area of the former Setana Town, there was a clinic run by 
Guinko Oguino.  She was the first female medical doctor in the 
country.  #4

INDUSTRY  

Distinctive industries in Setana are fishing, paddy rice cultivation, 
and dairy farming.  STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = pasture 2,046 hectares 
in 2015, paddy rice 1,537, beans ( soybeans 353, adzuki beans 84, other 
beans 7 ), fodder 268, potatoes 153, buckwheat 146, sugar beets 64, 
wheat 48, broccoli 23, spinach 10, orchards 5, radishes 3, melons 1, 

( continue ) --> other vegetables 46+X ( sweet corn, pumpkins, 
asparagus ), and other grains 11 ; dairy cows 2,886 head in 2015.  
STATISTICS OF FISHERIES = squid ( flying squid 618 tons in 2017, other 
squid 3 ), salmon 283, sea urchins 133, octopus 65, Pacific cod 46, 
seaweed ( ``konbu'' kelp 26, other seaweed 5 ), 

( continue ) --> olive flounder 22, atka mackerel 19, right-eye 
flounder 17, yellowtail 16, shellfish ( abalone 2, other shellfish 10 
( oysters ) ), shrimp/prawns 9, sharks 4, puffer fish 2, herring 0, 
other fish 92, and other marine animals 16.

Local specialties include the rice wine ( ``sake'' ) that is made 
from locally grown rice.  #5
 
NOTE  

#1

Among Hokkaidou's northern tip, southern tip, eastern tip, and western 
tip, the western tip is the most difficult to visit.  It's a steep sea 
cliff at Cape Obana.  Including small islands, the western tip is 
Oshima-ooshima Island in Matsumae Town, Oshima general sub-prefecture.  
It's an uninhabited island. 

Oota Shrine at Cape Obana is one of the oldest Shintou shrines in 
Hokkaidou.  It was established around 1441.  It is one of the most 
difficult shrines to visit. 

The shrine is located at the top of a rocky mountain.  Bears live. 
Visitors are like climbers.  They will pray at the shrine for a safety 
descent down the mountain.  In the past, mountaineering was a religious 
training. 

#2

On the map published by Takeshirou Matsuura in 1859, Haha-gawa ( now 
Baba River ) and Setaruheshi-nai are written together ( http://
koukita.github.io/touzaiezo/map/ ).

Tadashi Okudaira wrote that the upper course of the Baba River was 
called the Setanai River.  Sanbon-sugui-iwa Rocks, which Ainu people 
saw as dogs ( ``seta'' ), are located at the mouth of the Baba River 
( T. Okudaira et al. 2007 ).

I asked the town hall where the Setani River was.  They answered that 
it might be the current Toshibetsu River ( Shiribeshi-toshibetsu 
River ).  They showed a map of Setanai Basho ( Setanai trading place ) 
as evidence.  The map was drawn in the Ansei period ( 1855-1860 ) of 
the Edo era. 

Hiyama sub-prefecture and Oshima general sub-prefectures have a long 
history of Japanese settlement.  Some river names and place names were 
changed from Ainu language to Japanese.  The followings are examples.

( 1 ) The Baba River in Setana Town, which may have been called the 
Setanai River.
( 2 ) The Hime River in Otobe Town, whose old name was the Otohe River. 
( 3 ) The Torizaki River in Mori Town, whose old name was the Oniushi 
River.

( For river name in Ainu language, see Oshamanbe Town in Oshima general 
  sub-prefecture. )

As for the name ``Seta-nai'' ( ``dog, river'' in Ainu language ), there 
is an Ainu legend ; ``Once upon a time, large dogs came down from the 
mountains.  They were messengers of god.  They went into the sea and 
turned into rocks.''

( For dog, see Makkari Village in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture. )

( For dog rock, see Furubira Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

During a bus excursion, we stopped in front of Sanbon-sugui-iwa Rocks 
and ate lunch boxes.  One of the students met an elderly man walking 
along a national highway.

 He said,  ``During Okushiri earthquake, tidal waves ( ``tsunami'' ) 
surrounded Sanbon-sugui-iwa Rocks and reached this highway.  After the 
water subsided, sea urchins and flounders were left on the road.''

( For Okushiri earthquake, see Okushiri Town in Hiyama sub-
  prefecture. )  
 
#3

National Route 229 runs along the coast.  But, near Cape Obana, which 
is the western tip of Hokkaidou Island, the highway runs inland.  Cape 
Obana was the border between the former Taisei Town and the former 
Kita-hiayama Town.  There are strangely shaped rocks along the national
highway ; Mother Bear and Cub Rocks, Mammoth Rock, and Raccoon Rock.
 
#4  

Guinko Oguino ( 1851-1913 ) was born in Tawarase village ( now Kumagaya 
City, Saitama Prefecture ).  In 1885, she passed medical practitioner's 
examination and opened a clinic for gynecology and obstetrics.  She 
became the first female medical doctor in the country.  Her clinic was 
located in Yushima, Toukyou.

In 1891, her husband, Yukiyoshi Shikata, started developing Immanuel 
district.  Immanuel was located in what is now Imakane Town, Hiyama 
sub-prefecture.  It was in the upper reaches of the Shiribeshi-
toshibetsu River.  The area was developed by Christians.  

( For the name ``Immanuel,'' see Imakane Town in Hiyama sub-
  prefecture. )

In 1894, Guinko Oguino moved to Immanuel.  Then, her husband failed 
in development and became a missionary.  In 1897, she moved to Setana 
and opened a clinic.  ( Setana is located near the mouth of the 
Shiribeshi-toshibetsu River. )

In 1903, she went to Sapporo.  She wanted to open a larger clinic in 
Sapporo, however, she was told that her medical technique was 20 years' 
behind.  She was disappointed and returned to Setana.  In 1905, her 
husband died. In 1908, she returned to Toukyou. 

As for a medical doctor, I lived in a teachers' apartment in Sapporo.  
Next to the apartment was a clinic for internal medicine and 
pediatrics.  The elderly doctor said, ``I was originally internist.  
When I added pediatrics in this clinic, my friends scorned me.  But, I 
had to raise my children.''

He was proud that his three sons became medical doctors.  When I went 
to the clinic with the influenza, he said, ``The latest machine to 
test the influenza is expensive.  The debt will be left to my son, ha, 
ha, ha ( laugh ).''

After his sudden death, one of his sons came from a hospital and 
succeeded the clinic for about a year.  Then, the clinic was demolished 
and an apartment was built.
 
#5

A student submitted a graduation thesis to Faculty of Letters, 
Hokkaidou University.  It was about rice wine production in Setana 
Town.  The following is an excerpt ( e'x-cerpt ). 
	
``Rice wine producers are farmers.  They cultivate paddy rice and brew 
rice wine.  There are two trade names of rice wine.  One is Guinko-
monogatari and the other is Yoshiko.  ( ``Guinko-monogatari'' means 
Guinko's story.  Guinko Oguino was the first medical doctor in the 
country.  ``Yoshiko'' is also a woman's name. ) 

( continue ) --> Guinko-monogatari is made from brewery rice.  The 
variety is ``Guinpuu.''  The brewery rice is cultivated by rice-duck 
farming method.  It's a kind of organic farming.  Yoshiko is made from 
ordinary rice.  The variety is ``Kirara 397.''

( continue ) --> Rice wine producers wanted to use the names Kita-
hiyama and Suisen for their rice wine.  ``Kita-hiyama'' is the name of 
their town.  The word ``suisen'' means daffodil in Japanese.  Daffodil 
is the town's flower.  However, those names had been already 
trademarked.

( continue ) --> So, the name ``Guinko'' was selected.  She was a 
famous female doctor who lived in Setana.  The name ``Yoshiko'' was 
also selected because it is a common name in this region.  Actually, 
the wife and mother of rice wine producers are named Yoshiko.''
 
As for brewery rice, cultivation is difficult in Hokkaidou.  The grain 
is larger than that of ordinary rice.  ( The category ``guinjou'' is 
made from the core of brewery rice.  The core of ordinary rice is 
too small to brew ``guinjou.'' )  

Rice wine producers in Setana also use the ordinary rice variety, 
Kirara 397.  The rice wine retains its old fashioned flavor. 
( Hokkaidou's wine is made also from the ordinary grape variety, 
Naiagara.  ( ``Ordinary grapes'' are table grapes in Japan. )  Also, 
the locally grown mountain grapes ( Yama-budou ) are used.

As for brewery rice, the variety Yamada-nishiki is famous.  It has been 
used for the rice wine made in the Nada region ( now Hyougo 
Prefecture ) in the center of Honshuu Island.  Nada is famous for rice 
wine.
	
Nada's rice wine was shipped to Ezo ( now Hokkaidou ) by North-bound 
ships.  It is said that the rice wine transported by ship tastes 
better.  ( In the past, rice wine was not heat-treated. ) 

( For Kirara 397, see Uryuu Town in Sorachi general sub-prefecture ; 
  Pippu Town in Kamikawa general sub-prefecture. )

( For not-heat-treated rice wine, see the note of Akabira City in 
  Sorachi general sub-prefecture. )

( For rice wine, see Asahikawa City in Kamikawa general sub-
  prefecture ; Mashike Town in Rumoi sub-prefecture ; Kuriyama Town in 
  Sorachi general sub-prefecture. )  

( For wine, see Ikeda Town in Tokachi general sub-prefecture. )

( Single of the chapter ; ``West End Girls'' by Pet Shop Boys.  This 
  chapter is about the west end.  --> http://youtu.be/p3j2NYZ8FKs )

|                  |
|     IMAKANE      |
|                  |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 18  |
 ------------------
AREA 568 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 455, CULTIVATED 53 ; COUNTY = SETANA ; 
POP 7,710 ( 1990 ), 6,186 ( 2010 ) ; MEAN ANNUAL TEMP 7.9 C ( AV 1981-
2010 ), PRECIPI 1,327 MM, SNOW 657 CM ; COUNTRY SIGN = SEA COW. 
  
LOCATION   

Imakane ( i-ma-ka-ne ) is a town in Hiyama sub-prefecture, south 
Hokkaidou ( ``dou-nan'' ).  Imakane is located inland in the Oshima 
Peninsula.

( UTF code ; Imakane = \u4ECA\u91D1. )

The area corresponds to the middle to upper reaches of the Shiribeshi-
toshibetsu River, which flows to the Sea of Japan.  ( The downstream 
area is Setana Town. )  ( The Shiribeshi-toshibetsu River is called 
the Toshibetsu River by local residents. )

NAME 

The name ``Imakane'' is Japanese.  It is derived from two pioneers, 
Toujirou Imamura and Ishirou Kanamori ( Ima- and Kane- ).  Both 
were graduates of Toukyou Professional School ( now Waseda 
University ).  #1

HISTORY 

In the Edo era, the Matsumae Domain mined placer gold in the upper 
reaches of the Shiribetsu-toshibetsu River.  In the Meiji period, there 
were agate and manganese mines.

In 1893 ( Meiji 26 ), two pioneers settled in what is now Imakane 
district.  In the same year, a group of Christians ( Kirisuto-kyou-
dantai ) settled in Immanuel district.  #2

In 1897 ( Meiji 30 ), the administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) 
of Toshibetsu village was established.  It was separated from Setana 
village.  In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), Toshibetsu Village ( 2nd-class 
municipality ) was established.  

( UTF code ; Toshibetsu = \u5229\u5225. )

In 1929 ( Shouwa 4 ), Toshibetsu Village ( 1st-class municipality ) was 
established.  In 1947 ( Shouwa 22 ), Imakane Town was established.  At 
the time, Toshibetsu was renamed Imakane.

( For 1st-class and 2nd-class municipalities, see the note of Matsumae 
  Town. )

( For gold, see Naka-tonbetsu Town in Souya general sub-prefecture. )
 
TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC

The town hall of Imakane is located along the middle reaches of the 
Shiribeshi-toshibetsu River.  National Route 230 ( Pirika-kokudou or 
Hana-ishi-douro ) runs.  #3  #4

In the past, the Setana Line ran along the Shiribeshi-toshibetsu River. 
The railway crossed the Oshima peninsula.  It connected Setana Station 
on the Sea of Japan side with Kun-nui Station on the Pacific Ocean 
side.  ( Setana Station was located in the former Setana Town.  Kun-nui 
Station on the Hakodate Main Line is located in Oshamanbe Town. ) 

INDUSTRY 

Distinctive industries in Imakane are agriculture, animal husbandry, 
and forestry.  STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = pasture 1,446 hectares in 
2015, paddy rice 1,414, beans ( soybeans 376, adzuki beans 208 ), 
potatoes 396, wheat 273, fodder 229, sugar beets 117, buckwheat 69,

( continue ) --> carrots 32, broccoli 24, barley 19, tomatoes 13, 
onions 5, and other grains 24 ; dairy cows 2,021 head in 2015 and pigs 
and sows X ( concealed ).

Potatoes are sold with the trade-name ``Imakane-danshaku.''  As for 
animal husbandry, swine farms run by Nippon Ham Group are distributed 
in the mountains.  #5 

As for sightseeing, the Pirika Dam is located near Pirika Pass.  Its 
long long concrete dyke stands out along National Route 230.  The 
country sign ( gateway sign ) is Piiri and Piika, which are nicknames 
for sea cow fossils excavated from the dam construction site.  #6

NOTE   
 
#1

As for the name Imakane, local post office is called ``Imagane'' ( not 
Imakane ).  The former railroad station was also Imagane.  But, the 
name on the topographical map is Imakane.  ( Topographical maps are 
published by Geographical Survey Institute. )

Imakane's former name was Toshibetsu.  The name comes from ``tus-pet'' 
in Ainu language.  It means ``snake ( or rope ), river.''  Or, it comes 
from ``to-us-pet'' in Ainu language.  It means ``pond, many, river.''  
Both signified the Shiribeshi-toshibetsu River. 

The Shiribeshi-toshibetsu River was ranked as the cleanest river.  The 
ranking is listed in Current Water Quality of Class-A Rivers in the 
Country ( Zenkoku-ikkyuu-kasen-no-suishitsu-guenkyou ), which is 
published annually by Ministry of Land, Infrastructure, Transport, and 
Tourism.

( For clean river, see Rankoshi Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

( UTF code ; Zenkoku-ikkyuu-kasen-no-suishitsu-guenkyou = 
  \u5168\u56FD\u4E00\u7D1A\u6CB3\u5DDD\u306E\u6C34\u8CEA\u73FE\u6CC1. )

#2

A Christian group settled in Immanuel.  They wanted to make their 
utopia.  The name ``Immanuel'' means ``God with us'' in Hebrew.  
Immanuel was located in what is now Kamioka district.  The name ``Kami-
oka'' means ``god, hill'' in Japanese.

Guinko Oguino, the first female doctor in the country, came to 
Immanuel.  Her husband was a member of the Christian group.  An old map 
published in the Edo era shows the place name ``Imanpetsu.''  The 
Christian group may have imagined Immanuel.

( For Guinko Oguino, see Setana Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. )

#3

Hana-ishi district is located near the Pirika Dam.  The word ``hana-
ishi'' is Japanese.  It means ``agate ( a'get )'' in English and
``menou'' in Japanese.  Hana-ishi was the largest agate mine in the 
country in the Meiji period.  The agate was shipped to Fukui Prefecture 
and carved into ornaments ( Wakasa-menou-zaiku ). 

Nowadays, agate is mainly imported from Brazil.  When I visited Sao 
Paulo, Brazil, I entered a souvenir shop.  I met a Japanese man in the 
shop.  He recommended me to buy agate chopstick rests.  He said, 
``Agate is artificially colored, but this is natural.''  It was brown. 
He was the president of a construction company and knew many things.

#4

Near the center of Imakane Town, a loop bridge stands out.  The bridge 
continues up to Hikaridai district, an agricultural area on a hill.  
The loop bridge looks like an expressway, but it is an agricultural 
road called ``Nou-men-douro.'' 
--> http://maps.gsi.go.jp/#16/42.429863/140.024700

The agricultural road ( Nou-men-douro ) is constructed using gasoline 
tax.  The use of the tax is considered a deduction for gasoline tax 
used for agricultural machinery. 

Other loop bridges in Hokkaidou are located in Sunagawa City ( Sorach 
general sub-prefecture ), Toyoura Town ( Iburi general sub-
prefecture ), and Asari district in Otaru City ( Shiribeshi general 
sub-prefecture ).  
--> http://maps.gsi.go.jp/#16/43.486820/141.955290 
--> http://maps.gsi.go.jp/#16/42.661910/140.744562
--> http://maps.gsi.go.jp/#16/43.135989/141.040850

#5

``Danshaku'' is a potato cultivar.  The word ``danshaku'' means 
``baron'' in Japanese.  ( ``Danshaku'' and May-queen are popular potato 
cultivars for cooking.  ``Kona-fubuki'' is popular potato cultivar for 
processing.  The name ``kona-fubuki'' means ``powder snowstorm.'' ) 

( For ``danshaku'' potato, see Kucchan Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

( For May-queen potato, see Assabu Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. ) 

( For potato, see Shihoro Town in Tokachi general sub-prefecture. )

Imakane potatoes have a reputation for quality.  The potato acreage per 
farm is smaller than that of the municipalities in Tokachi general sub-
prefecture.  ( Tokachi is a major potato-producing region. )

A former high school teacher said ; ``Potatoes are cultivated in crop 
rotation in Tokachi.  Potatoes can be cultivated after potatoes in 
south Hokkaidou ( `dou-nan,' including Imakane Town ).''

In Tokachi, potatoes are cultivated after sugar beets.  Then, beans or 
wheat are cultivated after potatoes.  In Imakane, farmers take time on 
their smaller land.  They remove the so-called stray potatoes.

The word ``stray potato'' is translation of ``nora-imo'' in Japanese. 
Stray potatoes are the rest of the former harvest.  They give a bad 
impact on the next cultivation.  In Tokachi, stray potatoes are killed 
during the cold winter.  But, recently, not all stray potatoes are 
killed because of climate change.

#6

The Pirika Dam is 1,480 meters wide.  It's the widest concrete dam in 
the country.  The dam was constructed between 1975 and 1991 at the 
confluence of the three rivers ; the Shiribeshi-toshibetsu River, 
the Pirikabetsu River, and the Chuushibetsu River.

The Pirika Dam is used for power generation, irrigation, and flood 
control.  Driftwood collected from the dam lake is distributed free of 
charge for personal use, such as for wood-burning stoves and gardening.
 
Kur ( ku'-a ) Plaza Pirika is located in front of the dam.  It includes 
a hot spring, a ski resort, and a restaurant.  The specialty of the 
restaurant is dam curry.  Rice is blocking the curry like a dam.

( For curry and rice, see Hakodate City in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )

In the upper reaches of the Pirikabetsu River, Okupirika hot spring is 
located along Prefectural Route 999.  The name ``Pirika-betsu'' means 
``beautiful river'' in Ainu language.  The Kanji characters for Pirika 
also means ``beautiful, benefit, river.''

( UTF code ; Pirika = \u7F8E\u5229\u6CB3. ) 

( Single of the chapter ; ``A La Peche ( At The Fishing )'' by Flying 
  Pop's.  --> http://youtu.be/hiwgOWo7mDc )

|    SHIMAMAKI     |
|                  |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 19  |
 ------------------
AREA 437 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 393, CULTIVATED 3 ; COUNTY = SHIMAMAKI ; 
POP 2,502 ( 1990 ), 1,781 ( 2010 ) ; COUNTRY SIGN = WATERFALL.  

LOCATION   
 
Shimamaki ( shi-ma-ma-ki ) is a village in Shiribeshi general sub-
prefecture, central Hokkaidou ( ``dou-ou'' ).  Shimamaki is located at 
the foot of the Oshima Peninsula.  The area faces the Sea of Japan.  
Forests cover 90 percent of the area. 
 
( UTF code ; Shimamaki = \u5CF6\u7267. )

NAME

The name ``Shimamaki'' comes from ``suma-ko-mak-i'' in Ainu language.  
It means ``rock, there, back, place.''  Or, it comes from ``suma-oma-
i'' in Ainu language.  It means ``rock, exist, place.''  Both signified 
a large rock at the mouth of the Tomari River.  

( The rock is now a concreted cliff of 57 meters' high.  ( Map --> 
  http://maps.gsi.go.jp/#16/42.697608/140.056297/ ) )

HISTORY 

In the Edo era, the Matsumae Domain established Shimakomaki-basho 
( trading place ).  Merchants from Esashi started herring fishing in 
the early 19th century.  

( For herring fishing, see Esashi Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture and 
  Yoichi Town in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture. )

In the Edo era, placer ( ple'i-cer ) gold was mined in the upper 
reaches of the Chihase River and the Tomari River.  Today, beech 
forests spread in the upper reaches of those rivers.  #1
 
In 1872 ( Meiji 5 ), a branch office of the Hokkaidou Development 
Commission was established in Shimamaki county.  The administrative 
office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) of Nagatoyo village was established.  It 
was a continuation of village chief ( ``kochou'' ) system from the Edo 
era. 

( UTF code ; Nagatoyo = \u6C38\u8C4A. ) 

( In Japanese ; ``Shimamaki county'' is Shimamaki-gun.  ``The Hokkaidou 
  Development Commission'' is Kaitakushi. )
 
In 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), Shimamaki county and the administrative office 
of Nagatoyo village were re-established.  The administrative office 
included villages of Chiwase, Etorohe, and Harauta.  #2

( UTF code ; Chiwase = \u5343\u8D70.  Etorohe = \u6C5F\u6CE5\u8FBA. 
  Harauta = \u539F\u6B4C. ) 

In the same year, the administrative office of Hon-me village was 
established.  It included villages of Karu-usu and Utashima.  In 1873 
( Meiji 13 ), the county hall ( ``gun-yakusho'' ) of Suttsu and other 
three counties was established.  The others were Isoya, Shimamaki, and 
Utasutsu counties.  #3  

( UTF code ; Hon-me = \u672C\u76EE.  Karu-usu = \u8EFD\u81FC. 
  Utashima = \u6B4C\u5CF6. )

( For Suttsu county, see Suttsu Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

In 1887 ( Meiji 20 ), the administrative office of Nagatoyo and other 
six villages was established.  The others were villages of Chiwase, 
Etorohe, Harauta, Hon-me, Karu-usu, and Utashima.  In 1897 ( Meiji 
30 ), Suttsu sub-prefecture was established.  The county hall of Suttsu 
was abolished. 

In 1899 ( Meiji 32 ), Hon-me village was separated from Nagatoyo 
village.  The Oohira River became the border.  In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), 
Nishi-shimamaki Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was established in 
the area of Nagatoyo village.  Higashi-shimamaki Village ( 2nd-class 
municipality ) was established in the area of Hon-me village.  

( For 2nd-class municipality, see the note of Matsumae Town. )  

In 1910 ( Meiji 43 ), Suttsu sub-prefecture was abolished.  Shiribeshi 
sub-prefecture was established.  In 1956 ( Shouwa 31 ), Shimamaki 
Village was established by the merger of Higashi-shimamaki Village and 
Nishi-shimamaki Village.

VILLAGE HALL, TRAFFIC

The village hall of Shimamaki is located near the mouth of the 
Tomari River.  National Route 229 runs along the coast.  

( For the name ``Tomari,'' see Tomari Village in Shiribeshi general 
  sub-prefecture. )

The national highway and tunnels at Cape Motta opened in 1976.  After 
that, Shimamaki Village and Setana Town were connected by road.  
( Shimamaki is in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture.  Setana is in 
Hiyama sub-prefecture. )

Cape Motta Lighthouse is the 2nd highest lighthouse in the country.  
The elevation at the light is 282 meters.  A forest road leads to the 
lighthouse from Setana Town.  ( The highest lighthouse is Cape Amarube-
saki Lighthouse in Hyougo Prefecture.  The elevation of the light is 
284 meters. )
 
( UTF coe ; Motta = \u8302\u6D25\u591A. )
 
INDUSTRY

Distinctive industries in Shimamaki are fishing and sightseeing.  
STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = pasture 160 hectares in 2015, paddy rice 
24, orchards 12, and other vegetables 6+X ( ``X'' is concealed ) ; 
dairy cows - ( ``-'' is zero ) in 2015.  

STATISTICS OF FISHERIES = walleye pollock 1,097 tons in 2017, atka 
mackerel 435, squid ( flying squid 307, other squid 4 ), salmon 168, 
yellowtail 169, octopus 139, sand lance 112, puffer fish 61, Pacific 
cod 57, sea urchins 54, olive flounder 40, 

( continued ) --> crabs ( red snow crabs 35, other crabs 4 ), 
shrimp/prawns 23, right-eye flounder 15, shellfish 10, mackerel 4, 
seaweed 3, sharks 1, herring 0, other fish 196+X, and other marine 
animals 26. 
 
The main catches of fish in order of monetary value are sea cucumbers, 
atka mackerel, sand lance, and prawns ( as of 2010 ).  Sea cucumbers 
are exported to China.  They are an expensive delicacy in China.  #4
  
Ar for sightseeing, Garou Falls has been selected as one of the 100 
Best Waterfalls in Japan.  It's located on the Garou Highlands.  The 
Garou Highlands is located in the upper reaches of the Chihase River.  
The waterfall dorops 70 meters. 
  
Enoshima Beach ( Enoshima-kaigan ) has been selected as one of the 100 
Best Beaches in Japan.  ( This isn't Enoshima Beach in Kanagawa 
Prefecture. )  Several hot springs remain in Shimamaki Village ; Guunai 
Hot Spring, Chihase-gawa Hot Spring, Motta-kaigan Hot Spring, and Ougon 
Hot Spring.  #5
 
( UTF code ; Chihase-gawa = \u5343\u8D70\u5DDD.  Enoshima = 
  \u6C5F\u30CE\u5CF6.  Guunai = \u5BAE\u5185. )

NOTE
 
#1

The upper reaches of the Chihase River are the Garou Highlands ( Garou-
kougen ).  The elevation ranges from 500 to 800 meters.  Beech forests 
spread across the highlands.  They constitute one of the largest beech 
forests in the country, covering an area of 10,700 hectares.

( UTF code ; Garou Highlands = \u8CC0\u8001\u9AD8\u539F. )

The largest beech forests in the country are located in the Shirakami 
Mountains, straddling the border between Akita Prefecture Aomori 
Prefecture, covering an area of 16,971 hectares.  It has been 
designated as a World Heritage site.  ( by Ministry of Agriculture,
Forestry and Fisheries ``Overview Of The World Natural Heritage Site 
Shirakami-sanchi'' http://
www.rinya.maff.go.jp/j/sin_riyou/sekaiisan/sirakami_gaiyou.html  last 
accessed in August 2026).  

The largest beech forests of the World Heritage site remained because 
they are located on the border.  The antonym ( anto'nim ) of ``border'' 
is ``center.''  Japanese people didn't use Beech trees as much. 
 
( For beech tree, see Kuromatsunai Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

#2
 
The name ``Naga-toyo" means ``long, wealth'' in Japanese.  It was an 
auspicious name ( good-luck name ), given by the Shimamaki county's 
branch of Hokkaidou Development Commission. 

The reason for the naming is unknown, because the administrative office 
of Nagatoyo village was burned down in the Meiji period.  ( by 
``Shimamaki Village History Chronology ( Shimamaki-sonshi-nenpyou )'' 
published in 1984. )

( For auspicious name, see Chitose City in Ishikari sub-prefecture. )

#3 
 
The name ``Hon-me'' is derived from ``pon-moy'' in Ainu language, which 
means ``small, inlet.''  Or, it's derived from ``hump-nay'' in Ainu 
language, which means ``whale, river.'' 

Ainu people hunted whales using poison-tipped harpoons.  They also ate 
whales washed ashore.  On the day they ate whale, they didn't go out 
to crop fields, because they thought that the power of the fatty whale 
meat caused negative effects on crops.

Ainu people lived basically on hunting and gathering.  They also 
cultivated millet and foxtail millet for self-sufficiency.  The poison 
applied to harpoons was extracted from monks-hood. 

( In Japanese ; ``Foxtail millet'' is ``awa.''  ``Millet'' is ``hie.'' 
  ``Monks-hood'' is ``tori-kabuto.'' )

#4 
 
In China, sea cucumbers are written as ``sea mouse'' for their 
appearance.  They are also written as ``sea Ginseng'' because they have 
a tonic effect.

China is a large continental nation.  I met Mr. Lee from China and 
asked if he had ever eaten sea cucumber.  He said, ``No, I don't.  
The look is disgusting.''  He is from Sichuan Province, interior China. 
  
Then, I remembered a story.  ``In the state of Mato Grosso, located in 
interior Brazil, a Japanese-Brazilian managed a farm.  He bought prawns 
for the New Year's holiday.  He treated the farm workers to prawns, but 
they didn't eat, saying, `We don't eat worms.'''  ( by T. Nakasumi 
1990.  ``Pantanal'' published by Mumyousha-shuppan ( Akita City ). )
 
#5
 
Near Garou Falls, natural sparkling water ( carbonated water ) springs 
out.  The water is called ``Ryuu-jin-no-mizu'' in Japanese, which means 
``dragon, god, of, water. )'' 

( In Japanese ; ``Sparkling water'' is ``tansan-sui.'' ) 

( Dragon is a god of water.  If you find a stone monument inscribed 
  with ``Ryuu-jin ( dragon, god ),'' the location may have suffered 
  flood damage in the past. )

As for sparkling water, when I visited Brazil, I found they sold 
sparkling water and ordinary mineral water at super markets, kiosks, 
and restaurants.  
 
( In Portuguese ; ``Sparkling water'' is ``agua com gas.''  ``Ordinary 
  mineral water'' is ``agua sem gas.'' )  

( UTF code ; ``agua com gas'' = \u00E1gua com g\u00E1s. )

I liked to drink sparkling water in Brazil.  In Japan, sparkling 
drinks ( carbonated drinks ) available were sweet ones such as ciders 
and Coca-Cola.

After returning to Japan, I found that sparkling water was sold at the 
liquor section of supermarkets.  After around 2011, Japanese people 
started drinking sparkling water ( without sugar and liquor ) ( by 
``Tansan-no-rekishi ( History Of Carbonated Water )'' http://
www.asahigroup-japan.co.jp/material/tansan/history/  last accessed in 
August 2026. ) 

Today ( as of 2026 ), natural sparkling water from Kaneyama Town in 
Fukushima Prefecture is being sold.  Sparkling wines, including 
Champagne, and beers contain natural carbonation produced during 
fermentation. 

Japan receives a lot of rain fall, so once Japanese people thought 
water was free.  In 1983, water was sold in one-liter paper cartoons.
In 1996, water was sold in 500-milliliter PET bottles.  ( by Japanese
articles of Wikipedia on mineral water and PET bottle. )

I started buying mineral water in 1990 when I lived in a dormitory of 
University of Tsukuba.  The water came from Lake Kasumigaura and 
wasn't good.  

( For water, see Kuromatsunai Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

* * *
  
In Shimamaki Village, there is a road that goes along the Chihase River 
through the forests.  It reaches Garou Falls.  Meanwhile, there is no 
road in the upper reaches of the Tomari River, where Ougon Falls and 
Ougon Hot Spring are located.   

The name ``ougon'' means ``gold'' in Japanese.  The names of the hot 
spring and the water fall are derived from placer gold mining in the 
Edo era.  Today, ordinary tourists cannot go those places.  Because the 
road is broken and bears appear. 
 
In the past, forestry and mining were thriving and roads existed.  
Until the 1950s, there were manganese and limestone mines.
 
( For gold, see Naka-tonbetsu Town in Souya general sub-prefecture. )

( Single of the chapter ; ``Nano'' by Spangle Call Lilli Line.  -->
  http://youtu.be/0zUE1E3e7Mg )
