|                  |
|     RUSUTSU      |
|                  |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 25  |
 ------------------
AREA 120 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 81, CULTIVATED 22 ; COUNTY = ABUTA ; POP 
2,369 ( 1990 ), 2,034 ( 2010 ) ; COUNTRY SIGN = FERRIS WHEEL, HANG 
GLIDER, & MT SHIRIBETSU. 

( UTF code ; Abuta = \u867B\u7530. )

LOCATION   

Rusutsu ( ru-su-tsu ) is a village in Shiribeshi general sub-
prefecture, central Hokkaidou ( ``dou-ou'' ).  Rusutsu is an inland 
town.  The name ``Rusutsu'' is also pronounced as ``Rusuttsu.'' 

( UTF code ; Rusutsu, Rusuttsu = \u7559\u5BFF\u90FD. )

The area includes the divide that separates the Nukkibetsu River and 
the Nobori-enkoro River.  The Nukkibetsu river flows to Uchiura Bay on 
the Pacific Ocean.  The Nobori-enkoro river is a tributary of the 
Shiribetsu River, which empties in the Sea of Japan. 
 
There are two prominent mountains.  Mt. Shiribetsu-dake ( 1,107 
meters ) is a cone-shaped mountain to the north.  Mt. Nukkibetsu-yama 
( 994 meters ) is an eroded mountain to the south.  

( There is also Mt. Nukibetsu-yama ( 1,317 meters ) In Biratori Town, 
  Hidaka sub-prefecture. They have same Kanji characters.  The name for 
  the two mountains comes from ``nupki-pet'' in Ainu language, which 
  means ``murky, river.'' )

( UTF code ; Nukkibetsu-yama = Nukibetsu-yama = 
  \u8CAB\u6C17\u5225\u5C71. )

NAME  

The name ``Rusutsu'' comes from ``ru-sut'' in Ainu language.  It means 
``road, root.''  It signified the road that went around the foot of Mt. 
Shiribetsu-dake.  #1
 
HISTORY 

In 1871 ( Meiji 4 ), Higashi Honganji Temple from Kyouto Prefecture 
constructed the Honganji Road.  At the time, three families settled 
and became residents of the post town.  

In 1887 ( Meiji 20 ), Bunzou Hashiguchi opened farms in the areas that 
are now Rusutsu Village and Soubetsu Town.  ( Soubetsu Town is located 
in Iburi general sub-prefecture. )  #2
 
In 1897 ( Meiji 30 ), the administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) 
of Makkari village ( now Rusutsu Village ) was built.  It was separated 
from Abuta village ( now Touyako Town in Iburi general sub-
prefecture ).  

In 1901 ( Meiji 31 ), Kaributo village ( now Niseko Town ) separated 
from Makkari village.  In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ) Makkari Village ( 2nd-class 
municipality ) was established.  In 1917 ( Taishou 6 ), Kimobetsu 
Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was separated from Makkari Village. 
#3 

In 1922 ( Taishou 11 ), Makkari-betsu Village ( 2nd-class 
municipality ) was separated from Makkari Village.  In 1925 ( Taishou 
14 ), Makkari Village was renamed Rusutsu Village.  Thus, Rusutsu 
Village was originally called Makkari Village.  #4

( For 2nd-class municipality, see the note of Matsumae Town. )   

( For Honganji Road, see Kimobetsu Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

VILLAGE HALL, TRAFFIC
 
The village hall of Rusutsu is located south of Mt. Sori-oi-yama ( 715 
meters ).  Mt. Sori-oi-yama is located south of Mt. Shiribetsu-dake. 
National Route 230 ( Nakayama-kokudou ) passes.  ( ``Nakayama'' is the 
mountain pass that divides Sapporo City and Kimobetsu Town. )

( For Nakayama Pass, see Kimobetsu Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )
 
The altitude of the village hall is 371 meters.  It's the highest among 
the municipalities in Hokkaidou Prefecture.  #5 

INDUSTRY 

Distinctive industries in Rusutsu are sightseeing and agriculture. 
STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = potatoes 504 hectares in 2015, beans 
( adzuki beans 299, soybeans 67, other beans 7 ), sugar beets 261, 
pasture 120, wheat 74, carrots 45, yams 30, orchards 10, fodder 6, 

( continue ) --> cabbages 5, Chinese cabbages 2, paddy rice - ( zero ), 
and other vegetables X ( concealed, including asparagus, sweet corn, 
pumpkins ) ; dairy cows 146 head in 2015 and beef cattle 136. 
 
As for sightseeing, Rusutsu Resort is located at the foot of Mt. 
Nukkibetsu-yama.  A tower hotel, a Ferris wheel, and ski slopes are 
outstanding along National Route 230.  Rusutsu is one of the major
resorts in Hokkaidou.  It's managed by Kamori-kankou, which is a 
tourist company in Sapporo City, Ishikari sub-prefecture.  #6

NOTE    
 
#1
  
The word ``ru-sut'' was an Ainu word that meant a road at the foot of 
a mountain.  The road went around the west foot of Mt. Shiribetsu-dake. 
That corresponds to the current Prefectural Route 257 ( Rusutsu-
kimobetsu-sen ).  

In the Edo era, Takeshirou Matsuura traveled along the road twice.  In 
the Meiji period, this road became part of the Honganji Road, which 
connected Osaru-betsu on the Pacific Ocean side and Hiraguishi on the 
Sea of Japan side. 

( Osaru-betsu was located in what is now Date City, Iburi general sub-
  prefecture.  Hiraguishi is located in Sapporo City, Ishikari sub-
  prefecture. )

National Route 230 runs roughly along the Honganji Road.  The national 
highway goes around the east foot of Mt. Shiribetsu-dake and leads to 
Sapporo City. 

To the north of Rusutsu Village is Rusan district in Kimobetsu Town.  
The word ``Rusan'' comes from the Ainu word ``ru-o-san-i,'' which means 
``road, end, go out ( vanish ), place.''  

In Ainu language, ``ru'' means road.  When a place name contains the 
word ``ru,'' it usually means a mountain road. 

#2   

From 1879 to 1881, Bunzou Hashiguchi studied in the USA.  In 1883, he 
served as the director of a sugar factory in Nishi-monbetsu vilalge 
( now Date City, Iburi general sub-prefecture ).  
 
In 1887, he opened farms in the areas that are now Rusutsu Village and 
Soubetsu Town.  He aimed for large-scale Western-style farming, and 
imported agricultural machinery from the USA.  ( Rusutsu is located in
Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture.  Soubetsu is located in Iburi 
general sub-prefecture. ) 

The area of Hashiguchi Farm in Rusutsu was 1,800,000 ``tsubo'' ( 595 
hectares ).  The area of Hashiguchi Farm in Soubetsu was 100,000 
``tsubo'' ( 33 hectares ).  ( by Hokkaidou-kaitaku Club ``Bunzou 
Hashiguchi,'' http://
www.hokkaidokaitaku.club/retuden/kusawake/2020/hasiguchibunzo.html  
last accessed in August 2026. )

( For large farm in the Meiji period, see Kyougoku Town in Shiribeshi 
  general sub-prefecture. )
 
In 1888, Bunzou Hashiguchi became the principal of Sapporo Agricultural 
Collage ( now Hokkaidou University ).  Yasuaki Katou, who was a 
viscount ( ``shishaku'' ) and the final lord of the Iyo-oozu Domain, 
took over Hashiguchi Farm in Rusutsu.  In 1896, Bunzou Hashiguchi 
served as the Governor of Taihoku-ken in Taiwan. 

( In English ; ``Taihoku-ken'' is Taipei County. )
 
Rusutsu Village is a high-altitude and cool-climate region.  ( The 
elevation of the village hall is 371 meters. )  During the pioneering 
years, farmers cultivated flax, which grows under a cold climate, 
instead of rice.  Today, asparagus is a specialty of the village.

( In Japanese ; ``Flax'' is ``ama.'' )

( For asparagus, see Iwanai Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )  

#3 
  
In Rusutsu Village, there was Heimin-sha Farm from 1904 ( Meiji 37 ) to 
1906 ( Meiji 39 ).  In English, ``Heimin-sha'' can be translated into 
``Commoner's Company.''  It was a socialist collective farm.  
Socialists and Christians dreamed of their utopia in the north island, 
Hokkaidou.

In front of the village hall of Rusutsu is Red Shoes Park ( Akai-kutsu-
kouen ).  A statue of a mother and daughter stands in the park.  It 
represents the children's song Red Shoes ( Akai-kutsu ) written by Ujou 
Noguchi in 1921.  These are the lyrics ; 
 
``Akai-kutsu  Haite-ta  Onnano-ko  
  ( The little girl wearing red shoes, )

  Ijin-san-ni  Tsure-rare-te  Iiccha-ta''
  ( was gone with a foreigner. )

This song is based on the following true story.  
``A mother entrusted her young daughter to an American missionary 
  in Hakodate.  And the mother entered the socialist collective farm 
  ( Heimin-sha Farm ) in Rusutsu.  

  ( continue ) --> Her daughter contracted tuberculosis ( tu-ber-cu-
  lo'sis ) and died in an orphanage ( o'r-pha-nage ) in Azabu, Toukyou, 
  before she could emigrate to the USA.  

  ( continue ) --> The socialist collective farm failed in reclamation. 
  The mother moved to Sapporo.  In her later years, she passed away 
  without knowing of her daughter's death.''

Here is another children's song written by Ujou Noguchi in 1921.  The 
title is Blue Eyed Doll ( Aoi-me-no-nin-gyou ). 

``Aoi-me-wo-shita  Oningyo-wa  Amerika-umare-no  Seruroido  
  ( The blue-eyed doll is a celluloid, born in America, ) 

  Nihon-no-minato-e  Tsuitaa-toki  Ippai-namidawo  Ukabeteta.''
  ( When it arrived at a Japanese port, it filled with tears. ) 

* * * 

When I wrote this part, I remember Yuba Farm in the state of Sao Paulo,
Brazil.  I visited there in July 2008.  

A Japanese migrant, Isamu Yuba, opened the farm in 1935.  It's a 
collective farm.  About 50 people live.  They share their possessions, 
hold gatherings for Christian prayers, song and dance, and lead a self-
sufficient lifestyle.

I thought that their lifestyle wasn't suited to today's competitive 
society.  But, a Japanese Brazilian said, ``A person suffering from 
depression recovered after living on the collective farm.''
  
#4 

In the past, Rusutsu Village was called Makkkari Village.  The 
neighboring Makkari Village was called Makkari-betsu Village.  Usually, 
things were simpler in the past, but these places were complicated. 

Makkari elementary school was located in Makkari-betsu Village.  
Makkari Shrine was located in Makkari-betsu Village.  Many people went 
to the village hall of Makkari-betsu Village to submit documents for 
Makkari Village.  

In 1925, Makkari Village was renamed Rusutsu Village.  In 1941, 
Makkari-betsu Village was renamed Makkari Village. 

The Ainu word ``mak-kari-pet'' means the Makkari River, which flows 
along the south foot of Mt. Youtei-zan.  The place was today's Makkari 
Village, so the name ``Makkari'' is suitable to the former Makkari-
betsu Village ( now Makkari Village ).

The Ainu word ``ru-sut'' means a mountain road around Mt. Shiribetsu-
dake.  The place was today's Rusutsu Village, so the name ``Rusutsu'' 
is suitable to the former Makkari Village ( now Rusutsu Village ).  
Things returned to the way they were. 

#5

The altitude of a municipality is represented by the altitude of the 
municipality hall.  In Hokkaidou, the highest municipalities are ( 1 ) 
Rusutsu Village in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture, 371 meters, ( 2 ) 
Minami-furano Town in Kamikawa general sub-prefecture, 354 meters, 
( 3 ) Kamikawa Town in Kamikawa general sub-prefecture, 340 meters, 

( continue ) --> ( 4 ) Shimukappu Village in Kamikawa general sub-
prefecture, 333 meters, and ( 5 ) Yuubari City in Sorachi general sub-
prefecture, 312 meters.  Many municipalities in Hokkaidou are located 
along the coast and have low elevations.  This is because migrants came 
to Hokkaidou Island by ship and made villages along the coast.

In Japan, the highest municipalities are ( 1 ) Kawakami Village in 
Nagano Prefecture, 1,185 meters, ( 2 ) Kusatsu Town in Gunma 
Prefecture, 1,180 meters, and ( 3 ) Minami-maki Village in Nagano 
Prefecture, 1,030 meters.  Nagano Prefecture is the roof of Japan, 
Kamikawa general sub-prefecture is the roof of Hokkaidou Island.

The population of a municipality is also represented by the location of 
the municipality hall.  The population gravity center of the country is 
calculated by the sum of the municipalities' population.

The population gravity center of Hokkaidou is located in Kuriayama Town 
in Sorachi general sub-prefecture in 2015.  It's moving to the west.  
The population gravity center of Japan is located in Guifu Prefecture. 
It's moving to the east.  The population gravity center of the USA is 
located in the state of Missouri.  It's moving to the east. 

( For geographical gravity center, see Furano City in Kamikawa general 
  sub-prefecture. )

#6  
 
Rusutsu Resort includes ski slopes, a golf course, an amusement park, 
and a high-rise hotel.  The hotel hosted prefectural and national 
abacus competitions.
 
( In Japanese ; ``Abacus'' is ``soroban.'' )
 
In 1972, Rusutsu Ski Resort was developed by a real estate company in 
Saitama Prefecture.  In 1981, Kamori Kankou Co. in Sappro City took 
over the management.  In 1983, an amusement park, including a Ferris 
wheel, opened.  

In 1988, during the bubble economy period, a golf course opened.  In 
1989, the name was changed to Rusutsu Resort.  In 1993, a high-rise 
hotel with 23 floors opened.

In Rusutsu Resort, Mt. Nukkibetsu-yama is called Mt. Isola 
( i'-zo-ra ).  It was named after a aki resort in France.  ( When I 
hear this, I remember that Japanese people call apartments mansions.  )

Kamori Kankou, a tourist company, was founded in 1981.  Rusutsu Resort 
is the company's first management.  The predecessor of the company is a 
bear farm, which was established in Horobetsu Town ( now Noboribetsu 
City ) in 1958. 

After Rusutsu Ski Resort, Kamori Kankou started operations of ( 1 ) 
Sahoro Resort in Shintoku Town, Tokachi general sub-prefecture, ( 2 )  
Teine Olympia in Sapporo City, Ishikari sub-prefecture, and ( 3 ) 
Yuubari Resort in Yubari City, Sorachi general sub-prefecture.  The 
company revitalized bankrupt resorts. 
 
The head office of Kamori Kankou is located in Kamori-building No. 3.  
The address is Kita 4 - Nishi 4, Chuuou Ward, Sapporo City.  The 
building has signs of Rusutsu Resort and Rusutsu Plateau ( Rusutsu 
Kougen ).  

The Kamori building No. 3 was built in 1966.  It's one of the oldest 
concrete buildings in Sapporo.  ( The teacher's apartment in Hokkaidou 
University, which I lived, was also a concrete building built in 
1967. ) 

The Comprehensive Recreation Area Development Act, the so-called Resort 
Law, was enacted in 1987.  Then, resorts for domestic tourists were 
developed in the mountains and along the coast. 

( In Japanese ; ``The Comprehensive Recreation Area Development Act'' 
  is Sougou-hoyouchi-seibi-hou. ) 

The first resort designated under the Resort Law was Seagaia Resort.  
It opened in 1993 in Miyazaki Prefecture.  The high-rise hotel, 
standing 154 meters tall with 43 floors, opened in 1994.  Seagais is 
now Sheraton Grande Ocean Resort.  A high-rise hotel is the symbol of a 
resort. 
 
In the early 2000s, the government launched tourism policies aimed at 
foreign tourists.  For example, Prime Minister Jun-ichirou Koizumi 
started Visit Japan Campaign in 2003.

In 2003, the number of foreign tourists who visited Japan was 
5,211,725.  This is the World Ranking in 2003 ; ( 1 ) France 75.0 
million, ( 2 ) Spain 51.8, ( 3 ) USA 41.2, ( 4 ) Italy 39.6, ( 5 ) 
China 33.0, ( 6 ) UK 24.7, ( 7 ) Australia 19.1, ( 8 ) Mexico 18.7, 
( 9 ) Germany 18.4, ( 10 ) Canada 17.5,,, ( 32 ) Japan 5.2 ) ( by 
United Nation World Tourism Organization ). 
 
In 2017, an Australian professor visited our laboratory in Sapporo.  He 
was amazed that Japanese tourism policies were still going on and 
successful.  ( Japanese prime ministers changed frequently. )

In 2024, the number of foreign tourists who visited Japan was 
36,870,148.  This is the World Ranking in 2024 ; ( 1 ) France 102 
million, ( 2 ) Spain 93.8, ( 3 ) USA 72.4, ( 4 ) China 65.7, ( 5 ) 
Turkey 60.6, ( 6 ) Italy 57.7, ( 7 ) Mexico 45.0, ( 8 ) UK 38.2, ( 9 ) 
Germany 37.4, ( 10 ) Japan 36.9 ) ( by United Nation World Tourism 
Organization ). 
 
The success of the country's tourism is based on the propaganda in 
foreign countries and the economic development of neighboring China.  
In 2021, I moved to Hachiouji City, Toukyou, and took a tourism-
related job ( Department of Tourism Science ).

( For ski resorts, see Kucchan Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

( Single of the chapter ; ``Piano'' by Spangle Call Lilli Line.  -->
  http://youtu.be/bqkFlWWcvW0 )

|                  |
|    KIMOBETSU     |
|                  |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 26  |
 ------------------
AREA 190 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 159, CULTIVATED 10 ; COUNTY = ABUTA ; POP 
3,240 ( 1990 ), 2,490 ( 2010 ) ; MEAN ANNUAL TEMP 5.7 C ( AV 1981-
2010 ), PRECIPI 1,150 MM, SNOW 1,102 CM ( AV 1982-2010 ) ; COUNTRY SIGN 
= ASPARAGUS & MT YOUTEI.  

( UTF code ; Abuta = \u867B\u7530. )

LOCATION  
 
Kimobetsu ( ki-mo-be-tsu ) is a town in Shiribeshi general sub-
prefecture, central Hokkaidou ( ``dou-ou'' ).  The area is located in 
the upper reaches of the Shiribetsu River.  ( The river's source is 
located in neighboring Date City. )

( UTF code ; Kimobetsu = \u559C\u8302\u5225. )

Mt. Shiribetsu-dake ( 1,107 meters ) is located in the south.  Mt. 
Youtei-zan ( 1,898 meters ) is located in the west.  Both are cone-
shaped mountains.  #1
 
NAME  
  
The name ``Kimobetsu'' comes from ``kim-o-pet'' in Ainu language, which
means ``mountain, exist, river.''  Or, it comes from ``kim-un-pet'' in 
Ainu language, which means ``mountain, enter, river.''  Both signified 
the Kimobetsu River.  

The Kimobetsu River originates from Mt. Kimobetsu-dake ( 1,177 
meters ), which is located on the border between Kimobetsu Town and 
Sapporo City.  The river flows near Nakayama Pass and meets the 
Shiribetsu River in the center of Kimobetsu Town.

HISTORY    

From 1870 ( Meiji 3 ) to 1871 ( Meiji 4 ), Higashi Honganji Temple in 
Kyouto Prefecture constructed the Honganji Road by order of the Meiji 
government.  The Honganji Road connected Osaru-betsu in the Pacific 
Ocean side and Hiragushi in the Sea of Japan side. 

( Osaru-betsu was located in what is now Date City, Iburi general sub-
  prefecture.  Hiraguishi is located in Sapporo City, Ishikari sub-
  prefecture. ) 

In 1871 ( Meiji 4 ), Kimobetsu-ekitei ( a post house ) was built along 
the Honganji Road.  In 1902 ( Meiji 35 ), a group of settlers from 
the Nanbu region migrated.  They were called Nanbu-dantai.  ( The Nanbu 
region extended from northern Iwate Prefecture to eastern Aomori 
Prefecture. )  #2

In 1917 ( Taishou 6 ), Kimobetsu Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established.  The area was separated from Makkari Village ( now Rusutsu 
Village ).  In 1952 ( Shouwa 27 ), Kimobetsu Town was established.  

( See the note of Matsumae Town for 2nd-class municipality. ) 

TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC  

The town hall of Kimobetsu is located near the intersection of Route 
230 ( Nakayama-kokudou ) and Route 276 ( Shiribetsu-kokudou ).  Route 
230 runs along the Kimobetsu River and passes Nakayama Pass.  Nakayama 
Pass separates Kimobetsu Town from Sapporo City.  

Route 276 runs along the Shiribetsu River and enters Ootaki Village 
( now Date City ).  Formerly, JNR ( Japanese National Railways ) Iburi 
Line ran along this national highway.  #3  #4 

INDUSTRY  
 
Distinctive industry in Kimobetsu is agriculture.  STATISTICS OF 
AGRICULTURE = beans ( soybeans 60 hectares in 2015, adzuki beans 124, 
other beans 2 ), sugar beets 89, broccoli 56, pasture 50, fodder 13, 
melons 8, tomatoes 5, cabbages 4, flowers 1, water melons 1, 

( continue ) --> other industrial crops 5, potatoes X ( concealed ), 
other vegetables X ( concealed, including asparagus ), and paddy rice - 
( zero ) ; dairy cows X ( concealed ) in 2015. 
 
Asparagus cultivation and canned asparagus production have continued in 
Kimobetsu Town since before the war.  Cradle Kounou Co. produces canned 
asparagus.  #5
  
As for sightseeing, the roadside station ``Bouyou-nakayama'' is located 
on Nakayama Pass.  The name ``Bouyou'' is Japanese, which means 
``looking Mt. Youtei-zan.''  When we head west along National Route 230 
from Sapporo and cross Nakayama Pass, we'll see Mt. Youtei-zan ahead.

( For landscape along National Route 230, see Minami Ward in Sapporo 
  City, Ishikari sub-prefecture. )

The specialty of Bouyou-nakayama roadside station is ``ague-imo.''  
It's a potato corn dog.  The roadside station sells more than 400,000 
``ague-imo'' a year.  ( ``ague-imo'' --> http://
ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%81%82%E3%81%92%E3%81%84%E3%82%82 )  #6  #7
 
NOTE 

#1

Mt. Shiribetsu-dake is an old volcano that was active between 130,000 
and 50,000 years ago.  In geological chronology, this period is 
included in Quaternary.  Mt. Shiribetsu-dake is a strato-volcano.  A 
strato-volcano is a conical volcano built up by layers of lava and 
tephra. 

( In Japanese ; ``Quaternary'' is ``dai-yon-ki.''  ``Strato-volcano'' 
  is ``seisou-kazan.'' ) 
 
( For geological chronology for excursion, see Nihei lab 2024-2026_e. ) 

Mt. Youtei-zan is a young volcano, whose eruption occurred 1,000 years 
ago.  In geological chronology, this period is also included in 
Quarternary.  Mt. Youtei-zan is also a strato-volcano.  This mountain 
is called Ezo Fuji because the shape resembles Mt. Fuji.  ( Mt. Fuji is 
also a strato-volcano. )
 
In Ainu language, Mt. Shiribetsu-dake was called Pin-ne-shiri, which 
means ``man, be, mountain.''  Mt. Youtei-zan was called Mat-ne-shiri, 
which means ``woman, be, mountain.''
 
According to the Map of Geospatial Information Authority of Japan 
( Chiriin-chizu ), Mt. Pin-ne-shiri ( man, be, mountain ) exists in 
three locations ; 

( 1 ) Between Shin-totsukawa Town in Sorachi general sub-prefecture and 
Toubetsu Town in Ishikari sub-prefecture.  ( 2 ) In Samani Town, Hidaka 
sub-prefecture.  ( 3 ) In Nakatonbetsu Town, Souya general sub-
prefecture. 

As for Mat-ne-siri ( woman, be, mountain ), Mt. Machine-yama is located 
between Shin-totsukawa Town and Toubetsu Town.  It's adjacent to Mt. 
Pin-ne-shiri.  Mt. Matsune-shiri-dake is located in Naka-tonbetsu Town. 
It's also adjacent to Mt. Pin-ne-shiri.
 
Also, there are Mt. O-akan-dake and Mt. Me-akan-dake in Kushiro City, 
Kushiro general sub-prefecture.  They are included in Akan-mashuu 
National Park.  

The name ``O-akan-dake'' means ``man, Akan, mountain'' in Japanese.  
The name ``Me-akan-dake'' means ``woman, Akan, mountain.''  These names 
come from ``akam-pin-ne-sir'' ( wheel, man, be, mountain ) and ``akam-
mat-ne-sir'' ( wheel, woman, be, mountain ) in Ainu language.  Wheels 
refers to a pair. 
  
( For Mt. Pin-ne-shiri, see Shin-totsukawa Town in Sorachi general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For Mt. Youtei-zan, see Makkari Village in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )
 
I lived in Tsukuba Ctiy, Ibaraki Prefecture, for 20 years.  Mt. Tsukuba 
stands in the city.  The mountain has twin peaks, Nantai-san ( 871 
meters ) and Nyotai-san ( 877 meters ).  The name ``Nan-tai-san'' means 
``man, body, mountain'' in Japanese.  The name ``Nyo-tai-san'' means 
``woman, body, mountain.''  They sound sensual.
 
#2  

There are grasshopper mounds in Rusan district in Kimobetsu Town and 
also in Teusu district in Kyougoku Town.  A booklet states the 
following.  ( by  Kimobetsu Town Education Board ( Kimobetsu-chou-
kyouiku-iinkai ) 2019.  ``Histric Site Guide Book ( Shiseki-gaido-
bukku ).'' ) 

( In Japanese ; ``Grasshopper mound'' is ``batta-zuka.'' )
 
``A large outbreak of migratory locusts ( `tonosama-batta' ) occurred 
  in the Tokachi Plain from 1880 ( Meiji 13 ) to 1884 ( Meiji 17 ).  
  The hordes ( ho'r-z ) of migratory locusts went west through the 
  Hidaka mountains and reached to sub-prefectures of Ishikari, Iburi, 
  Hidaka, and Shiribeshi.  

  Farmland and pastures changed from green to brown.  To protect 
  agriculture in Hokkaidou Island from the locust plague, and to 
  prevent the hordes of migratory locusts flew to Honshuu Island, the 
  Hokkaidou Development Commission payed 200,000 yen.  ( The value was 
  today's 900,000,000 - 2,000,000,000 yen ).  
 
  The Hokkaidou Development Commission bought adult and larval locust, 
  killed them, and buried them.  Thus, grasshopper mounds were made.'' 
  
In the west from the Hidaka Mountains, other grasshopper mounds can be 
found in ( 1 ) Teine-yamaguchi district of Teine Ward, Sapporo City, 
Ishikari sub-prefecture and in ( 2 ) Date City, Iburi general sub-
prefecture.  In the East from the Hidaka Mountains, grasshopper mounds 
exist in ( 3 ) Shintoku Town in Tokachi general sub-prefecture. 

Grasshopper mounds are designated as Historic Site ( Shiseki ).  The 
Kanji characters for grasshopper ( ``batta'' ) means ``flying, insect 
emperor.'' 

( UTF code ; ``batta'' = \u98db\u8757. )

( For grasshopper mound, see Shintoku Town in Tokachi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

#3

There are two gas stations in Kimobetsu Town ( as of 2013 ).  They are 
ENEOS ( the former Nipporn Oil Corporation ) in the built-up area and 
Hokuren ( Hokkaidou's federation of agricultural cooperatives ) at the 
intersection of National Route 230 and National Route 276.   

In August 2013, I went on a short trip by motorbike.  The route was 
Sapporo - Lake Shikotsu - Kimobetsu - Nakayama Pass - Sapporo.  I took 
National Route 276 along Lake Shikotsu and entered Kimobetsu Town from 
Hiroshima Pass.  National Route 276 is a beautiful highway.

Hiroshima Bridge spans the upstream of the Shiribetsu River.  Near the 
bridge, a 660 cc mini truck appeared from a farm.  An old woman drove 
it.  I followed her on a Kawasaki 650 cc.  She drove slowly and entered 
a Hokuren gas station.  The distance was 11 kilometers.

It may be a long distance to the nearest gas station in Hokkaidou.  
Visitors and new residents need to know.
 
#4  

The Iburi Line connected Date City and Kucchan Town.  In Kimobetsu 
town, there were stations of Misono, Kita-suzukawa, Kimobetsu, and 
Rusan.  The train went along National Route 276 and Prefectural Route 
695. 

( For the Iburi Line, see Kyougoku Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

Hirafu Shrine is located along National Route 276, near Rusan Bridge.  
The name ``Hirafu'' signifies General Hirafu Abe in the 7th century.  
He is said to have occupied this region.
 
( For the name ``Rusan,'' see Rusutsu Village in Shiribeshi general 
  sub-prefecture. ) 

( For the name ``Hirafu,'' see Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture. )
  
#5
 
As for asparagus cultivation in Kimobetsu Town, the following is a 
machine translation of T. Okudaira's text ( by T. Okudaira et al. 
2007 ).  ( The translation was made on this web-site in August 2026 ; 
http://duckduckgo.com/?q=translate&t=h_&ia=web )

``Located on the southeastern slope of Mount Yotei, the ground 
  temperature rises quickly after snowmelt, and the soil is rough and 
  highly permeable.  As a result, asparagus cultivation began in 1929, 
  and soon after, a canning factory was established, marking a 
  remarkable development.''

#6   
 
Nakayama Pass on National Route 230 separates Kimobetsu Town from 
Sapporo City.  The altitude is 835 meters.  When we hear news of 
``Snow at Nakayama Pass,'' winter approaches.  People in Sapporo City 
change tires.  

There is a ski ground at Nakayama Pass.  The name is Nakayama-touge-
sukii-jou.  It opens in early winter and spring.  The months are 
November, December, April, and May.  Many other ski resorts operate 
from December to March. 

National Route 230 runs along the former Honganji Road.  The most 
difficult section of the road was Nakayama Pass.  Even after the road 
became a national highway, it was called ``The Devilish Mountain Road''
in the 1960s, because vehicles plunging off the road.

( In Japanese ; ``The Devilish Mountain Road'' is Ma-no-yama-michi. )

In July 1992, I traveled around Hokkaidou by motorbike.  At the time, I 
was an undergraduate student.  I disembarked at the Port of Muroran.  
On the second day, I crossed Nakayama Pass.  It was raining.  I fell 
off my Yamaha 400 cc. 
 
#7

As for sightheeing, there is Rusutsu Golf Resort in Fujimi-dai district 
in Kimobetsu Town.  The name is ``Rusutsu'' even though the location is 
Kimobetsu.  This is because the golf course is part of Rusutsu Resort 
in neighboring Rusutsu Village. 

Fujimi-dai district is near the center of Kimobetsu Town.  The name 
means ``Mt. Fuji, view, plateau'' in Japanese.  The word ``Mt. Fuji'' 
in this case means ``Mt. Youtei-zan,''  whose another name is Ezo-fuji.
 
According to the book ``Kimobetsu Town History ( Kimobetsu-chou-shi )'' 
published in 1969, a large farm opened in Fujimi-dai district.  It was 
at the end of the Meiji period ( around the 1910s ).  Tenant farmers in 
the farm cultivated Dalmatian chrysanthemums.  

( In Japanese ; ``Dalmatian chrysanthemum'' is ``jochuu-guiku.'' )  

Dalmatian chrysanthemums were processed into insecticide.  The 
insecticide was exported.  The harvest of Dalmatian chrysanthemums was 
poor in the Fujimi-dai district, because the place is high and cold.  
Many tenant farmers left. 

( Single of the chapter ; ``Lorenzo ( For Lorenzo Labissier )'' by 
  Canaro.  --> http://youtu.be/UvjoaacZLpw )

|                  |
|     KYOUGOKU     |
|                  |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 27  |
 ------------------
AREA 232 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 195, CULTIVATED 22 ; COUNTY = ABUTA ; POP 
3,775 ( 1990 ), 3,811 ( 2010 ) ( + ) ; COUNTRY SIGN = FUKIDASHI PARK & 
MT YOUTEI.  
 
( UTF code ; Abuta = \u867B\u7530. )
 
LOCATION

Kyougoku ( kyou-go-ku ) is a town in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture, 
central Hokkaidou ( ``dou-ou'' ).  Kyougoku is located in the middle 
and upper reaches of the Shiribetsu River.  ( The source of the river 
is in neighboring Date City, Iburi general sub-prefecture. )  

( UTF code ; Kyougoku = \u4EAC\u6975. ) 

The Shiribetsu River is known as a clean river.  Mt. Youtei-zan ( 1,898 
meters ) stands in the west of the town.  The east is the border with 
Sapporo City.  #1

NAME  

The name ``Kyougoku'' comes from Viscount ( va'i-count ) Takanori 
Kyougoku, who was the lord of Marugame Domain in Kagawa Prefecture.  In 
1897 ( Meiji 30 ), he established Kyougoku Farm.  The area was 800 
hectares.  #2

( In Japanese ; ``Viscount'' is ``shishaku.'' ) 

( For large farm in the Meiji period, see Niki Town in Shiribeshi 
  general sub-prefecture. ) 

HISTORY   

In 1910 ( Meiji 43 ), Higashi-kucchan Village ( 2nd-class 
municipality ) was established.  This was separated from Kucchan 
Village.  In 1923 ( Taishou 12 ), Higashi-kucchan Village ( 1st-class 
municipality ) was established. 

( UTF code ; Higashi-kucchan = \u6771\u5036\u77E5\u5B89. )

In 1938 ( Shouwa 13 ), Kyougoku Farm was opened up to tenant farmers.  
In 1940 ( Shouwa 15 ), Kyougoku Village was established.  It was 
renamed from Higashi-kucchan.  In 1962 ( Shouwa 37 ), Kyougoku Town was 
established. 

( For 1st-class and 2nd-class municipalities, see the note of Matsumae 
  Town. ) 
 
TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC
 
The town hall of Kyougoku is located along the Shiribetsu River, which 
flows around the eastern foot of Mt. Youtei-zan.  The rivers of Pe-e-
pe-nai and Wakka-ta-sappu meet the Shiribetsu River near the town hall.

National Route 276 ( Shiribetsu-kokudou ) passes near the town hall.  
Formerly, the Iburi Line of JNR ( Japanese National Railways ) ran 
along the national highway.  The railway connected Date-monbetsu 
Station in Date City and Kucchan Station in Kucchan Town.  #3
 
INDUSTRY 

Distinctive industries in Kyougoku are upland farming, sightseeing, and 
power generation.  STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = beans ( soybeans 223 
hectares in 2015, adzuki beans 284, other beans 5 ), wheat 352, 

( continue ) --> sugar beets 202, pasture 162, carrots 148, fodder 21, 
onions 19, buckwheat 14, broccoli 2, other vegetables 22+X, and paddy 
rice X ( concealed ) ; dairy cows X head in 2015.  There is no data for 
fisheries, but there is a salmon hatchery in the Shiribeshi River.  #4

( In Japanese ; ``Upland farming'' is ``hatasaku.'' )

The water of Kyougoku is famous.  Fukidashi Park is located at the foot 
of Mt. Youtei-zan.  The park has been designated as one of 100 Best 
Waters since 1985 by the Ministry of Environment.  ( The Ministry of 
Environment ( Kankyou-shou ) was Environment Agency ( Kankyou-chou ) 
between 1971 and 2001. )

Some coffee shops in Sapporo City use Kyougoku's water.  Seicomart 
sells Kyougoku water and ice.  ( Seicomart is a convenience store 
chain in Hokkaidou. )  Kyogoku water is very soft.  The hardness is 23 
mg/L.  #5 

( For moderately hard water ( 105 mg/L ), see Kuromatsnai town in 
  Shiribeshi sub-prefecture. )

NOTE  

#1
 
Mt. Youtei-zan is one of the 100 Mountains in the country.  The summit 
marks a border among five municipalities ; Kucchan Town, Niseko Town, 
Makkari Village, Kimobetsu Town, and Kyougoku Town. 

Kyougoku Town is adjacent to Sapporo City, but there is no road leading 
to Sapporo.  The border between Kyougoku and Sapporo is Mt. Muine-
yama ( 1,464 meters ).  

There is no name for the mountain range that separates Sapporo from 
these municipalities to the west ; Akaigawa, Kyougoku, Kimobetsu, and 
Otaru.  I call it the Sapporo Mountains ( Sapporo-sanchi ). 

#2 
 
In the Meiji period, large farms were established in Hokkaidou.  Many 
of the landowners were members of the noble family, former feudal 
loads, or members of the imperial family. 

( In Japanese ; ``Feudal load'' is ``han-shu.''  ``Imperial family'' is 
  ``kouzoku.''  ``Noble family'' is ``kazoku.'' )

These are examples ;  
( 1 ) Imperial Ranch, located in Shin-hidaka Town, Hidaka sub-
prefecture ; established in 1872 ; 66,000 hectares.
 
This became Niikappu Ranch managed by Edwin Dun in 1877, whose area 
was 6,800 hectares.  Then, it was divided into National Livestock 
Breeding Center, Niikappu Ranch, whose area is 2,040 hectares, and 
into Hokkaidou University Ranch ( Hokkaidou University Shizunai 
Livestock Farm ), whose area is 470 hectares.

( In Japanease ; ``Imperial Ranch'' is Goryou-bokujou.  ``Hokkaidou 
  University Ranch'' is Hokkaidou-daigaku-shizunai-kenkyuu-bokujou.  
  ``National Livestock Breeding Center, Niikappu Ranch'' is Kachiku-
  kairyou-sentaa-niikappu-bokujou.  ``Niikappu Ranch'' in the Meiji 
  period is Niikappu-bokuba-jou. )

( For Hokkaidou University Ranch, see the section of Hokkaidou 
  University Ranch in Shin-hidaka Town, Hidaka sub-prefecture. )

( 2 ) Tokugawa Farm, located in Yakumo Town, Oshima general sub-
prefecture ; established in 1879 ; 496 hectares.  ( The Edo era was the 
era of the Tokugawa Shogunate. ) 

( 3 ) Kurokawa-mouri Farm, located in Yoich Town and Niki Town, 
Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture ; established in 1881 ; 700 hectares.

( Kurokawa-mouri Farm was opened by Tei-ichi Awaya, who was the pioneer 
  of Ooe village ( now Niki Town ).  He came from the former Choushuu 
  Domain ( now Yamaguchi Prefecture ).  The load of the Choushuu Domain 
  was the Mouri clan, so the farm was named ``Mouri.''  ``Kurokawa'' is 
  a place name in Yoichi.  It was the former Kurokawa village.  

  The name ``Kurokawa'' came from Kiyoataka Kuro-da and Shiguehiro 
  Mune-kawa.  Kiyotaka Kuroda was the undersecretary of the Hokkaidou 
  Development Commission.  Shiguehiro Munekawa was the leader of 
  samurai migrants from the former Aizu Domain, Fukushima Prefecture. )

( UTF code ; Kurokawa-mouri = \u9ED2\u5DDD\u6BDB\u522.  Shiguehiro 
  Munekawa = \u5B97\u5DDD\u8302\u53CB. )

( 4 ) Hashiguchi Farm, located in Rusutsu Village, Shiribeshi general 
sub-prefecture ; established in 1883 ; 595 hectares.

( 5 ) Maeda Farm, located in Kyouwa Town, Shiribeshi general sub-
prefecture ; established in 1884 ; 2,500 hectares.

( 6 ) Uryuu Farm, located in what are now Uryuu Town, Hokuryuu Town, 
Numata Town, and Fukagawa City, Sorachi general sub-prefecture ; 
established in 1889 ; 49,500 hectares.  This farm was made under the 
Hokkaidou Land Grant Act.

( In Japanese ; ``The Hokkaidou Land Grant Act'' is Hokkaidou-tochi-
  haraisague-kisoku. ) 

( For the Hokkaidou Land Grant Act, see Uryuu Town in Sorachi general 
  sub-prefecture. ) 

( 7 ) Maeda Farm, located in Teine Ward, Sapporo City, Ishikari sub- 
prefecture ; established in 1894 ; 2,109 hectares in the early 1910s.

( 8 ) Kyougoku Farm, located in Kyougoku Town, Shiribeshi general sub-
prefecture ; established in 1897 ; 800 hectares.

These large farms were run by absentee landlords.  The situation was 
resemble to Brazil.  In Brazil, large farms ( fazendas ), which have 
several thousands of hectares, are run by absentee landlords ( by H. 
Maruyama et al. 2009.  Traditional management of fazenda and its 
problems.  ``Chiri-kuukan'' 2, 99-132. )

In the first half of the 20th century, these large farms in Hokkaidou 
Island were released to tenant farmers.  Although small in scale, 
tenant farmers acquired independent management.

In Japan, including Hokkaidou, most farms are run by farmers who live 
on the site.  While in Brazil, absentee landlords continue to operate 
large farms.  Brazil has been an exporter of agricultural products.   

* * *

Besides these large farms in Hokkaidou, former feudal clans managed 
administrative regions.  For example, the Inada clan from Tokushima 
Prefecture managed an area what is now Shizunai district in Shin-hidaka 
Town, Hidaka sub-prefecture.  

The Date ( da'-te ) clan from Miyagui Prefecture managed areas what are 
now Date City in Iburi general sub-prefecture and Toubetsu Town in 
Ishikari sub-prefecture. 

#3

National Route 276 is the only national highway in Kyougoku Town.  The 
Iburi Line used to run along it.  There were stations of Minami-
kyougoku, Higashi-kyougoku, Kyougoku, and Kitaoka. 

The name Kitaoka is Japanese.  Kitaoka district is located in a hill 
( ``oka'' ) to the north ( ``kita'' ) of Kyougoku Town.  As for 
Higashi-kyougoku, the Kanji characters resemble to Toukyou.

( UTF code ; Higashi-kyougoku = \u6771\u4EAC\u6975. )
 
The river name ``Wakka-ta-sappu'' means ``drinking water, to, come'' in 
Ainu language.  In the upstream of the Wakka-ta-sappu River, Kucchan 
Iron Mine was in operation between 1916 and 1969.  The iron mine was 
also called Wakikata Mine.  The name was derived from Wakka-ta. 

Kyougoku Light Railway was in operation between 1919 and 1920.  It was 
built to transport iron ore ( limonite ( la'imo-nait ) ) to the 
steelworks in Muroran Ward ( now Muroran City, Iburi general sub-
prefecture ).  

The light railway connected Wakikata Station and Kucchan Station on the 
Hakodate Main Line.  Later, it became the JNR Iburi Line and connected 
Kucchan Station and Date-monbetsu Station on the Muroran Main Line. 
 
( For the Iburi Line, see Soubetsu Town, Date City, Kimobetsu Town, and 
  Kucchan Town in Iburi general sub-prefecture. ) 

Kai district is located in the upstream of the Pe-e-pe-nai River.  The 
area was developed by migrants from Yamanashi Prefecture ( former Kai 
province ).  Adjacent to Kai district in Kyougoku Town is Yamanashi 
district in Kucchan Town.  

Today, there are no residents in Kai and Yamanashi districts.  The land 
that migrants had cleared returned to forest.
 
In Kai district, there is a round lake.  It looks like a caldera lake. 
But, it's an artificial lake.  The map of the Geospacial Information 
Authority of Japan labels it as the Kyougoku Underground Power Plant
( http://maps.gsi.go.jp/#15/42.957710/140.948882/  last accessed in 
June 2019 ).

#4  
 
As for rivers in Kyougoku Town, the Kanbetsu Power Station on the 
Shiribetsu River started generating electricity in 1924.  It's the 
oldest existing hydroelectric dam in Hokaidou. 

( UTF code ; Kanbetsu = \u5BD2\u5225. ) 

The Kyougoku Hydroelectric Power Plant on the Pe-e-pe-nai River started 
generating electricity in 2014.  It's a pumped-storage power plant.  
The reservoir is the Kyougoku Underground Power Plant and looks like a 
caldera ( see Note No. 3 above ). 

The downstream of the Kyougoku Hydroelectric Power Plant is Futaba Dam 
on the Pe-e-pe-nai River.  It was built from 1966 to 1987 for 
irrigation.  The name ``Pe-e-pe-nai'' comes from ``pe-pe-nai'' in Ainu 
language, which means ``water, water, river.''  It refers to a river 
with abundant water. 

( For ``pe-pe-nai,'' see Toyoura Town in Iburi general sub-
  prefecture. )

#5  

Fukidashi Park is a roadside station.  Tourists get off tour buses, 
holding empty plastic bottles to collect the water.  The water is soft 
and mellow.  It lasts for a long time without growing algae. 
 
( Except for facilities such as Fukidashi Park, spring water and river 
  water in Hokkaidou are not suitable for drinking.  There is a risk of 
  infection with echinococcus.  Spring water and river water should be 
  filtered. )

Near Fukidashi Park, Kyougoku Hot Spring opened in 1996.  It's a day-
trip bathing facility.  Professor Tooru Ikeda of the Faculty of 
Letters, Hokkaidou University, recommended it.  Also, Kyougoku's 
buckwheat noodles and Toufu ( soybean curd ) are good because the water 
is good. 

( Single of the chapter ; ``An'' by Spangle Call Lilli Line.  -->
  http://youtu.be/HvmCpKTjTCA )

|                  |
|     KUCCHAN      |
|                  |
|  MAP 2 - NO. 28  |
 ------------------
AREA 261 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 191, CULTIVATED 45 ; COUNTY = ABUTA ; POP 
18,030 ( 1990 ), 15,568 ( 2010 ) ; MEAN ANNUAL TEMP 7.0 C ( AV 1981-
2010 ), PRECIPI 1,477 MM, SNOW 1,062 CM ; COUNTRY SIGN = SKIING POTATO. 
#1

( UTF code ; Abuta = \u867B\u7530. )

LOCATION 

Kucchan ( ku-ccha-n ) is a town in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture, 
central Hokkaidou ( ``dou-ou'' ).  ( The official spelling is 
``Kutchan.'' )  The Shiribetsu River and the Kutosan River flow.  

The area extends from the north slope of Mt. Niseko-an-nupuri to the 
north slope of Mt. Youtei-zan.  There is Half Moon Lake on the foot of 
the Mt. Youtei-zan.  #2  

( The elevation of Mt. Niseko-an-nupuri is 1,308 meters.  Mt. Youtei-
  zan is 1,898 meters.  In Japanese, ``Half Moon Lake'' is called 
  hanguetsu-ko. )

( UTF code ; Kucchan = \u5036\u77E5\u5B89.  Kutosan = 
  \u5036\u767B\u5C71. )
 
NAME   

The name ``Kucchan'' comes from ``kut-sam-un-pet'' in Ainu language, 
which means ``cliff, side, exist, river.''  Or, it comes from ``kut-
san-i'' in Ainu language, which means ``belt, flow, thing.''  Both 
signified the Kutosan River, which meets the Shiribetsu River near 
the town hall.

HISTORY

In 1892 ( Meiji 25 ), the Colonial Land Division was laid out in 
Kucchan wilderness ( Kucchan-guen-ya ).  The land was delimited like 
a chessboard.  The migrants who had developed Yoichi and Niki villages 
moved to Kucchan.  

Then, migrant groups, including San-in Immigrant Company, moved from 
Honshuu Island to Kucchan.  San-in Migrant Comapny recruited migrnats 
from Shimane and Tottori prefectures.

( In Japanese ; ``Migrant group'' is ``ijuu-dantai.''  ``San-in Migrant 
  Company'' is San-in-ijuu-kaisha.  ``The Colonial Land Division'' is 
  Shokuminchi-kukaku. )
 
( For the Colonial Land Division, see Shin-totsukawa Town in Sorachi 
  general sub-prefecture ; Makkari Village in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )
 
In 1896 ( Meiji 29 ), the administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) 
of Kucchan was established.  In 1904 ( Meiji 37 ), the Hakodate Main 
Line opened.  

( UTF code ; ``kochou-yakuba'' = \u6238\u9577\u5F79\u5834. )
 
In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), Kucchan Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was 
established.  In 1910 ( Meiji 43 ), Shiribeshi sub-prefectural bureau 
was established.  Kucchan was selected because it was located in the 
center of the area, although Otaru Ward ( now Otaru City ) along the 
coast had the largest population.
 
In 1915 ( Taishou 14 ), Kucchan Village ( 1st-class municipality ) was 
established.  In 1916 ( Taishou 5 ), Kucchan Town was established.  #3

( For 1st-class and 2nd-class municipalities, see the note of Matsumae 
  Town )

TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC 

The town hall of Kucchan is located between the Shiribetsu River and 
the Kutosan River.  The Hakodate Main Line, National Route 5, 
( Youtei-kokudou ), Route 276 ( Shiribetsu-kokudou ), and Route 393 
( Akaigawa-kokudou ) pass near the town hall.  #4

Formerly, the Iburi Line of JNR ( Japanese National Railways ) ran 
along Route 276.  The railway was closed in 1986.  Along National Route 
5, there is a statue of Theodor von Lerch ( le'-ru-hi ).  #5  #6

Kucchan is a town of government offices.  There are Shiribeshi general 
sub-prefecture bureau and local offices of national and prefectural 
governments. 
 
There are two high schools ; Kucchan High School and Kucchan 
Agricultural High School.  At the agricultural high school, Atsushi 
Satou, who graduated from Faculty of Letters, Hokkaidou University, 
taught geography.  He also instructed the baseball team.

INDUSTRY   

Distinctive industries in Kucchan are sightseeing and agriculture. 
STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = potatoes 1,137 hectares in 2015, beans 
( adzuki beans 718, soybeans 291, other beans 8 ), wheat 457, sugar 
beets 434, paddy rice 229, pasture 215, fodder 97, buckwheat 93, 

( continue ) --> onions 37, carrots 26, cabbages 3, melons 3, yams 3, 
broccoli 2, cucumbers 1, radishes 1, spinach 1, tomatoes 1, other 
vegetable 67+X ( sweet corn, pumpkins, asparagus ), and other grains 
11 ; dairy cows 797 head in 2015 and hens 25,000. 

As for sightseeing, two ski resorts are located ; Grand Hirafu Ski 
Resort and Hanazono Ski Resort.  These are two of the five ski resorts 
on Mt. Niseko-an-nupuri.  The others are Niseko Village, Niseko 
International, and Moiwa in Niseko Town. 

( For other ski resorts on Mt. Niseko-an-nupuri, see Niseko Town in 
  Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture. ) 
 
The foot of Grand Hirafu Ski Resort is Yamada district ( now Niseko-
hirafu district ).  In winter, the area is like a foreign country.  
Tourists come from Australia and China.  Condominiums and hotels are 
managed by foreign companies. Convenience stores sell products in 
English.  #7  #8  #9

NOTE  
 
#1

Kucchan's country sign ( gateway sign ) features a skiing potato.  The 
potato is round and bumpy.  It's ``danshaku'' potato.  ``Danshaku'' is 
a major potato cultivar for cooking.  In Hokkaidou, the cultivated area 
of ``danshaku'' is the second largest among potatoes.  ( The largest is 
``kona-fubuki,'' which is a cultivar for processing. )
  
``Danshaku'' is cultivated mainly in ( 1 ) the Tokachi Plains, ( 2 ) 
Okhotsk general sub-prefecture, and ( 3 ) municipalities around Mt. 
Youtei-zan, including Kucchan and Kyougoku towns.  Another major 
potato cultivar for cooking is May-queen.  It's long and has a smooth 
surface.

( For ``danhaku'' potato, see Imakane Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. )  

( For May-queen potato, see Assabu Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. )  

( For potato, see Shihoro Town in Tokachi general sub-prefecture. ) 

The word ``danshaku'' means ``baron'' in Japanese.  In 1908 ( Meiji 
41 ), Baron Ryoukichi Kawata imported potatoes from the seed company 
Sutton & Sons in England.  One of the potato varieties was called 
``danshaku.''

Baron Kawata was the senior managing director of Hakodate Dock ( a 
shipbuilding company ).  Later, the name of the potato was clarified.  
Danshaku is the American cultivar ``Irish Cobbler.''

The flavor of ``danshaku'' potatoes improve when stored, although 
Japanese people like new potatoes ( ``shin-jaga'' ).  British people 
make Christmas cakes several months before Christmas.  In Japan, the 
expiration ( expa-ra'tion ) date of a Christmas cake is one or two 
days.  

Preserving potatoes is more difficult than grains, because potatoes 
contain a high amount of water.  Grains are dried for storage.  
Potatoes originate from the highlands of South America.  Native 
Americans ( Indios ) freeze, dry, and preserve potatoes.  The potatoes 
are ``chuno'' ( chu'u-nho ).  

( UTF code ; ``chuno'' = chu\u00F1o. )

#2  

The Shiribeshi River is a clean river.  I wonder why the river is 
clean.  It flows through the built-up areas, agricultural fields, and 
ski resorts. 

( For clean river, see Rankoshi Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

The Shiribeshi River is used for tourism in Kucchan Town.  The water is 
divided into a canoe area and a rafting area.  The rafting area 
includes whitewater rafting tours in the spring.  

Mt. Iwao-nupuri ( 1,116 meters, volcano ) is located next to Mt. 
Niseko-an-nupuri.  On Mt. Iwao-nupuri, a sulfur mine existed from the 
late Edo era to the Meiji period. 

Mitsui Co. devised a steam sulfur refining method in 1886 and increased 
sulfur production on Mt. Iwao-nupuri.  The company opened other sulfur 
mines in Hokkaidou.  Mitsui became the huge conglomerate 
( ``zaibatsu'' ) before World War II. 

The sulfur of Mt. Iwao-nupuri was shipped to the Port of Iwanai, then 
to Hyougo Prefecture.  Factories manufactured matches from sulfur, and 
the matches were exported abroad from the Port of Koube.  Today, sulfur 
is refined from petroleum.  ( Petroleum is manufactured from imported 
crude oil. ) 

( For match stick production, see Kami-furano Town in Kamikawa general 
  sub-prefecture. ) 

( For sulfur production, see Furano City and Kami-furano Town in 
  Kamikawa general sub-prefecture ; Shari Town in Okhotsk general sub-
  prefecture ; Teshikaga Town in Kushiro general sub-prefecture. ) 

#3
 
According to the book ``Niseko Town History ( Niseko-chou-shi ) 
published in 1982, the American pilot Art Smith exhibited aerobatics in 
the summer of 1917 ( Taishou 6 ).  His moves included the roll 
( ``outen''), the upside down ( ``gyakuten''), the leaf drop 
( ``konoha-otoshi'' ), and the corkscrew ( ``kiruku-nuki'' ).   

In the novel `` The Razor's Edge'' by Somerset Maugham, published in 
1944, there is an expression about flying, ``In the air, way up, I felt 
that I was part of something great and beautiful.''  

When I visited Brazil in August 2001, I flew in a small plane, and felt 
the same way.
HTTP://2242.MOOO.COM/POSI/01BR3/7591.JPG
HTTP://2242.MOOO.COM/POSI/01BR3/7606.JPG

( For aerobatic, see Kita Ward, Sapporo City, Ishikari sub-
  prefecture. )

#4

Young people moved to Kucchan Town from all over the country.  They 
started new businesses.  Around 50 new shops opened, including cafes, 
cake shops, snowboard shops, and nail salons.  Also, Chinese 
restaurants were opened by chefs, who came down from the mountains 
( ski resort hotels ). 

The center of the town is located at the foot of Mt. Niseko-an-nupuri.  
The terrain is a slope.  In winter, snow is pushed into roadside 
ditches and washed away.  Poles hung from utility poles are used to 
remove snow accumulated in ditches.

I visited Kucchan Town on October 21st, 2012.  It snowed in the 
mountains.  An town hall official said, ``The temperature was 30 
degrees Celsius on September 23rd.'' 

#5  
  
Major Lerch ( Theodor von Lerch ) from Austria introduced skiing to 
Japan.  In January 1911, he taught skiing at the army in Takada Town 
( now Takada City ), Niigata Prefecture.  From February to March in 
1912, he taught skiing at the army in Asahikawa Town ( now Asahkawa 
City ), Hokkaidou Prefecture.  

In April 1912, he climbed Mt. Youtei-zan on skies and descended.  The 
mountain skiing was featured repeatedly in the Otaru Shinbun 
( newspaper ).  Then, Japanese people started mountain skiing.  

( For Mt. Youtei-zan, see Makkari Village in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

There are three statues of Major Lerch ; ( 1 ) Along National Route 5 
in Kucchan Town, Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture ; ( 2 ) At Asahikawa 
Airport in Higashi-kagura Town, Kamikawa general sub-prefecture ; ( 3 ) 
At Moiwayama Shrine on Mt. Moiwa in Sapporo City, Ishikari sub-
prefecture.  He is enshrined as a deity.
   
( For Major Lerch, see Minami Ward in Sapporo City, Ishikari sub-
  prefecture. )

( For shrine, see Kuriyama Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 
 
In 1902, 199 soldiers died during a winter training on Mt. Hakkouda in 
Aomori Prefecture.  Foreign countries reported the accident.  The King 
of Norway and the Swedish army gave pairs of skies to the Japanese 
Army.  

Japanese people didn't know how to use the skies.  It was before the 
Russo-Japanese War ( 1904-1905 ).  After the war, Major Lerch came to 
Japan in November 1910 and taught skiing.  Initially, skiing was 
introduced to the army.  Today, Self Defense Force personnel practice 
skiing.  They ski wearing leather boots.

( For Mt. Hakkkouda disaster, sea Yakumo Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

#6  

Railway forest ( ``tetsudou-rin'' ) remains along the former Iburi 
Line.  The Fuudo-kan Museum is located near the forest.  It's managed 
by Kucchan Town.  It was built at the former site of Rokugou Station.

( UTF code ; ``tetsudou-rin'' = \u9244\u9053\u6797. )
 
The museum was named after the book ``Fuudo ( Climate )'' published by 
Tetsurou Watsuji in 1935.  The station's name ``Rokugou'' came from 
Road No. 6 ( ``rokugou-sen'' ), which is part of the Colonial Land 
Division in Kucchan wilderness. 

( Cf.  Rokugou district is located in Furano City, Kamikawa general 
  sub-prefecture.  It's known for the TV drama `Kita-no-kunikara ( From
  The North Country ) and The University of Tokyo Hokkaidou Forest. 
  The name Rokugou in Furano means the place at the foot of Mt. Tai-
  roku-zan ( 1,459 meters ). )

The Fuudo-kan Museum opened in 2002.  The cost of the building 
construction was 200 million yen.  It was the same as the annual snow 
removal cost of Kucchan Town ( by a curator of the museum ).  

( The snow removal cost of a municipality excludes national and 
  prefectural roads in the municipality.  They are covered by Ministry 
  of Land, Infrastructure, Transport, and Tourism and the Hokkaidou 
  Government.  Maintaining the building and personnel of the museum 
  is another cost. )

Kucchan Town has been designated as a Special Heavy Snowfall Zone 
( Tokubetsu-gousetsu-chitai ).  Its average annual snowfall is 1,062 
centimeters.  A curator of the museum said, ``Lack of exercise in 
summer can be overcome in winter'' ( by snow shoveling ).
 
A wing of a Zero fighter is displayed in the museum.  The Zero fighter 
was used for icing experiments on the top of Mt. Youtei-zan.  When I 
saw it, I remembered that I had made plastic models including Zero 
fighters when I was a child.  Japan was a country of manufacturing 
industry.

( For the Iburi Line, see Kyougoku Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

( For Zero fighter, see Niseko Town in Shiribeshi sub-prefecture. )  
   
#7  
 
As for Grand Hirafu Ski Resort located in Yamada district in Kucchan 
Town, in December 1961, Niseko Kouguen Kankou Co. opened Hirafu Ski 
Ground.  It installed Kougen lifts.  The company was an affiliate of 
the marine transport company Nittou-shousen. 

In 1965, the lift company San-morittsu Lift installed Alpine Lift.  
( The name ``San-morritsu'' was named after the ski resort St. Moritz 
in Switzerland. )  

In 1985, the real estate company Toukyuu took over Hirafu Ski Ground.  
The company also developed pensions and villas.  It was during the ski 
boom among Japanese people during the bubble economy period.  In 2004, 
Toukyuu purchased San-morittsu Lift, and the name of the ski resort 
changed to Grand Hirafu Ski Resort.  

The name ``Yamada'' is derived from Yamada Farm established in 1894.  
The farm also discovered a hot spring and opened Yamada Hot Spring.  
Yamada Farm was located on the foot of Niseko-an-nupuri.  It was far 
from the Colonial Land Division of Kucchan Wilderness ( Kucchhan-guen-
ya ), which completed in 1892 ( Meiji 25 ).

( Updated in 2025.  Part of Yamada district was separated and changed 
  to Niseko-hirafu district in October 2022.  The name ``Hirafu'' comes 
  from General Hirafu Abe in the ancient era. )

( For Hirafu Abe, see Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture. ) 

* * * 

As for Hanazono Ski Resort located north from Grand Hirafu Ski Resort, 
it was also opened by the real estate company Toukyuu in 1992.  The 
site had been designated for National Vacation Village ( Kokumin-
kyuuka-mura ), but it wasn't realized.

In 2004, after the domestic ski boom subsided, the Australian company 
Nihon Harmony Resort purchased Hanazono Ski Resort and nearby 
pensions.  In 2007, the Hong Kong company Pacific Century Premium 
Developments purchased Hanazono Ski Resort. 

* * *

As for other ski grounds in Kucchan Town, there is Asahiga-oka Ski 
Ground (  Asahi-ga-oka-skii-jou ) in front of Kucchan Station.  It's 
managed by the town.  

From 1971 to 2009, National Weiss Ski Ground ( Kokusetsu-waisu-sukii-
jou ) was in operation on the east slope of Mt. Weisshonn ( Waisu-
horun ).  The lifts were removed, but ski tours using snowmobiles are 
offered for foreign skiers. 

( Mt. Weisshorn ( 1,045 meters ) is located on the border between 
  Kutchan and Kyouwa towns in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture. ) 

( For Weisshorn, see Kyouwa Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

* * * 

Around Mt. Niseko-an-nupuri, the first rope toe was installed in 1956 
at Asahiga-oka Ski Ground in 1956.  It was intalled by Kucchan Town 
Chamber of Commerce and Ski Association of Japan.

The first ski lift around Mt. Niseko-an-nupuri was installed in 1961 at 
the ski slope behind Yamada Hot Spring in Kucchan Town.  It was 
installed for the 40th National Ski Championship ( Dai-yonjikkai-
zenkoku-skii-sensyuken-taikai ).  ( by ``History Of Kucchan'' http://
www.nihi.net/student/history.html  last accessed in July 2019. )   

( For the first ski lift in the country, see Minami Ward in Sapporo 
  City, Ishikari sub-prefecture. )

Before modern ski resorts opened, Mt. Niseko-an-nupuri was a mecca for 
mountain skiing.  In Kucchan Town, before the Second World War, the 
slope behind Yamada Hot Spring was used for mountain skiing.  

( For mountain skiing, see Niseko Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

In 1938, the 11th All Japan Student Downhill Championship ( Dai-juu-
ikkai-zen-nihon-gakusei-senshuken-taikai-kassou-kyougui ) was held in 
Yamahda Hot Spring.  The ski slope was what is now Grand Hirafu Ski 
Resort. ( by ``History Of Niseko-hirafu'' http://
www.nihi.net/area/history003.html  last accessed in July 2019. )  

( For ski boom, see the note of Iwanai Town, Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For ski resort, see Akaigawa Town, Niseko Town, and Rusutsu Village 
  in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture ; Chuuou Ward, Minami Ward, 
  and Teine Ward in Sapporo City, Ishikari sub-prefecture ; Furano 
  City and Shimukappu Town in Kamikawa general sub-prefecture ; 
  Shintoku Town in general Tokachi sub-prefecture. )
 
#8   
 
Japanese government promoted tourism.  There were two major waves in 
the promotion.  One was the Comprehensive Resort Areas Development Law 
( Sougou-hoyouchi-seibi-hou ), the so-called Resort Act, in the late 
1980s.  It attracted domestic tourists. 

High-rise hotels and ski resorts opened in the mountains.  The ocean 
resort Seagaia opened in Miyazaki Prefecture ( by T. Nihei 2018 ). 

The second wave was the Visit Japan Campaign in 2003.  It attracted 
foreign tourists.  In 2017, Professor Amati Marco of RMIT ( The Royal 
Melbourne Institute of Technology ) University, Australia, came to 
Hokkaidou.  He said, ``it's surprising that Japan's tourism policy 
continued so long.  Moreover, it's successful.''    

Although the country's prime ministers change frequently, tourism 
policy continues.  It's successful based on China's economic 
development and overseas promotions.  

In 2021, I retired from Hokkaidou University, moved to Toukyou, and 
took my career to the tourism industry ( Department of Tourism 
Science ).

#9 

During a bus excursion to Yamada district in Kucchan Town in October 
2012, I noted these.

( 1 ) The site of the highest land price in the town is in front of 
Seicomart in Yamada district.  ( Seicomart is a convenience store 
chain in Hkkaidou. )  It's about 500,000 yen per one `tsubo' ( 3.3 
square meters ).  It used to be 1,000,000 yen per one `tsubo.''
 
Investment from overseas continues.  There is a T-junction at the foot 
of the Grand Hirafu Ski Resort.  Below that is a condominium area.  
It's called Hirafu Area.  Tourists from Australia and China stay.  The 
area used to be a pension village for domestic tourists. 

Australians ski in the morning, about between 7 AM and 9 AM.  The 
skiing is called ``first track'' or ``first chair.''  The snow 
condition is good.  Some of them go outside ski grounds and get lost.  
After skiing, they go hot springs, hiking, and shopping and eat in 
Sapporo City.  

( In Japanese ; ``T-junction'' is ``tei-ji-ro.''  The English letter 
  ``T'' and the Kanji letter ``tei'' resemble. )

( For T-junction, see the note of Otobe Town in Hiyama sub-
  prefecture. )
 
( 2 ) The beginning of international tourism is the opening of NAC 
( Niseko Adventure Center ) in 1995.  It's a tourism company managed by 
an Australian manager.  It offers ski lessons in winter and rafting and 
kayaking tours from spring to summer. 

( 3 ) Hotel Guinrei-sou was located adjacent to Gland Hirafu Ski 
Resort.  It was in operation from 1968 to 2008.  The building was 
demolished.  The land was owned by Hokkaidou Prefecture, so town 
official went to Sapporo City, which is the prefectural capital, and 
submitted documents requesting its use for tourism purposes.  

( In Japanese ; ``The land owned by Hokkaidou Prefecture'' is  
  ``dou-yuu-chi.'' )

( Updated in 2025 ;  Hotel Sansui Niseko opened in 2022 at the site of 
  Hotel Guinrei-sou. )

Hotel Niseko Alpen is located adjacent to Gland Hirafu Ski Resort.  
This was founded by a dentist, who was the first person to 
seek international tourism.  

( Updated in 2025 ;  Hotel Niseko Alpen was in operation between 1986 
  and 2023. ) 
 
( 4 ) Near Seicomart ( a convenience store chain in Hokkaidou ),  there 
are six-story condominiums and low-rise cottages.  Group travelers from 
Hong Kong stayed the former.  Individual travelers from Australia stay 
the latter.

( 5 ) The number of foreign-owned cottages increases.  Foreigners 
spend one or two weeks in the cottages.  The construction of the 
cottages complied with the landscape ordinance ( ``keikan-jourei'' ).  
The name of a cottage contains one Kanji character, for example, 
``tamashii,'' which means soul, and ``kizuna'', which means bond.  

( UTF code ; ``kizuna'' = \u7D46.  ``tamashii'' = \u9B42. ) 

( 6 ) The largest number comes from Hong Kong.  They ski at the low 
altitude.  They enjoy looking at the snow.  The beginner courses are 
the most crowded.  Many tourists also come from Taiwan.  The number is 
200,000 annually.  An elderly Japanese couple spends 500,000 to 600,000 
yen.  Tourists decreases in autumn and spring.

( 7 ) Hirafu Station on the Hakodate Main Line is near the ski resort, 
but skiers don't use it.  ( The expresses trains run along the Pacific 
coast.  Kucchan Town is located on the Sea of Japan side. ) 

Hirafu Station is an unmanned station.  The former station office is 
now a guesthouse.  ( According to Wikipedia, the average daily number 
of passengers using Hirafu Station was 10 or fewer per day, from 2011 
to 2015. )

( 8 ) Kucchan Town's specialy is ``gousetsu-udon,'' which means ``heavy 
snow, wheat noodle.''  The wheat noodle containes potato starch and 
comes in a large portion.  The price is 700 yen.  

( Single of the chapter ; ``Tecleando ( Typing Tango )'' by Troilo. 
  --> http://youtu.be/qBPNyoIi_Vk )

 ------------------
AREA 305 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 216, CULTIVATED 43 ; COUNTY = IWANAI ; 
POP 7,961 ( 1990 ), 6,428 ( 2010 ) ; MEAN ANNUAL TEMP 8.3 C ( AV 1981-
2010 ), PRECIPI 905 MM, SNOW 743 CM ( AV 1984-2010 ) ; COUNTRY SIGN = 
SCARECROW.  

LOCATION  
 
Kyouwa ( kyou-wa ) is a town in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture, 
central Hokkaidou ( ``dou-ou'' ).  The Horikappu River flows through 
the Iwanai Plain and empties in the Sea of Japan.  

To the south is the Niseko Mountains, including Mt. Chise-nupuri 
( 1,134 meters ) and Mt. Waisu-horun ( 1,045 meters ).  Mt. Waisu-horun
was named after Weisshorn ( 4,505 meters ) in Switzerland.  ( Waisu 
district is located to the south of Kozawa Station on the Hakodate Main 
Line.  The name is derived from Mt. Waisu-horun. )

( UTF code ; Kyouwa = \u5171\u548c. )

NAME 

The name ``Kyou-wa'' means ``together, harmony'' in Japanese.  It was 
named by a public offering when three municipalities were merged in 
1955 ( Shouwa 30 ).  According to ``Kyouwa Town History ( Kyouwa-chou-
shi )'' published in 1972, the name also imitates the pronunciation of 
Shouwa.

HISTORY

OLD HATTARI VILLAGE ; The area extended to the north from the Horikappu 
River.  The name comes from the Ainu word ``kamuy-hattar,'' which means 
``god, abyss.''  In Ainu language, ``god'' often means ``bear.''  Maybe
bears appeared along the river to catch salmon. 
 
( UTF code ; Hattari = \u767A\u8DB3. )

In 1880 ( Meiji 13 ), the administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) 
of Hattari was established.  In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), Hattari Village 
( 2nd-class municipality ) was established.  

( For 1st-class and 2nd-class municipalities, see the note of Matsumae 
  Town. )
 
OLD MAEDA VILLAGE ; The area extended to the south from the Horikappu 
River.  The name comes from the Maeda clan, which was the lord of Kaga 
Domain ( now Ishikawa Prefecture ).  The etymology is the same as Maeda 
district in Teine Ward, Sapporo City, Ishikari sub-prefecture.  

( UTF code ; Maeda = \u524D\u7530. )

In 1884 ( Meiji 17 ), Maeda Farm started reclamation.  In 1897 ( Meiji 
30 ), the administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) of Maeda village 
was established.  In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), Maeda Village ( 2nd-class 
municipality ) was established.  In 1923 ( Taishou 12 ), Maeda Village 
( 1st-class municipality ) was established.  #1

OLD KOZAWA VILLAGE ; The area was located inland.  The name comes from 
``sak-rupespe'' in Ainu language, which means ``summer, trail along 
stream.''  Or, it comes from ``sak-rupes-pet'' in Ainu language, which 
means ``summer, trail along, river.''

The name was shortened to Sakkuru, whose Kanji characters means 
``summer, small, stream.''  Then, the name Sakkuru was shortened again, 
and the pronunciation was changed to Kozawa.  The name ``Kozawa'' means 
``small, stream'' in Japanese.  The etymology is the same as Hayakita 
district in Abira Town, Iburi general sub-prefecture.  

( UTF code ; Kozawa = \u5C0F\u6CA2.  Sakkuru = \u590F\u5C0F\u6CA2. )

In 1883 ( Meiji 13 ), the administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) 
of Kozawa village was established.  In 1909 ( Meiji 42 ), Kozawa 
Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was established.   

* * *

In 1955 ( Shouwa 30 ), Kyouwa Village was established by the merger of 
three villages ; Hattari, Maeda, and Kozawa.  In 1971 ( Shouwa 46 ), 
Kyouwa Town was established.  
 
TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC 

The town hall of Kyouwa is located along the middle reaches of the 
Horikappu River.  National Route 276 runs near the town hall.  In 
Kozawa district inland, National Route 5 and the Hakodate Main Line 
run.  #2 

Formerly, the Iwanai Line connected stations of Kozawa, Kunitomi, 
Horoni, Maeda, Nishi-maeda, and Iwanai.  Kunitomi Station was 
established for Kunitomi Mine.  ( For Kunitomi Mine, see the INDUSTRY 
section below. ) 

INDUSTRY

Distinctive industries in Kyouwa are agriculture, electronic device, 
and sightseeing.  STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = paddy rice 1,579 hectares 
in 2015, wheat 455, melons 329, buckwheat 328, potatoes 319, beans 
( soybeans 111, adzuki beans 42 ), watermelons 173, fodder 115, 

( continue ) --> pasture 26, radishes 31, suager beets 24, broccoli 10, 
green onions 2, yams 1, and other vegetables 162+X ( including sweet 
corn, pumpkins, asparagus ) ; dairy cows - ( zero ) in 2015.  The 
municipality's area faces the Sea of Japan, but there is no fishing 
port. 

Paddy fields spread in the Iwanai Plain.  Vegetables and wheat are 
cultivated in paddy fields after the enactment of the rice reduction 
policy ( ``guentan-seisaku'').  

Raiden watermelon ( Raiden-suika ) is a local specialty.  The name 
``Raiden'' is derived from Raiden coast or Mt. Raiden ( 1,211 meters ) 
in Iwanai county.  

( For asparagus cultivation, see Iwanai Town, Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

( For the name ``Raiden,'' see Iwanai Town, Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )
 
As for electronic device industry, Sumitomo Kunitomi Electronics 
( Sumitomo-kunitomi-denshi ) is located at the intersection of National 
Route 5 and National Route 276.  The company manufactures crystalline 
materials used in smart-phones.  It also manufactures magnetic 
materials used in small motors. 

( In Japanese ; ``Crystalline material'' is ``kesshou-zairyou.'' 
  ``Magnetic material'' is ``jisei-zairyou.'' ) 

Before the establishment of Sumitomo Kunitomi Electronics, there was 
Kunitomi Mine between 1908 and 1948.  Copper, zinc, silver, and gold 
were produced.  It was managed by Sumitomo Metal Mining ( Sumitomo-
kinzoku-kouzan ).  

Following the closure of Kunitomi Mine, copper and zinc smelters 
operated between 1948 and 1973.  The copper and zinc were imported from 
abroad.  In 1974, the production of electronic material started.  In 
2009, Sumitomo Kunitomi Electronics was established by separated from 
Sumitomo Metal Mining.

As for sightseeing, the Scarecrow Village Museum ( Kakashi-furusato-
kan ) opened in 1994, using an abandoned wooden school building.  It 
conveys the history of the pioneering period. 

Shinsen Pond ( Shinsen-numa ), at an elevation of 760 meters, lies at 
at the foot of Mt. Chise-nupuri ( 1,132 meters ).  The name Shin-sen 
means ``god, hermit ( ha'a-mit )'' in Japanese.  It's a mysterious 
pond. 

NOTE 

#1  
  
Maeda Farm was established by Toshitsugu Maeda ( 1858-1900 ), who was 
the head of the Maeda clan in the Kaga Domain.  He bought land in 
Riyamunai Village.  The area was 2,500 hectares ( 25 square 
kilometers ).  

After 1884 ( Meiji 17 ), 79 families migrated.  They were vassals of 
the former Kaga Domain ( now Ishikawa Prefecture ).  Maeda Farm was 
managed directory by the company owned by Toshitsugu Maeda.  Migrants 
became tenant farmers.  

( UTF code ; Riyamunai = \u68A8\u91CE\u821E\u7D0D. ) 
  
Toshitsugu Maeda studied in England as a member of Iwakura Mission.  
After returning to Japan, he opened Maeda Farm to practice European 
farming, including dairy farming, upland farming, butter and cheese 
processing, and the breeding of bulls.  
 
In 1894 ( Meiji 27 ) and 1985 ( Meiji 28 ), other two Maeda farms 
opened in Barato and Karukawa in Ishikari sub-prefecture.  The 
locations were what are now Kita Ward and Teine Ward, Sappro City.
 
In 1899 ( Meiji 32 ), the original Maeda Farm in Maeda village, located 
in what is now Kyouwa Town, was divided into 53 farms and discontinued. 
( by K. Hanaki 2012.  ``Farmers Of The North 2/3 ( Kita-no-noushi-tachi
2/3 )'' http://takosi.cocolog-nifty.com/blog/files/kitanonousitati2.pdf 
last accessed in August 2026. )

( For large farm in the Meiji period, see Kyougoku Town in Shiribeshi 
  general sub-prefecture. )
   
#2

The souvenir from Kozawa Station on the Hakodate Main Line is Tunnel-
mochi, which means ``tunnel rice cake.''  It was sold at the station.  
Now it's sold in front of the station.  The name ``tunnel'' refers to 
the Inaho Tunnel, which opened in 1904.  The tunnel is located between 
Kozawa Station in Kyouwa Town and Guinzan Station in Niki Town. 

Tunnel-mochi is a rare souvenir.  I haven't seen it even in the 
souvenir shop of Sapporo Station.  ( The souvenir shop is Dosanko 
Plaza.  It sells various souvenirs from all over Hokkaidou.  While the 
souvenir shops in the airport sell similar souvenirs. )
 
( Single of the chapter ; ``Sugar'' by Spangle Call Lilli Line.  -->
  http://youtu.be/yCAmtckfZgU )
