  \u9AD8\u7802\u753A.  Wakatake-chou = \u82E5\u7AF9\u753A. )

( 3 ) One administrative office for eight towns and one village ; 
Kaiun-chou, Kondon-chou, Nobuka-chou, Nobuka-ura-machi, Okusawa-mura, 
Ryuutoku-chou, Shibai-machi, Shinchi-chou, and Wakamatsu-chou.

( UTF code ; Kaiun-chou = \u958B\u904B\u753A.  Kondon-chou = 
  \u91D1\u96F2\u753A.  Nobuka-chou = \u4FE1\u9999\u753A. 
  Nobuka-ura-machi = \u4FE1\u9999\u88CF\u753A.  Okusawa-mura = 
  \u5965\u6CA2\u6751.  Ryuutoku-chou = \u7ADC\u5FB3\u753A.  
  Shibai-machi = \u829D\u5C45\u753A.  Shinchi-chou = 
  \u65B0\u5730\u753A.  Wakamatsu-chou = \u82E5\u677E\u753A. )

( 4 ) One administrative office for four villages ; Asari-mura, 
Hariusu-mura, Kumausu-mura, and Zenibako-mura.

( UTF code ; Asari-mura = \u671D\u91CC\u6751.  Hariusu-mura = 
  \u5F35\u7893\u6751.  Kumausu-mura = \u718A\u7893\u6751.  
  Zenibako-mura = \u92AD\u51FD\u6751. )

In the same year ( 1880 ), in Takashima county ( Takashima-gun ), one 
administrative office was established for four villages ; Ironai-mura, 
Shukutsu-mura, Takashima-mura, Temiya-mura, and Ironai-mura.

( UTF code ; Shukutsu-mura = \u795D\u6D25\u6751.  Takashima-mura = 
  \u9AD8\u5CF6\u6751.  Temiya-mura = \u624B\u5BAE\u6751. )

In the same year, in Oshoro county ( Oshoro-gun ), two administrative 
offices were established.  One was for Momonai-mura and Shioya-mura.  
The other was for Oshoro-mura and Ranshima-mura.

( UTF code ; Momonai-mura = \u6843\u5185\u6751.  Oshoro-mura = 
  \u5FCD\u8DEF\u6751.  Ranshima-mura = \u862D\u5CF6\u6751.  Shioya-mura 
  = \u5869\u8C37\u6751. ) 

#3
 
In 1880 ( Meiji 13 ), the Horonai Line opened between Temiya Station 
and Sapporo Station.  ( Temiya is located along the Port of Otaru. )  
In 1882 ( Meiji 15 ), the railway was extended to Horonai Station.  
( Horonai was a coal mine located in what is now Mikasa City. )

In 1905 ( Meiji 38 ), the Hokkaidou Raiway ( Hokkaidou-tetsudou ) 
opened between Hakodate Station and Takashima Station.  The railway is 
the present-day Hakodate Main Line.  Takashima Station at the time was 
the present-day Otaru Station.  Otaru Station at the time was the 
present-day Minami-otaru Station. 

In 1909 ( Meiji 42 ), the section of the Horonai Line between Otaru 
Station ( now Minami-otaru Station ) and Temiya Sation was renamed the 
Temiya Line.  The first coal loading pier ( pi'-er ) was located in 
front of Temiya Station.

( For coal mining, see Yuubari City in Sorachi general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For the Horonai Line, see Iwamizawa City and Mikasa City in Sorachi 
  general sub-prefecture. ) 

The steam locomotives on the former Horonai Line were of the American 
type.  The ones that ran between Yokohama and Shinbashi stations and 
between Koube and Oosaka stations were of the British type.

A replica of the American steam locomotive is on display at the Otaru 
Museum.  It's a transcontinental locomotive.  It has a large chimney to 
prevent the spread of embers.  The fuel was firewood ( not coal ) and 
water.  

The front of the stem locomotive was equipped with a cowcatcher.  The 
locomotive was like the one out of a Western ( --> http://
en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jupiter_(locomotive) ).  The cowcatcher was 
designed for the bison that inhabited the American continent.
 
The first steem locomotives in Hokkaidou were named Yoshitsune-gou, 
Benkei-gou, and Shizuka-gou.  They ran on the Hornonai Line ( now the 
Hakodate Main Line ), transporting coal and people. 

( For Yoshitsune legend, see Biratori Town in Hidaka sub-prefecture. )

Joseph Ury Crawford was an American railroad engineer.  He worked for 
the Hokkaidou Development Commission in the Meiji period.  In Sapporo, 
he laid a railroad along the edge of an alluvial fan.  Below the fan 
was a wetland ( now Hokkaidou University ).  Above the alluvial fan was 
a sloping area ( now the center of Sapporo City ). 

Benjamin Smith Lyman was an American geologist who worked for the 
Hokkaidou Development Commission in the Meiji period.  He discovered 
coal strata in Horonai ( now Mikasa City ) in the Yuubari Mountains. 

( For Joseph Ury Crawford and Benjamin Smith Lyman, see Mikasa City in 
  Sorachi general sub-prefecture. )

#4

Before the Second World War, the Port of Otaru flourished.  They 
exported coal, herring lees ( ``nishin-kasu'' ), kidney beans 
( ``inguen-mame'' ), potatoes, and timbers.  Herring lees were used 
as fertilizer. 

Kidney beans were exported to the United Kingdom and other European 
countries.  European people prefer kidney beans to soybeans or adzuki 
beans.  

Timbers were processed into ``inch-wood'' and exported to European 
countries for making furniture.  There used to be large and straight 
trees in Hokkaidou Island.  

( ``Inch-wood'' refers to lumber in inches.  ( 1 inch = 2.54 
  centimeters. )  Meanwhile, Japanese lumber was sawn by the  
  ``shakkan'' system.  ( 1-shaku = 30.3 centimeters, 1-sun = 3.03 
  centimeters, 1-bu = 0.3 centimeters. )  

( For unit conversion, see Nihei lab 2004.  ``Unit Conversion''  
  HTTP://2242.MOOO.COM/UNIT.TXT )
 
Before the Second World War, Otaru's economy flourished.  Banks were 
established.  Otaru was called the Wall Street of the North.  Old 
bank buildings remain, including the Bank of Japan, Hokkaidou Bank, 
Hokkaidou Takushoku Bank, Mitsui Bank, and Yasuda Bank. 

Trade with Sakhalin ceased after the war.  The primary energy source 
shifted from coal to oil in the 1970s.  Otaru's function as a logistics 
hub declined.

#5 

Ferries from the Port of Otaru connect cities along the Sea of Japan.
The ferry company is Shin-nihonkai Ferry.  The cities are Niigata in 
Niigata Prefecture, Tsuruga in Fukui Prefecture, and Maizuru in Kyouto 
Prefecture.  Ferries also carry milk containers from Otaru to Maizuru.  

The Port of Otaru has regular cargo shipping routes that connect to 
Shanghai, Qingdao, Dalian in China, and Vladivostok in Russia.

Eye-catching facilities at the Port of Otaru include gantry cranes for 
loading containers, Taiheiyou Cement silos ( sa'i-los ), and silos for
animal feed and grain run by Marubeni and Nissin.
 
Professor Taisuke Miyauchi of Hokkaidou University, Faculty of Letters, 
said, ``More than 10 years' ago, I moved from Fukui Prefectural 
Unversity to Hokkaidou University.  I arrived at the Port of Otaru by 
ferry with my car.  Professor Seki, my predecessor, came to the port 
to meet me.''
 
#6 
 
The survey for the first Population Census was conducted in 1920 
( Taishou 9 ).  The three most populous municipalities in Hokkaidou 
were Hakodate Ward ( 144,749 ), Otaru Ward ( 108,113 ), and Sapporo 
Ward ( 102,580 ).  

( For the first Population Census, see Yuubari City in Sorachi general 
  sub-prefecture. ) 

According to Otaru City, the population peaked at 207,093 in 1964 
( Shouwa 39 ).  It was during the high economic growth period.  The 
population has declined since then.

The terrain of Otaru is sandwiched between mountains and the sea.  It 
hindered the expansion of residential and industrial areas.  Old port 
cities have slopes.

( For slope, see Hakodate City, Oshima general sub-prefecture. )
 
The center of Sapporo City is located 30 kilometers east from the 
center of Otaru City.  The center of Sapporo City is located on a 
plain.  The terrain is an alluvial fan.  The population of Sapporo 
increases.  

Otaru City has been designated as a Depopulated Region since 2010.  Its
population still remains 7th largest in the prefecture, but it's a 
Depopulated Region due to its high rate of depopulation.

( In Japanese ; ``Depopulated Region'' is ``kaso-chiiki.'' )

Using the Special Bonds for Promoting Self-reliance in Depopulated 
Regions, so-called Depopulation Bonds ( Kaso-sai ), hospitals and other 
medical facilities were established in the center of Otaru City. 
The location is Okusawa-juuji-gai, which means ``Okusawa, cross, 
road'' in Japanese.  

( UTF code ; Okusawa-juuji-gai = \u5965\u6CA2\u5341\u5B57\u8857. )

( For the name ``juuji-gai'' ( cross road ), see Otobe Town in Hiyama 
  sub-prefecture. ) 
 
The population pyramid of Japan has a peculiar shape.  It looks like 
a star.  One of the large groups is the baby boomers.  The other is 
the second generation of the baby boomers. 

The baby boomers were born just after the Second World War, which ended 
in 1945.  Maybe, the spirits who died in the war have come back.  The 
second baby boomers were born in the early 1970s.  Then, depopulation 
of the country started in 2008.
 
( For depopulation, see Oshamanbe Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture ; Yuubari City and Utashinai City in Sorachi general sub-
  prefecture ; Enbetsu Town in Rumoi sub-prefecture. ) 

( For population pyramid, see Shakotan Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

#7    
 
Otaru is a city of slopes.  Many houses built on slopes.  There are
houses even built just above expressway tunnels.  

There are two housing estates on the outer fringe of the central Otaru.
They are Bouyou-dai district and Sakura-machi district.  During a bus 
excursion in 2014, I heard these ; 
 
``Sakura-machi district is the first housing estate in Otaru City.  
  It's a new town.  ( The name `Sakura-machi' means `cherry, town' 
  in Japanese. ) 

  Kiichirou Noguchi, who was the second president of Kita-no-homare 
  brewery, purchased hilly land to build this housing estate.  ( Kita-
  no-homare is a rice wine brewery company. )  Land purchasing started 
  in 1931.  Land consolidation was completed in 1940. 

  Sakura-machi district was built without excavating the hills.  Houses 
  and streets line up along contour lines.  The center of Sakura-machi 
  district is a roundabout. 

  In front of the roundabout is a shrine.  The name is Kumausu Shrine.  
  In Otaru, there were many shrines and portable shrines ( `mikoshi' ). 
  Many artisans crafted portable shrines and Buddhist altars.'' 

( Roundabout and rotary are the same in Japanese.  Both are called 
  rotary.  In Hokkaidou University, there are two roundabouts.  One is 
  located at the north end of the central street.  The other is located 
  at the south end of the central street, near the statue of William 
  Smith Clark. )

( For roundabout and rotary, see Nanae Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture ; Kushiro City in Kushiro general sub-prefecture. )

The explanation at the bus excursion continued ;

``Bouyou-dai housing estate is adjacent to Sakura-machi.  ( The name 
  `Bou-you-dai' means `looking, sea, hill' in Japanese. )  It's the new 
  housing estate where sales began in the 1980s.  

  Bouyou-dai housing estate was developed by Mitsubishi Estate Co.  An 
  executive of the real estate company graduated from Otaru High School 
  of Commerce ( Otaru-koutou-shougyou-gakkou ; now Otaru University of 
  Commerce ).  Bouyou-dai was part of Mt. Kenashi-yama ( 548 meters ).  
  This housing estate is located on slopes, as well as Sakura-machi.  

  The planned population for Bouyou-dai housing estate was 11,000.  The 
  current population is 3,300.  Bouyou-dai is the largest new town in 
  Otaru City.  ( This area includes Bouyou Ski Jumping Hill, Bouyou 
  Soccer and Ruby Fields, and Marine Hill Hotel. )'' 
 
Today, many people commute from Sapporo City to Otaru City.  One of 
the organizers of the bus excursion, Mr. Kanamori, also lives in 
Sapporo and commutes to Otaru-chouryou High School.

( For slope, see Hakodate City in Oshima general sub-prefecture. )
 
#8
 
The east part of the Port of Otaru is Otaru-chikkou district.  Otaru-
chikkou Station on the Hakodate Main Line is adjacent to Otaru Marina.  
The name ``chikkou'' is Japanese, which means ``making, port.''  The 
district was developed as a storage to construct the port.  The station 
opened in 1910 ( Meiji 43 ). 

Otaru-chikkou Station was managed by Japanese National Railways 
( Kokutetsu ).  The site was large, including a freight yard, a coal 
yard, and a train depot ( ``sharyou-kichi'' ).  In 1970, coal 
transportation from the pier ( pi'ar ) in front of this station 
discontinued.  In 1986, freight transportation discontinued. 

( For coal mining, see Yuubari City in Sorachi general sub-
  prefecture. )

In 1997, the area around Otaru-chikkou Station was redeveloped into a 
commercial area.  In 1999, Mycal Otaru opened.  It was a commercial 
complex including SATY ( food shopping center ), VIVRE ( clothing 
shopping center ), Warner Mycal Cinemas, and the Hilton Otaru 
( hotel ).  Land for making shifted to land for consumption.

In 2001, Mycal went bankrupt.  The commercial complex was changed to 
Posful ( shopping center ), then to Wing Bay Otaru ( today's shipping 
center ).  The Hilton Otaru was changed to Grand Park Otaru ( hotel ).  

Mycal was a large retail company from Oosaka Prefecture.  It was the 
symbol of the babble economy.  In 1989, Mycal Honmoku opened in 
Yokohama City.  It was opened at the former site of United States 
Force's housing estate.

Looking at the landscape of Otaru-chikkou, I remembered an old proverb, 
``Those who prosper decline ( Jousha-hissui ).''  This comes from ``The 
Tale Of The Taira Family,'' an oral narrative from the 12th century. 

( For the Tale of the Taifa Famiy, see Nihei lab 2026_w. ) 
 
#9 

Professor Emeritus Katsuhiko Yamashita explained Otaru's past 
industries as follows ;

``In the Meiji period, there were rice milling factories and breweries 
  for `miso' ( fermented soybean seasoning ), soy sauce, and `sake' 
  ( rice wine ). 

  In the Taishou period, rubber and metal industries started.  They 
  produced rubber soles for `tabi ( split-toe footwear ).'  
  ( Split-toe footwear --> http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tabi ). 

  Hokkai-seikan Co. produced cans for salmon, sea trout, and crab. 
  ( They were caught in the North Pacific Ocean ( Hokuyou ) ).  Touyou-
  mokuzai ( Oriental Lumber ) Co. was founded in 1940, based on its 
  experience in manufacturing wooden crates for canned seafood.

  As for rice milling industry, Kyousei Co. operated four rice mills 
  and a power plant along the Asari River.  A railroad connected those 
  rice mills with Asari Station ( on the Hakodate Main Line ).  In the 
  Taishou period, those rice mills and a power plant were washed away 
  by a flood.  ( The Taishou period continued from 1912 to 1926. )'' 

The founder of Kyousei Co. was Kisaburou Numata.  He was one of the 
wealthy merchants in Otaru.  The company sold rice in Hokkaidou Island 
and Sakhalin Island.  

( In Japanese ; ``Wealthy merchant'' is ``gou-shou.''  ``Sakhalin'' is 
  Karafuto. )

( For Kisaburou Numata, see Numata Town in Sorachi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

#10   
 
Glass manufacturing in Otaru started in 1901 ( Meiji 34 ) ( by ``Otaru 
And Kita-ichi Glass'' http://kitaichiglass.co.jp/ayumi/ ).  Kerosene 
lamps were produced.

After the Russo-Japanese War ( 1904-1905 ), the Japan-Russia Fisheries 
( Nichi-ro-gyogyou-kyouei ) was Agreement was concluded in 1907.  
Asahara Glass ( now Kita-ichi Glass ) in Otaru started to produce 
glass fishing floats in 1910.

In the past, fishing floats were made of glass.  Today, glass fishing 
floats decorate seafood bars in Susukino.  ( Susukino is an 
entertainment district in Sapporo City. )

During the Meiji period, the sheet glass for houses was imported from 
the Kansai region in Honshuu Island.  The glass was made by Asahi Grass 
Co.  The type of the glass was the smallest one, which was called 
``hanshi-ban.'' ( The size of ``hanshi-ban'' is 14 x 10 inches 
( 35 x 25 centimeters ) ).''  

In the past, glass windows were expensive.  In Hokkaidou, small glass 
windows were used to retain indoor warmth.  I like small windows that
are reminiscent of old European houses.  

Seen through old sheet glass, the view outside looks slightly 
distorted.  We can find old sheet glass at herring mansions 
( ``nishin-goten'' ).  

Yuugo Ono wrote a chapter titled ``The climate and culture of 
Hokkaidou'' ( by Takeo Ichikawa ed. 1981.  ``The Climate And Culture Of 
Japan'' published by Kokon-shoin, ISBN 9784772213226 ).  The following 
is a quotation. 

``During the pioneering years of the USA, immigrants settled in the 
  West.  But, some people only intended to acquire land.  If the houses 
  had glass windows, the residents intended to settle there.  The glass 
  was small and expensive.  It was made in the East.

  In Honshuu Island, people open glass windows on summer evenings and 
  enjoy the cool air.  In Hokkaidou Island, there is no need to open 
  glass windows.  People can enjoy the cool air indoors.''

In Japanese, the act of enjoying the cool evening air is called ``yuu-
suzumi.''  Today, Toukyou's summer is too hot.  People cannot open 
windows even at night.  

( For herring fishing, see Yoichi Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For sheet glass, see Utashinai City in Sorachi general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For small window, see Nihei lab 2023-2026_t. )
   
#11 
  
The Otaru Canal is a tourist destination.  It was constructed from 1914
to 1923, during the Taishou period.  It was part of the Port of Otaru.

The Otaru Canal was a reclamation ( re-cla-ma'i-tion )  canal, which 
was made by reclaiming ( re-cle'i-ming ) offshore while utilizing an 
existing shoreline.  Conventional canals were constructed by 
excavating.

( In Japanese ; ``Excavating canal'' is ``kussaku-shiki-unga.'' 
  ``Reclamation canal'' is ``umetate-shiki-unga.'' )

Before the Second World War, cargo ships anchored off the coast.  Small 
boats transported cargo between ships and quays along the Otaru Canal. 
Such cargo handling is called offshore cargo handling.
 
( In Japanese ; ``Offshore cargo handling'' is ``okidori-shiki'' or 
  ``oki-niyaku.'')

After the war, wharves were constructed.  The Otaru Canal was unused.  
Sludge and mud accumulated, emitting a foul odor.  It became a smelly 
sewer.  A plan emerged to convert the canal into a road. 

In the 1980s, a movement to preserve the Otaru Canal arose.  As a 
result, half side of the canal was reclaimed, while the other half side 
was preserved.  The width of the canal was halved.  The reclaimed part 
is now Prefectural Route 17 ( Rinkou-sen ).  

Masao Kanamori, a teacher at Otaru-chouryou High School, says that he 
participated in the Otaru Canal preservation movement when he was a 
high school student.  At the time, he waved a flag and chanted, ``No 
landfill!! ( `umetate-hantai' ).''  

( Active people became high school teachers and university teachers.  
  Once, William Smith Clark, the president of Sappro Agricultural 
  College ( now Hokkaidou University ), said, ``Boys, be ambitious.'' )

In this way, warehouses along the Otaru Canal were preserved.  They 
were converted into restaurants, bars, and souvenir shops.  On the 
other hand, the warehouses in Arihoro district weren't preserved.  
Arihoro was the base for bean exports during the First World War.  The 
number of old warehouses is decreasing. 

#12  
 
These are historical buildings located in the fringe of central Otaru.

( 1 ) Guin-rin-sou ; It's a hotel and restaurant at Cape Hiraiso, near 
Otaru Chikkou Station.  One of the buildings was a herring mansion 
( ``nishin-goten'' ) built in 1900.

( UTF code ; Guin-rin-sou = \u9280\u9C57\u8358. ) 

The herring manshion in Guin-rin-sou was moved from neighboring Yoichi 
Town to Otaru City in 1938.  The name ``guin-rin'' means ``silver, 
scale'' in Japanese.  It signified the prosperity of herring fishery. 

( Guin-rin-sou is located in Sakura-machi district.  For Sakura-machi 
  district, see Note No. 7 above. )

( 2 ) The Former Aoyama's Second Residence ( Kyuu-aoyama-bettei ) ; 
It's a restaurant and museum, near Otaru Aquarium.  The building was 
a herring mansion, too.  

The Former Aoyama's Second Residence was built from 1917 to 1923. 
It was modeled after the Honma's Residence in Sakata City, Yamagata 
Prefecture.  

The Main Aoyama's Residence exists in Yuza Town ( Yuza-machi ) in 
Yamagata Prefecture.  It's also used as a local museum. 

( The Honma family was a wealthy merchant ( ``gou-shou'' ) and landlord 
  in Yamagata Prefecture.  There is a saying, ``I cannot hope to rival 
  Mr. Honma, but I want to be a feudal loard.''  ( Honma-sama-ni-wa  
  Oyobimo-senuga  Semete-naritaya  Tonosama-ni. ) ) 

( 3 ) Otary City Herring Mansion ( Otaru-shi-nishin-goten ) ; It's 
located on the grounds of Otaru Aquarium.  It was built in 1897 in 
Tomari village, Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture, and moved to Otaru 
City in 1958.  

( For aquarium, see the note of Shakotan Town, Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For herring fishery, see Yoichi Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

( 4 ) Wakou-sou ; It's an Western-style residence located in Shiomi-dai 
district.  It's situated on a hill along the Katsunai River. 

( UTF code ; Wakou-sou = \u548C\u5149\u8358. )

Wakou-sou was built in 1922 by Kiichirou Noguchi, who was the second 
president of Kita-no-homare brewery.  ( Kita-no-homare is rice wine. ) 
The building was used as a hotel from 1948 to 1966.  

( For Kiichirou Noguchi, see Note No. 7 above. )  

* * * 
 
Other tourist destinations include Hokkaidou Wine, which is located at 
the foot of Mt. Kenashi-yama ( 548 meters ).  Sushi street in the old 
built-up area also attracts many tourists.

South Korean tourists visit Otaru City even in winter.  This is because 
a movie filmed in Otaru became a hit in the country.

( While the number of South Korean tourist increased, the number of 
  South Korean students decreased.  I didn't meet any South Korean 
  students after I moved to Hokkaidou University.  Meanwhile, many 
  Chinese students are studying.

  When I was a graduate student, during the 1990s, many South Korean 
  students were studying in Japan.  One of my friends married a South 
  Korean student.  Since then, South Korea became an international 
  country and many young people intend to study Western countries. ) 

( For international student, see Oshananbe Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For international tourist, see Kucchan Town in Shiribeshi general 
  sub-prefecture. )
  
#13

Zenibako Industrial Park is located adjacent to Sapporo City.  One 
hundred factories produce various products.  ( This information is 
based on the excursion held in 2014. )  

The factories in Zenibako Industrial Park produce food ( processed 
seafood, confectionery, bread, soups, sources, meat products, box 
lunches, and Otaru beer ), packaging materials ( cardboard, 
polyethylene, polystyrene ( poly-sta'i-rin ) foam ), machinery, steel, 
paper, and plywood. 

Otaru Beer is a local brewery founded in 1999.  It's operated by Aleph 
Inc.  The company runs Bikkuri Donkey, which is a hamburger restaurant 
chain.  It also runs Ekorin Village, which is a theme park of 
agriculture and English garden, located in Eniwa City, Ishikari sub-
prefecture. 

( Co. = Company.  Corp. = Corporation.  Inc. = Incorporation.  ) 

Hokkai Steel Co. ( Hokkai-kougyou ) produces steel.  The company 
uses electric furnaces ( fa'r-neces ).  The material is scrap iron.  
The furnaces are operated at night using low-cost nighttime electricity 
rates.  Hokkai Paper Work Co. ( Hokkai-shikou ) manufactures paper 
products.  It uses recycled paper collected in Sapporo City.
 
Shinguu Business Co. ( Shinguu-shoukou ) produces plywood and 
chainsaws.  It's a long-established company that originated in Shinguu 
City, Wakayama Prefecture.  The company entered Hokkaidou to produce 
railway sleepers in the Meiji Period.  

( The year that this company entered Hokkaidou was 1907, according to 
 ``Shinguu-shoukou Centennial History''  http://
  www.shingu-shoko.co.jp/history/  last accessed in August 2026. ) 

#14
  
I visited Kita-no-homare brewery ( Kitano-homare-shuzou ) in Otaru 
City during the excursion held in 2014.  I heard these ;

``The founder, Kichijirou Noguchi, came from Ishikawa Prefecture to 
  Otaru in 1886 ( Meiji 19 ).  He started soy sauce ( `shouyu' ) 
  brewery.  Then, he changed his business to rice wine brewery in 1897 
  ( Meiji 30 ).  

  This brewery still uses an old warehouse that was used for soy sauce 
  brewery in the Meiji period.  The salt from that time still remains 
  on the floor.

  ( Brewing soy sauce in an ordinary house is difficult, but making soy 
    paste ( ``miso'' ) isn't very difficult, so there are many kinds of 
    homemade soy pastes. )

  Kita-no-homare brewery also rice wine ( ``sake'' ) and raw rice 
  wine ( ``guen-shu'' ).  Raw rice wine It's sold in blue bottles.  The 
  alcohol content is 19 percent.  It isn't pasteurized or diluted with 
  water. 

  The alcohol content of raw rice wine is like distilled spirits 
  ( ``shoucyuu'' ).  The alcohol content of ordinary rice wine is 15 
  percent.  It's diluted with water.  

  ( In Hokkaidou, distilled spirits with an alcohol content of 20 
    percent are popular.  People drink it undiluted. ) 

  The difference in water is the difference in rice wine.  Kita-no-
  homare brewery uses subterranean current ( ``fukuryuu-sui'' ) from 
  Mt. Tengu ( 532 meters ).  Tanaka brewery ( Tanaka-shuzou ) is 
  located nearby.  The rice wine of Tanaka brewery significantly 
  differs from that of Kita-no-homare brewery.

  ( The distance between these breweries is 80 meters.  Tanaka brewery 
    uses water from a deep well. )
 
  The quantity of rice wine made by Kita-no-homare brewery is enough to 
  be sold at supermarkets throughout Hokkaidou.  But, it's insufficient 
  for nationwide.  The quantity is neither small nor large.

  It's difficult to sustain the operation at this scale.  The smaller 
  or larger, the better.  So, this brewery was acquired by the large 
  company Oenon Holdings in 2007.'' 

( For distilled spirits ( ``shouchuu'' ), see Nishi Ward in Sapporo 
  City, Ishikari sub-prefecture. ) 

( For raw rice wine, see the note of Akabira Town in Sorachi general 
  sub-prefecture. )

( For rice wine, see Asahikawa City in Kamikawa general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

* * *

I wrote a continuation in 2019, during a sabbatical leave at Hokkaidou 
University.
 
The rice wine production by Kita-no-homare brewery ended in 2015.  
Its 130 years of histroy were over.  I remember this saying, ``All 
things are impermanent, prosperity ends.''  This was said  in The Tale 
Of The Taira Family ( Heike-mono-gatari ).

( In Japanese ; ``All things are impermanent'' is ``Shogyou-mujou.'' 
  ``Prosperity ends'' is ``Jousha-hissui.'' ) 

( UTF code ; Jousha-hissui = \u76DB\u8005\u5FC5\u8870.  Shogyou-mujou = 
  \u8AF8\u884C\u7121\u5E38. ) 

( The Tale Of The Taira Family is an oral tale from the 12th century.  
  It depicts that Japan is a periphery like scattered millet 
  ( ``zokusan-henchi'' ).  It's a geopolitical point of view. )

( UTF code ; ``zokusan-henchi'' = \u7C9F\u6563\u8FBA\u5730. )

( For The Tale Of The Taira Family, see Kamoenai Village in Shiribeshi 
  general sub-prefecture ; Nihei lab 2026_w. )

A modern factory in Asahikawa City, run by Oenon Holdings, continues
to produce the rice wine Kita-no-homare.  Tanaka brewery, neighboring 
to the former Kita-no-homare brewery, continue to produce rice wine 
in Otaru City.  Tanaka brewery don't mass-produce.  They have a small 
number of devoted fans. 
 
Tanaka is small, Kita-no-homare is medium, Oenon is large.  Kita-no-
homare disappeared.  In life, you may need to select small or large.  
( The menu at Jirou-ramen noodle shop is only large or small. )

As for the name ``Kita-no-homare,'' I remember the TV drama ``From 
The North Country ( Kita-no-kuni-kara ).''  ( I'm the generation of 
the Shouwa period.  People watched television back then. )  In the TV 
drama, an elderly farmer who cannot adapt to the modern life dies.

The elderly farmer sold his horse in a winter.  The next day, he was 
found frozen to death under a bridge.  Next to his body lay a 1-shou 
( 1.8 liters ) bottle of rice wine.  The name was Kita-no-homare.

( For 1.8 liters of rice wine, see Kuriyama Town in Sorachi general 
  sub-prefecture. ) 

( For agricultural horse, see Obihiro City in Tokachi general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For an elderly man who drank and fell from a log bridge to die, see 
  Yakumo Town in Oshima general sub-prefecture.  ( The writer of the TV 
  drama may have known the man. ) )

( Single of the chapter ; ``Malandraca ( Scoundrel )'' by Pugliese.  
  --> http://youtu.be/o21XY8F2wQA )

 -----------------
During a bus excursion in 2011, I saw a red building on the top of a 
mountain.  That was Keishou-en restaurant.  The name ``Keishou-en'' 
means ``scenic, garden'' in Japanese.  It was a Jinguisu-kan 
restaurant.  The name ``Jinguisu-kan'' means a grilled mutton dish in 
Hokkaidou.  It isn't the Mongol emperor Genghis Khan.  #1

( For Jinguisu-kan, see Takikawa City in Sorachi general sup-
  prefecture. )

Keishou-en restaurant had a spectacular view of the Sea of Japan.  
Before the motorization, which started in the late 1960s, the 
restaurant was popular among celebrities who owned private cars.  The 
restaurant opened in 1957 and closed in 2010.  

( For motorization, see the note of Otobe Town in Hiyama sub-
  prefecture. )

( UTF code ; Keishou-en = \u666F\u52DD\u5712. )  

In the past, near Keishou-en restaurant, there was Jinkou-en.  The name 
``Jin-kou-en'' means ``god, engineering, garden'' in Japanese.  It was 
a theme park of the world created by god. 

( UTF code ; Jinkou-en = \u795E\u5DE5\u5712. ) 

The Sasson National Highway ( Sasson-kokudou ), which is now part of 
National Route 5, opened in 1934 ( Shouwa 9 ).  The theme park Jinkou-
en opened around the year.  ( The name ``Sa-sson'' means Sapporo and 
Otaru. ) 

Jinkou-en was a leisure facility before the Second World War.  The site 
is now Hariusu Mine, which extracts gravel for urban construction.  Bus 
stops with names of Jinkou-en and Keishou-en still exist today ( as of 
2019 ).

In the west of Otaru City, there was another theme park in the 1930s. 
It was Ryuuguu-kaku.  The name ``Ryuu-guu, kaku'' means ``dragon's 
palace ( sea god's palace ), observation tower'' in Japanese.  Otaru 
has long been a tourist destination.

( For the Hokkaidou's first tourist road, see the Otaru South section 
  in Otaru City, Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture. ) 
 
Near the former sites of Jinkou-en and Keishou-en, National Route 5 
runs along the sea cliffs.  The national highway used to run inland.  
It passed through the mountains. 
 
In 1904, during the Russo-Japanese War, a military road was built in 
the mountains.  It connected Asari district and Hariusu district in 
Asari Village ( now Otaru City. )  The mountainous road was built
to avoid Russian naval fire.  
 
The military road became a national highway.  Later, because there were 
too many slopes, a new route was built along the coast.  That was part 
of what is now National Route 5.

As for railroad, the former Horonai Line opened in 1880.  The railway 
was part of what is now the Hakodate Main Line.  Hariusu Station on 
the Horonai Line opened in 1905. 
 
The station was built at a fishing village, away from the built-up area 
of Hariusu.  The station served as an evacuation site for tunnel 
accidents, and as a site for shipping herring and gravel.

The station was crowded in summer because there was Hariusu Beach in 
front of it.  The beach closed in 1998.  Hariusu Station closed in 
2006.  #2 

( For herring fishing, see Yoichi Town in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

NOTE 
  
#1  

The scenic restaurant Keishou-en had a hotel.  It was destroyed by fire 
in 1970.  The hotel was the regular lodging for the professional 
baseball team the Toukyou Yomiuri Giants.

The Toukyou Yomiuri Giants played games at Sapporo Maruyama Stadium in 
summer.  Famous players were Shigueo Nagashima ( 1936-2025 ) and 
Sadaharu Ou ( 1940- ). 

( As for Sadaharu Ou, the former professional baseball player, there is 
  a Sadaharu Ou Memorial Baseball Ground near my apartment in Hachiouji 
  City.  It's for high school players.  I asked university students if 
  they knew the baseball player.  One of thme replied, ``I don't know, 
  but my father was a fan of his.'' )

Today, professional games aren't held at Maruyama Baseball Stadium. 
Sapporo Dome Stadium is for professional baseball.  At Maruayama 
Baseball Stadium, the baseball club of Hokkaidou University plays 
matches. 

( Maruyama Baseball Stadium is located in Chuuou Ward, Sapporo City,
  Ishikari sub-prefecture.  Sapporo Dome Stadium is located in 
  Toyohira Ward, Sapporo City, Ishikari sub-prefecture. )

I visited Maruyama Baseball Stadium to watch a game in which a member 
of our laboratory was competing.  The player was Mr. T who came from 
Nagano Prefectural Nagano High School.  At Hokkaidou University, he was 
the team captain and the 4th batter ( clean up hitter ).  
 
He said that he ate 7-gou of rice a day.  The volume of ``1-gou'' is 
150 grams of uncooked rice.  I eat less than 1-gou of rice a day.  His 
graduation thesis was about the consolidation of agricultural 
cooperatives in Hokkaidou ( by Nihei lab 2024-2026_e ).  

( For rice consumption, see Enbetsu Town in Rumoi sub-prefecture. ) 

* * * 

Sapporo Dome Stadium is the home of the Hokkaidou Nippon-ham Fighters
( as of 2019 ).  Nippon-ham Fighters moved from Toukyou to Hokkaidou. 

I heard this from Professor Taisuke Miyauchi of Hokkaidou University ;
``In the past, Sapporo citizens were fans of the Toukyou Yomiuri 
  Giants.  After the Nippon-ham Fighters moved to Sapporo, they turned 
  into fans of the Nippon-ham Fighters.''

Nippon-ham is a food company.  It runs swine ( pig ) farms in Oshima 
and Shiribeshi general sub-prefectures in Hokkaidou, and other 
locations in the country.  It also runs meat processing factories in 
Yakumo Town and Hakodate City, Oshima general sub-prefecture,  
Asahikawa City in Kamikawa general sub-prefecture,   
 
( continued ) --> Chikusei City and Toride City in Ibaraki Prefecture,
Kamakura City in Kanagawa Prefecture, Yoshida City in Shizuoka 
Prefecture, Kisosaki Town in Mie Prefecture, Ono City in Hyougo 
Prefecture, and Ishii Town in Tokushima Prefecture.  

Foreigners work for the factories of Nippon-ham.  The situation 
resembles the meat processing plants in the USA ( by N. Yagasaki et al. 
eds. 2003.  ``American High Plains'' published by Kokon-shoin, ISBN 
4772215727 ).  Foreign workers bring new culture such as music and 
food.  

( For Nippon-ham factory, see Yakumo Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. )
 
( For baseball, see Toyohira Ward in Sapporo City, Ishikari sub-
  prefecture. ) 

( For sport, see Takikawa City in Sorachi general sub-prefecture. )

* * *

Eating pork, rather than beef, has been part of the country's culture.
Even in the Edo era, pigs for meat were grazed in the Satsuma Domain 
( now Kagoshima Prefecture ), the Mito Domain ( now Ibaraki 
Prefectrue ), and the Hirado Domain ( now Ngasaki Prefecture. ) 
( by Japan Pork Producers Association ``History of Swine Farming'' 
  http://jppa.biz/story/history/  last accessed in August 2026. )   

During the Edo period, eating meat was ostensibly prohibited for 
Buddhist principles.  My hometown is located near Mito City, Ibaraki 
Prefecture.  They eat ``sukiyaki'' made with pork.  ( ``Sukiyaki'' is a 
hot pot dish with thinly sliced beef. ) 

( For swine industry, see Toubetsu Town in Ishikari sub-prefecture. )

#2

Hariusu Station ( obsolete ) was built away from the built-up area 
of Hariusu district.  

   The site of the former Hariusu Station   
   --> http://maps.gsi.go.jp/#15/43.164861/141.103694/

   Built-up area of Hariusu district   
   --> http://maps.gsi.go.jp/#15/43.154557/141.121337/

Professor Emeritus Katsuhiko Yamashita stated ;
``The residents near Hariusu Station were fishers.  In years when 
  herring catches were poor, they submitted petitions and were able to 
  use the trains for free.  

  The residents of Hariusu built-up area went to the station walking 
  on the railroad.  Fatal accidents occurred due to collision with 
  trains.''

The name ``Hariusu'' comes from ``haru-us'' in Ainu language.  It means 
``food, many.''  Wild edible plants such as ``oo-uba-yuri'' ( large 
heart-leaf lily ) and ``gyouja-nin-niku'' ( wild onion ) grow there. 
( Actually, those plants are common in the mountainous areas in 
Hokkaidou. )

* * *

In Hariusu, there is a shoe shop that makes shoes from the hide of 
brown bear.  It's Home Base Boot Maker ( http://
www.homebasebootmaker.com/ )

I have three pairs of the shoes made at the shoe shop.  The shop used 
to be located near Hokkaidou University.  I watched the shoemaker 
making shoes on my way to the office.  The scene reminded me of an old 
map room.  In the past, maps were made by hand. 

The shoemaker said ; 
``As for leather, high-quality cattle hide is imported from the USA.  
  Brazil is also a major producer of cattle hide.'' 

( Both countries raise ( re'iz ) large numbers of cattle and produce 
  large quantities of cattle hide.  Cattle hide includes steer hide and 
  cowhide. )

``Japan is self-sufficient in pig hide.  It's used for the inside of 
  shoes.''
 
( Japanese people have traditionally raised ( re'ized ) pigs and 
  chickens ( by Nihei lab 2026_w ).  For pork, see Note No. 1 above. ) 

``Hide and skin are different.  Animal skins are salted and tanned to 
  be hides.''

( In Japanese ; ``Skin'' and ``hide'' are ``kawa.'' )

( UTF code ; ``kawa'' ( skin ) = \u76AE.  ``kawa'' ( hide ) = \u9769. ) 
 
When I visited the shoe shops for the first time, I was wearing a pair 
of Red Wing shoes.  Those were made in the USA.  Watching my shoes, 
the shoemaker said ; 

``Red Wing shoes are sturdy.  Coal miners wore them in America.''

He told me that the Red Wing shoes I wore were made with a double-layer 
construction, using thick leather for both the outer and inner layers. 

Hearing it, I understood why the shoes were heavy.  One evening, when I 
went to a bar in Susukino, I took off the shoes to enter a table room.  
A woman who put customers' shoes in the cabinet said, ``What heavy 
shoes!''

I wore those shoes everyday.  I walked in them everywhere, from 
Brazilian savanna to the snows of Hokkaidou.  The leather shoes lasted 
more than 20 years. 

( For coal mining, see Yuubari City in Sorachi general sub-
  prefecture. ) 

( For brown bear, see Noboribtsu City in Iburi general sub-
  prefecture. )

The shoemaker continued ; 
``When I was learning shoe making in Toukyou, one day, I was sewing 
  shoes outside my apartment.  An elderly woman approached me and said, 
  `Go Shinbashi, you can earn.''' 

Shinbashi is a busines district in Toukyou.  Shinbashi Station is 
located next to Toukyou Station.  It's the first railway station in the 
country.  Business persons wear suits and leather shoes.  To walk a 
long distance in leather shoes, the shoes need to be made to fit each 
person's feet. 

( As for Shinbashi, one of our laboratory members was a former 
  business person who used to commute there from Hachiouji.  He was in 
  his late 60s.  He said that he used to got up at five and return 
  home after eleven. ) 

* * *

Today, leather shoes are made in factories.  It's difficult to make 
leather shoes that fit each person's feet.  Nike and New Balance killed 
leather shoes.  ( When I wrote this part, I listened to the song 
``Video Killed The Radio Star'' http://youtu.be/Y09n7GSEwiQ )

On the Tama Hills in Toukyou, where I live now, the residents wear 
fabric shoes.  One reason is that the roads are hard.  They are made of 
bricks, stones, and tiles.  They aren't good for leather shoes.  Plus, 
the hot and humid climate isn't suitable leather products ( by Nihei 
lab 2023-2026_t ).
 
In the north island, Hokkaidou, the asphalt is softer than it is in 
Honshuu Island.  The softness is more resistant to freezing.  Leather 
shoes are good on the soft asphalt.  

( Developed countries are located in the north.  London is located at 
  50 degrees north latitude.  The residents spend more money on clothes 
  and shoes ( by Nihei lab 2023-2026_t ). )

* * *
  
I visited Brazil in 2001.  One night, our study group had drinks and a 
meal at a restaurant.  A small boy came into the restaurant.  He had a 
shoe polish box and asked if he could polish my shoes.  I wore leather 
shoes.  I denied because I didn't know what to say and what to do. 

After he left, a Japanese professor got angry, saying, ``What are bad 
parents!  What are they doing now?  They make their child work at 
night!  The boy must study now!''

I also visited Brazil in the 2010s.  One night, young kids also came 
into the restaurant.  But, they didn't have a shoe polish box.  At the 
time, most Brazilian people also wore soft fabric shoes.  The young 
kids just begged for money.  

The same Japanese professor was at our table.  He was calm and gave 
money to the kids without a word.  He said to me, ``In this country, 
people who have give to those who don't have.''  Everything's changed.

I live in Toukyou now.  My son goes cram school at night.  Which is 
happier, the kids in Brazil or my son in Toukyou.  Toukyou is the 
prefecture of the lowest birth rate in the country. 

( Single of the chapter ; ``Sea'' by Spangle Call Lilli Line.  -->
  http://youtu.be/MdG9gjMGwc8 )
 _________________
|                 |
|   OTARU SOUTH   |
|                 |
|                 |
 -----------------
During a bus excursion, we went down National Route 393 ( Akaigawa-
kokudou ), passing Mt. Kenashi-yama ( 548 meters ).  We stopped at an 
observatory, from which we could watch the built-up area of Otaru City. 
Akaigawa-kokudou is a tourist road called the Maple Road. 

( For Mt. Kenashiyama, see Akaigawa Village in Shiribeshi general sub- 
  prefecture. )

( For the Maple Road, see Akaigawa Village in Shiribeshi general sub-
  prefecture. )

Hokkaidou Wine is located along the national highway.  The winery was 
established in 1974.  Its vineyards are located in Otaru City, Yoichi 
Town, and Urausu Town.  ( Otaru and Yoich are located in Shiribeshi 
general sub-prefecture.  Urausu is located in Sorachi general sub-
prefecture. )

In Urausu Town, Hokkaidou Wine mainly grows German varieties.  The 
winery also cultivates a common variety of grape in Otaru City and 
Yoichi Town. 

Common grape varieties are ``seishoku-hinsyu'' in Japanese, which means 
''raw eating, variety.''  They are also called table grapes.  Grape 
varieties for wine are ''jouzou-hinshu'' in Japanese, which means 
``brewery, variety.'' 

The common grape variety is Naiagara.  It was bred in 1872 in Niagara 
County in the state of New York, USA.  Hokkaidou Wine's Naiagara is 
processed into white wine.  #1

* * *  

Prefectural Route 1 ( Doudou-ichi-gou ) connects the center of Otaru 
and Jouzankei Hot Spring.  The road passes Asari-gawa Hot Spring, 
Asari-gawa Hot Spring Ski Resort, Lake Otarunai, Sapporo Kokusai Ski 
Resort, and Lake Sapporo. #2

Lake Otarunai is a dam lake in the upper reaches of the Asari River. 
Lake Sapporo is a dam lake in the upper reaches of the Toyohira River. 

The name at the bottom of Lake Otarunai was Gakkari-zawa.  The name is 
Japanese and means ``disappointment, stream.''  There were hopes and 
disappointments in the pioneering period.

( For river name, see the note of Oshamanbe Town in Oshima general sub-
  prefecture. ) 
 
NOTE

#1

Hokkaidou Wine's Niagara is white wine.  It's 1,200 yen per bottle at 
convenience stores ( as of 2019 ).  It's made from Niagara, a table 
grape variety.  Seicomart, a convenience store chain in Hokkaidou, also 
sells Chilean white wine for 500 yen.  The imported wine is made from 
Chardonnay variety. 

A small paper tag hanging from bottles of Niagara wine reads ``This is 
local.''  The taste is old-fashioned.  It's like grape juice.  It's a 
bit unusual at first, but it gets better as you get used to it.  That's 
culture.

Wine brewery was imported to Japan in the Meiji period, about 150 years 
ago.  At first, wineries produced wine with a flavor similar to rice 
wine ( ``sake'').  So, old-fashioned Japanese wine is sweet.  
( Although modern rice wine isn't sweet. )

Speaking of food culture, the spaghetti in the USA is mushy.  Slurping 
( sula'-ping ) long noodles isn't their culture.

( For wine, see Ikeda Town in Tokach general sub-prefecture. ) 

( For old-fashioned rice wine, see Kuriyama Town in Sorachi general 
  sub-prefecture. )

#2
 
Prefectural Route 1 opened in 1932.  It was the first toll road in 
Hokkaidou.  The road soon became toll-free, because cars seldom passed 
the road.  It was more than 30 years ahead of the motorization in the 
country.
 
( For motorization, see the note of Otobe Town in Hiyama sub-
  prefecture. ) 

The opening of the toll road had a global background.  The World War I 
occurred between 1914 and 1918.  Otaru prospered through the export of 
beans.  Business persons started buying cars.  But, the Great 
Depression hit the country between 1930 and 1931.

* * *

As for the incipient of the Japanese automobile industry, Ford Morotor 
Company from the USA established a factory in Yokohama in 1924.  
General Motors Company from the USA established a factory in Oosaka in 
1927.  They may have paved the way for Japanese automobile companies 
in Yokohama and Oosaka.

( Nissan Motor Co. has been headquartered in Yokohama City.  Daihatsu 
  Motor Co. was called Oosaka-hatsudou-ki. )

In my generation and the older, there are many car enthusiasts.  But, 
young people aren't interested in cars very much.  It’s somewhat 
regrettable, because automobile is Japan’s leading industry.  

Automobile became too complex for ordinary people to understand.  
Sometimes, simple things are better, because they hold the power to 
stir the imagination.

( Single of the chapter ; ``Bien Compadre ( Good Friend )'' by 
  Pugliese.  --> http://youtu.be/grk8hLtwtg4 )
 _________________
|                 |
|   OTARU WEST    |
|                 |
|                 |
 -----------------
The western part of Otaru City is the former Shioya Town.  It was 
merged in 1958.  The name ``Shioya'' comes from ``so-ya'' in Ainu 
language, which means ``cliff, coast.''  Or, it comes from ``su-ya,'' 
which means ``pot, coast'' in Ainu language.

Shioya was a fishing village and prospered from herring fishery.  After 
the herring catch declined, the owners of fishing grounds invested in 
grape cultivation.  The last arrival of large herring school was in 
1954 ( by ``Otaru Fish'' http://otaru-fish.jp/history/later/  last 
accessed in August 2026 ). 

Shioya grapes ( Shioya-budou ) became famous.  The Fruit Road ( Fruit-
kaidou ) runs through Shioya district in Otaru City, Yoichi Town, and 
Niki Town.  Shioya grapes are for eat ( table grapes ), although grapes 
for processing, especially for wine brewery, are increasing along the 
road.  

( For fruit, see Yoichi Town in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture. )

( For herring, see Yoichi Town in Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture. )

Ranshima district is located west from Shioya district.  Ranshima 
Station opened in 1903 ( Meiji 36 ) on the Hokkaidou Railway ( now 
Hakodate Main Line ).  In the same year, Ranshima Beach ( Ranshima-
kaisui-yoku-jou ) opened.  Ranshima is the oldest beach resort in 
Hokkaidou.

Ranshima is one of the three major beaches for Sapporo residents, along 
with Zenibako Beach in Otaru City and Ishikari Beach in Ishikari City.  

Along National Route 5, there is a stone monument to Sei Itou.  Sei 
Itou ( 1905-1969 ) was a novelist.  He was born in Sumiyakizawa Village 
( now Matsumae Town, Oshima general sub-prefecture ).  

He studied at the former Otaru Junior High School ( now Otaru Chou-ryou 
High School ) and Otaru High School of Commerce ( now Otaru University 
of Commerce ).  He became a best-selling novelist in Toukyou.  #1  #2

( In Japanese ; ``Otaru Junior High School'' is Kyuusei-otaru-chuugaku. 
  ``Otaru High School of Commerce'' is Otaru-koutou-shougyou-gakkou. )

NOTE   
 
#1  
 
Two novelists, Sei Itou and Takiji Kobayashi, graduagted from Otaru 
High School of Commerce ( now Otaru University of Commerce ).  

Sei Itou translated the novel ``Lady Chatterley's Lover,'' written by 
the English novelist D. H. Lawrence.  Then, Sei Itow was convicted of 
an offense involving obscene documents.  In the trial, he asserted his 
freedom of expression.  

He also translated the novel Ulysses, written by the Irish novelist 
James Joyce.  Those novels, Lady Chatterley's Lover and Ulysses, were 
once banned in Western countries due to their sexual expressions.  
Novelists broke taboos and people buy their works.  

Takiji Kobayashi ( 1903-1933 ) wrote ``The Crab Cannery Ship ( Kani-
kou-sen ).''  It's proletarian literature.  It's about the working 
class. 

The novel depicts workers on a crab cannery ship at North Ocean Fishing 
( Hokuyou-gyogyou ).  It also depicts an explosion at Yuubari coal 
mine as follows ;  

``In less than 1/500th ( one five hundredth ) of a second, he felt his 
  body being thrown somewhere like a piece of paper.  Several trolleys, 
  propelled by the gas pressure, flew past him, lighter than empty 
  matchboxes.'' 

( For coal mining, see Yuubari City in Sorachi general sub-
  prefecture. )

( For Sei Itou ( novelist ), see Matsumae Town in Oshima sub-
  prefecture. ) 

( For Takeo Arishima ( novelist ), see Niseko Town in Shiribeshi 
  general sub-prefecture. ) 

( For Takiji Kobayashi ( novelist ), see Tsukigata Town in Sorachi 
  general sub-prefecture. )

* * * 

Today, universities are like the Crab Cannery Ship.  Teachers are told, 
``Earn money from outside.''  The web-page of University of Tsukuba, 
where I graduated, shows how much each teacher earned money.  They are 
like salespeople at an exploitative company. 

( In Japanese, ``exploitative company'' is black company. )

According to Sasaki ( 2009 ), there were 765 universities in Japan in
2008 ( by H. Sasaki 2009.  The social role of 765 universities in 
Japan.  ``Mejiro University Higher Education'' 15, 1-8. ).

According to Google's AI Overview ( searched in July 2026 ), the number 
of universities in the country was 796.  The same source indicates 
that there were 192,000 university faculty members, and incidentally, 
220,000 Self Defense Forces personnel in the country.

#2

In June 2018, a teacher and two students came from Otaru Chou-ryou High 
School to my office in Hokkaidou University.  One of the students took 
a geography textbook out of her rucksack, and I signed on it.  ( I was 
one of the writers. )

I asked her where she was from.  She was from Kyougoku Town 
( Shiribeshi general sub-prefecture ).  She said that she would stay at 
a friend's house in Otaru that day, because Kucchan Station ( on the 
Hakodate Main Line ) was closed due to heavy rain the previous day.

I surprised to hear that she commuted from Kyougoku to Otaru to study. 
The distance between Kucchan and Otaru stations are 62 kilometers, and 
Kyougoku is located about 15 kilometers east from Kucchan Station.    

When I heard her story, I remembered some students at Hokkaidou 
University lived in dormitories in Sapporo City when they were high 
school students.  They returned to their hometown on weekends. 

Once, I asked a student at Hokkadou University, ``Where are you from?'' 
She answered, ``Shari Town'' ( Okhotsk general sub-prefecture ).  I 
continued, ``Oh, really?  Which high school did you graduate from?'' 
She answered,`` Sapporo-higashi'' ( Sapporo East ).

On another day, I asked the same question to another student.  She 
answered, ``Niikappu Town''  ( Hidaka sub-prefecture ).  I continued, 
``Oh, really?  Which high school did you graduate from?''  She 
answered, ``Sapporo-higashi'' ( Sapporo East ).
 
Many students come to Hokkaidou University from these high schools in 
Sapporo City ; Minami ( South ), Kita ( North ), Nishi ( West ), and 
Higashi ( East ).  Among them, Sapporo Higashi is a gate for students 
from the central, northern, and eastern parts of Hokkaidou.  

Education is a challenge in rural areas.  Many people moved to cities 
for education.  

( For high school student, see Ashibetsu City in Sorachi general sub-
  prefecture. )

( Single of the chapter ; ``Out Of Sight'' by Spangle Call Lilli Line.  
  --> http://youtu.be/1l11glbu1dw )
